- `image_alt_ai_service.dart` — Vision consumer: suggests WCAG alt-text and searchable tags for an image via the local backend.
- `image_dedup_service.dart` — Finds byte-identical image files by md5 to clean up libraries.
- `image_sidecar_store.dart` — Shared read/mutate/atomic-write layer for the per-directory JSON sidecars of captions and descriptions.
- `image_reference_service.dart` — Finds and rewrites image references in Marp markdown files.
- `image_rename_service.dart` — Renames an image file on disk and rewrites its Marp references + migrates caption/description sidecars; the open-decks sync stays with the caller.
- `image_library_scanner.dart` — Bounded, batched, cancellable recursive scan of the image-picker's search paths (newest first). Shares the file/depth caps with the deck scan via `utils/library_scan_limits.dart` (20000 files, depth 32), stats and sorts in async batches so a huge or mounted library does not block the caller, and reports when the list was truncated or a directory was unreadable.
- `asset_staging.dart` — `AssetStaging`: the waiting room for media of a deck that has no folder on disk yet. Per session a temp folder laid out like a real project (`images/`, `media/`), so bytes are safe on insert and the ordinary save-time copy lifts them into place. `isStagedPath` tests against the *root*, not the session folder, so a deck recovered after a restart still recognises its earlier copies instead of calling them external. `pruneStale` (startup, fire-and-forget) drops session folders whose *files* have not been touched for `RecoveryService.defaultMaxAge` — files, because a directory timestamp means something different per platform; the shared constant keeps staging from being cleared before the recovery drafts that point into it.
- `image_service.dart` — Validates and manages imported image and media asset files. `importIntoDeck` is the single entry for material the user already pointed at (dropped, or picked from the library); it returns the source path rather than null when copying fails, so a slide never silently goes blank. `copyImagesToProject` runs over the slide's whole reference set (`slide_image_refs.dart`), so a picture typed into a rich-text body lands in `<project>/images/` too instead of keeping an absolute path out of the recipient's reach.
- `image_usage.dart` — The image library's two questions, which used to exist twice with the same blind spot: `slideIndexesUsingImage` (which slides use this file — the answer someone deletes on) and `slideWithImageReplaced` (repoint them after a duplicate is cleaned up). Both read the whole reference set via `slide_image_refs.dart`, so an image that only appears in a rich-text body counts as a use. What actually differed between the two call sites — *how* a path resolves, with or without the containment guard — comes in as an `ImagePathResolver`.
- `git/asset_rights_index.dart` — Repository-wide rights scan over the configured forge: reuses current assessments, scans unassessed image blobs locally, commits their sidecars atomically, and appends administrator dispositions with optimistic head checking.
- `openkat/*.dart` — the OpenKAT import pipeline (#672/#767), directory → deck: `openkat_directory_scanner.dart` walks a folder of JSON exports into a manifest (every file gets a status — ok, duplicate, conflict, unrecognized, invalid, or too large; the byte cap is the input gate, the folder comes from outside) and groups them per organisation and report date; `openkat_json_adapter.dart` holds the adapter contract and the date parsing (OpenKAT stamps filenames as `<organisation>_20260319200604.json` — fourteen digits, no separators) while `openkat_export_adapters.dart` carries the two shapes OpenKAT actually writes, both sharing the envelope `{organization_code, organization_name, organization_tags, data}`: the **organisation report** (`data` is one flat summary) and the **per-asset reports** export (`data` keyed by report type, then by object reference). Their shared innards — `finding_types` lists, `services` maps, `number_of_compliant` beside `number_of_ips` — live once in `OpenKatShapes`. Note what the shapes teach: in the per-asset export the findings sit in the sub-reports, not in `findings-report` (which is empty unless a findings report was explicitly requested), and `controlScores` returns scores **with their denominator**, without which `OpenKatControlScore.ratio` is null and no trend can be computed; `openkat_normalizer.dart` turns raw objects into systems/findings, and `openKatSystemAnchor` in it holds the OOI-key grammar: a primary key starts at the object type and the *network* it was found in, and a composite type concatenates its parts' keys, so the anchor is found by **shape** — the last segment that reads as a host — rather than by counting segments. That is what folds every path and header of one website back onto that website; counting positions produced systems called `internet` and made one site look like dozens; `openkat_aggregator.dart` builds the cross-organisation totals and trends, including `history` (the stand at every measurement date, each organisation carrying its last known measurement forward) and `organizationComparison`, and it buckets every severity outside critical/high/medium/low into `openKatOtherSeverity` so a breakdown always explains its total; `openkat_report_composer.dart` renders those into slides that carry an `ocideck_openkat_view` marker in their notes — the marker is what lets a re-import replace generated slides while preserving manual ones — with #672 view limits as defaults so a thousand-school import still reads. It picks the slide *type* per view: scorecards for figures with a previous value, a line chart for the history, a heatmap for severity per organisation, tables for the rankings, and bullets with group headings for the recommendations OpenKAT supplies. A view with nothing to say is omitted rather than emitted empty, and severity colours are fixed per band (`_severityColors`) so one band means one colour across every slide; `openkat_import_service.dart` is the one entry point (import fresh, or update an existing deck) and writes the manifest plus per-organisation JSON next to the deck under `data/openkat/`, atomically. The models live in `models/openkat/` (own entry).
- `openkat/openkat_rocky_client.dart` — GET-only Rocky REST client for live server connections (2026-08-05): Knox token auth, NetGuard + pinning, `listOrganizations`, paginated `listAggregateReports` (aggregate type only), optional `fetchReportJson` probing `/api/v1/report/{pk}/json/` (path A when upstream has it). No recipe CRUD, Octopoes/Bytes, or session download. Desktop-only send path; web gets denial reasons instead.
- `openkat/openkat_report_facts.dart` — the canonical reporting query layer: selects the latest usable snapshot on/before each as-of date (or the prior usable one), builds source traces and measurement usages, and calculates rankings, lifecycle, controls, CVE, asset and monitoring facts. It owns deterministic ranking and CVE/org/system/finding deduplication, so scenarios cannot invent their own selection rules.
- `openkat/openkat_report_capabilities.dart` — assesses required/optional report capabilities at the selected measurements: freshness, truly chronological history, comparable coverage, stable identities, denominators and adapter-declared CVE, monitoring and `openedAt` features. Absent declarations, unknown monitoring statuses and missing dates remain unavailable rather than becoming inferred facts.
- `openkat/openkat_report_scenarios.dart` — declarative scenario registry for 22 stable IDs, and their ordered registered blocks: `management-overview`, `organization-comparison`, `portfolio-trend`, `finding-type-prevalence`, `critical-high-concentration`, `control-coverage`, `recommendations-overview`, `organization-overview`, `weekly-comparison`, `finding-lifecycle`, `finding-age`, `system-hotspots`, `system-changes`, `control-changes`, `asset-inventory`, `monitoring-coverage`, `monitoring-changes`, `cve-exposure`, `cve-landscape`, `cve-changes`, `data-quality` and `measurement-accountability`. The wizard places these under organisations/management, organisation/progress, CVEs, and data-quality/accountability.
- `openkat/openkat_report_rankings.dart` — shared deterministic ordering helpers for report facts; severity bands and all ties are normalised before a block presents them.
- `openkat/openkat_report_engine.dart` — headless orchestration: validates a typed request and chronological comparison, gathers measurements/traces and diagnostics, validates intrinsic block preconditions after scenario composition, then asks the composer for a normal `Deck`.
- `openkat/openkat_report_composer.dart` / `openkat_report_composer_context.dart` / `openkat_report_composer_blocks.dart` / `openkat_report_composer_assets.dart` / `openkat_report_composer_security.dart` — map a validated plan to ordinary `Deck`/`Slide` objects. The façade owns dispatch and the shared context owns bounded tables, omission notices and stable scenario/block/view IDs; the renderer parts cover summaries, comparisons, trends, prevalence, age/lifecycle, systems, CVEs, controls, recommendations, assets, monitoring and measurement accountability. Imported Markdown is neutralised, typed facts are localised in Dutch or English, and generated originals receive process-authenticated provenance so copied, edited or unauthenticated slides never acquire replacement rights. No new slide type or report format is introduced.
- `openkat/openkat_deck_generator.dart` — compatibility façade for the existing initial-import/re-import route. It composes `management-overview` through the new facts/plan layer and keeps the `ocideck_openkat_view` markers that refresh generated slides while preserving manual ones. An unsafe legacy or externally edited origin stops with `OpenKatUnsafeUpdateException`.
- `openkat/openkat_slide_provenance.dart` — fingerprints each generated OpenKAT original as canonical slide Markdown (SHA-512 without its own marker). The proof survives save/reopen, detects edits to externally copied blocks, and lets the updater remove obsolete generated views without mistaking user work for an origin.
- `openkat/openkat_wizard_service.dart` — `OpenKatWizardGateway` and its concrete service: prepare a read-only directory scan, derive evidence-gated UI options, and delegate preview/build to the reporting engine. On update it uses `OpenKatDeckGenerator.updateGenerated` so manual slides survive.
- `display_window_service.dart` — `DisplayWindowService` (#672): applies a slide's non-destructive view limit as a *projection* over bullets, table rows or a `ChartSpec` — the original data is never modified, callers get a new copy plus a `DisplayWindowResult` with total/shown and the optional "N of total" caption. The judgement calls live here and nowhere else: bullets only support first/last; a time-series chart asked for top/bottom quietly ranks by *last* instead, because value-sorting a timeline destroys its chronology; the *Overig* bucket only sums where summing is honest; and equal values keep their source order (explicit tie-break on source index — Dart's sort is not stable, and a deck must show the same top-N on every reopen). `isNumericColumn` is public because the editor's warning builds on the same judgement. The `Slide.projectionWithViewLimit()` extension at the bottom is the one entry point renderer and export share.
- `markdown_body_blocks.dart` — Splits a rich-text markdown body into the blocks the paginator packs into pages: paragraphs, headings, lists, fenced code, math — and an `![alt](path)` alone on a line, which is an image in the running text (FILE_FORMAT § *Rich text*). `parseMarkdownImageBlock` reads Marp's `w:`/`h:` directives out of the alt text and `markdownImageBlockBox` turns them into a box, measured against `kMarkdownImageDirectiveSlideWidth` (1280, Marp's own slide width and the one the HTML export uses) rather than `kReferenceSlideWidth` (960), so a `![w:600]` cannot mean one thing in the app and another in the export. The box comes from the markdown and never from the file: pagination is synchronous and may not wait for a decode, so without `h:` a fixed `kMarkdownImageDefaultHeightFraction` of the reference width is reserved and the picture is `contain`-fitted inside it. The paginator and the renderer call this same function, so the reserved and the drawn height cannot drift apart. A `$$…$$` block gets the same treatment through `displayMathBlockHeight`: a formula renders at a fixed size but is rarely one line tall — a fraction, a root, a big operator with limits or a stacked script makes it two to three times higher — so its reserve is estimated from the height-adding constructs rather than measured as a text line, erring generous so a tall formula never slides into the logo band (#947).
- `markdown_safety.dart` — Scans raw `.md` for executable content and blocks unsafe imports.
- `management_summary.dart` — Derives the management summary from the deck (severity counts, scope coverage, standards used).
- `front_matter_merge.dart` — The front-matter half of the file-format contract: which keys OciDeck owns, the `ocideck_format` version rules, and the surgical update that leaves every other line untouched. Also the definition of "a key sits at column 0, an indented line belongs to the block above" that the reader, the writer and the checker all share — without it, CSS inside a `style: |` block set deck fields. `kRetiredFrontMatterKeys` holds the keys OciDeck no longer writes but still owns: taking them off the list entirely would mean "unknown, so leave it alone", and the base64 would stay in the file forever.
- `markdown_service.dart` — Serializes decks to Marp markdown and parses it back (the file-format contract), including the standard Marp visual subset in `MarpStyle`; unknown directives and extended background layers are passed through verbatim. `generateDeck` skips every slide with `renderPage > 0`: a render copy of a rich-text page has no shape in the file format — it carries the whole body and differs only in a field that is not serialized — so writing the expanded list out gave the same slide N times over, each time complete. The filter lives here rather than at the call site because the rule holds everywhere; `expandRichTextForRender` yields a list to *draw*, never one to write. Writes **no base64**: what is opaque, or is about the document instead of in it, lives in a sidecar. Part `markdown_parse/markdown_service_parse_front_matter.dart` holds the `--- … ---` header; `markdown_parse/markdown_service_parse_columns.dart` reads the visible `<h3>`/`<ul><li>` of a two-column slide, which is the content itself rather than a rendering of a comment above it. The reader takes a slide's type from the same registry the writer uses (`slideTypeByMarpClass` in `models/slide.dart`) instead of its own list of string literals.
- `markdown_front_matter_codec.dart` — Small front-matter codec used by `MarkdownService`: it parses and writes the owned Marp visual keys without growing the parser's central class.
- `marp_source_preservation.dart` — Recognises Marpit body syntax that the typed slide model cannot represent losslessly, so the complete authored block can stay in free Markdown rather than being reordered or dropped.
- `miauw_codec.dart` — The MIAUW disposition (client exclusions and confirmations) as the `<name>.miauw.json` sidecar — since #756 sidecar version 2, with a timestamp per decision and tombstones for withdrawn ones, because the file now travels to a git repository where two reviewers edit it independently. Also `mergeMiauw` (union per EIS id, the decision taken last wins, a tombstone wins the tie) and the read-only decoder for the base64 front-matter keys the sidecar replaced, so an older file still opens; version-1 sidecars stay readable, their undated entries losing to any dated decision.
- `markdown_service_finding.dart` — Parses/serializes the `finding` slide group's id/role markers and header spec.
- `markdown_validator.dart` — Line-anchored structural pre-flight against the parser's expectations.
- `disk_traces.dart` — `DiskTraces`: the one inventory of what OciDeck leaves on this disk, and how to take it back. Removing a git connection deletes `git_clone/<slug>/`, `git_mirror/<slug>/` and the `git_outbox::<slug>::` keys; a `LocalConnection` is never touched, because that path is the user's own folder. `removeGitTraces` **refuses** while unpushed commits are queued and reports the count instead — those commits exist nowhere else, so the caller must have asked the user first (`discardPendingWork`). Style logos are cleaned in two halves: `removeStyleLogos` for exactly the paths a profile change dropped, and `pruneOrphanStyleLogos` as the startup sweep with a 7-day floor, so a second window that just imported a style cannot lose its logo. `restrictToOwner` is Linux-only: one fixed `chmod 700` over the app-support directory and the staging root (never `/tmp` itself), which is what makes PRIVACY.md's "relies on your OS account protections" true there rather than aspirational.
- `seal_codec.dart` — The document seal and the visible signature as the `<name>.seal.json` sidecar. They share one file because they are one act: a signature without a seal has no anchor, a seal without a signer does not say who stands behind it.
- `marp_html_service.dart` — Builds the self-contained, sanitised HTML export with embedded assets. Parts: `marp_html/marp_html_service_charts*.dart` (charts to inline SVG), `marp_html/marp_html_service_cockpit.dart` (authentic/classic instrument SVG on the same `CockpitGridPlan`/`CockpitCellPlan` as the painter — dial left, read-out window in the flank, label strip below, scale numerals on the face; the panel keeps the proportions of the app panel on a titled 16:9 slide so both worlds make the same beside/below choice; text zones sit in per-zone clip paths as a last resort and every id carries a per-SVG suffix because several cockpit slides share one document; authentic meters get the staggered `cockpitPowerOn` CSS sequence and reduced-motion fallback), `marp_html/marp_html_service_canvas.dart` (Procesverbetering `canvas` slides to the same Scene SVG the preview draws), `marp_html/marp_html_service_tree.dart` (Procesverbetering `tree` slides), `marp_html/marp_html_service_flow.dart` (Procesverbetering `flow` slides), `marp_html/marp_html_service_menu.dart` (choice-`menu` slides to the same blocks the preview draws, in the layout the slide carries — a target block becomes an `<a>` to the target slide's `#anchor`, a text block a plain card, colours from the theme accent inline; the three layouts ship in `_menuCss` as `menu-grid`, `menu-grid menu-stack` and `menu-ring`, the ring's angles computed here — over the shared `menuDiscFraction`/`menuRingRadius` — and written out as percentages, so the export needs no script; categories become headings with their blocks under them rather than tabs, because an exported page has no presenter to switch them and half the menu behind a button nobody presses is worse than all of it in view; for the fragment links to land, `_renderSections` gives every slide's `<section>` an `id` from its `ocideck_slide_anchor` — marked v18 emits no heading ids, so without it a `#anchor` link would scroll nowhere), and `marp_html/marp_html_service_reporting*.dart` — the six table-backed reporting types (`scorecard`, `assets`, `discoveries`, `checklist`, `scope-matrix`, `findings-summary`), which would otherwise reach the recipient as a bare Markdown table while the app shows cards, bars and coverage counters. Slide images are inlined as `data:` URIs via an injected `HtmlImageResolver`; the service itself never reads a file, so it still builds on web. The stylesheet is split: `_structuralCss` always travels (timeline, sign-off, redaction block, TLP banner, print rules), the theme only adds colours and fonts — they used to *replace* each other, so a themed export lost all four. In slides mode the style profile's presentation logo rides along too (`_presentationLogoCss` in `marp_html/marp_html_service_images.dart`): the embedded `data:` URI is laid on each logo slide via `.slide.logo-safe::before`, in the same corner/size as the in-app overlay (`_LogoOverlay`) and reserving the same strip (`logoSafeReserve`), so the HTML matches what the presenter, beamer and PDF/PPTX show; `_renderSections` tags a slide `logo-safe` unless it opts out with `no-logo`. A pseudo-element is used deliberately — the render script wipes each `<section>`'s `innerHTML` before it renders the Markdown, so a real `<img>` would not survive. The continuous document mode keeps its own header/footer logo band (`_withDocumentChrome`) instead. In the continuous document CSS (`marp_html_service_css.dart`) a `.document hr` (a `---` thematic break) reads as a rule on screen but carries `page-break-after:always` under `@media print`, so an author's `---` page break becomes a new sheet when the recipient prints or saves to PDF (DOCUMENT_MODE.md §13). When `build` is called with `chapterPageBreak: true` (the *Nieuw hoofdstuk op een nieuwe pagina* setting) it also injects `@media print{.document h1{page-break-before:always}}` with the first chapter reset to `auto`, so every chapter starts on a fresh sheet on print without a blank opening page; the CSS is absent when the flag is off (DOCUMENT_MODE.md §13.5). Also writes the third-party notices into that file: a `/*! @license … */` banner on every inlined bundle (MathJax and Mermaid ship minified without one) and a collapsed `<details>` block with the full licence texts, plus the OFL text whenever EB Garamond is embedded as base64 — forwarding an export makes the user the distributing party.
- `html_image_embedder.dart` — Turns image bytes into what goes into the HTML: caps the long edge at `kHtmlEmbedMaxEdge`, re-encodes to JPEG (PNG only when pixels are really transparent), passes SVG/GIF through untouched, and **strips EXIF** — re-encoding does not do that by itself, so without it a photo's GPS location and camera serial travelled to every recipient. A pure function, so `ExportService` can run it in an isolate.
- `markdown_table_lines.dart` — De regelherkenning van een Markdown-tabel (`isMarkdownTableLine`, `isMarkdownTableDelimiterRow`, `opensAtomicMarkdownTable`) plus het celsplitsen waar de scheidingsrij op leunt. `opensAtomicMarkdownTable` is de *toelatingsregel* van `EmbeddableTableSyntax` — kop en scheidingsrij met evenveel kolommen, geteld zoals de embed telt — en bewust strenger dan GFM: te streng laat hooguit een hele tabel langs de scanners lopen, te ruim verklaart een tabel veilig die de omzetting juist stukmaakt. Losgetrokken uit `markdown_table_codec.dart` omdat die via `models/slide.dart` → `improvement/canvas_spec.dart` → `l10n/app_localizations.dart` het hele Flutter-materiaal binnenhaalt: een parser die zichzelf zuiver noemt kan die import niet doen, en juist zo'n parser heeft de predicaten nodig om een gemarkeerde tabel te herkennen zonder hem te decoderen. De codec exporteert alles hier door, dus elke bestaande aanroeper ziet één oppervlak. Zuiver Dart, geen imports — dat is de eigenschap die het bestand draagt.
- `pentest_blocks.dart` — De grammatica van de vijf pentest-enveloppen in een plat document (PENTEST_DOCUMENT.md §5) en **de enige plek waar de blokgrens staat**. `scanPentestBlocks` doet één lineaire, fence-bewuste regelpas (` ``` ` én `~~~`, met runlengte) en levert per envelop het bronbereik, het gemeten kopniveau en het *atomaire deelbereik* — het stuk dat als één embed reist en daarom nooit door de Quill-conversie mag. De twee prozablokken (`finding`, `sign-off`) zijn kopniveau-begrensd: ze lopen tot de volgende kop van gelijk of hoger niveau, óf tot de markerregel van het volgende blok. De drie tabelblokken begrenzen zichzelf, zoals de tijdlijn. Een envelop levert alleen een blok op als hij welgevormd opent; een weesmarker blijft een gewone regel, precies zoals `isDocumentTimelineEnvelope` losstaat van `analyzeMarkedTimeline`. Zuiver Dart en per toetsaanslag aanroepbaar: nooit een echte Markdown-parse.
- `markdown_table_codec.dart` — The Markdown-table cell coding (escape `|`, `\`, newline as `<br>`), shared by the parser, the serializer and the HTML export so the three cannot drift. Since the office tables it is also the document mode's table codec: `isMarkdownTableLine`/`isMarkdownTableDelimiterRow` let the reader and `DocumentDeckBridge` recognise a table start, `splitMarkdownTableRow`/`decodeMarkdownTableRows` read the cells (a `<br>` becomes a real newline, so a document table cell can be multi-line), and `encodeMarkdownTable` writes the grid back with per-column alignment — one codec for the app's internal report tables and the user's own document tables alike.
- `bundled_licenses.dart` — The licence texts for everything OciDeck bundles that is **not** a Dart package: four font families, the YuNet model and the five JS/CSS bundles. Flutter's own `NOTICES` asset covers packages (including both `third_party/` forks) and nothing else; this registers the rest with `LicenseRegistry` so `showLicensePage` is complete, and pins the `.txt` files as declared assets so they are actually in the binary.
- `mermaid_render_service.dart` — Renders Mermaid diagrams to cached inline SVG. Headless: on desktop/mobile via a shared WebView whose host is a widget in `lib/widgets/mermaid_render_host.dart`; on web via the JS-interop path below (the WebView cannot render there — `webview_flutter_web` has no `runJavaScriptReturningResult`, #851).
- `mermaid_config.dart` — Single source of truth for the Mermaid init settings (`kMermaidInitConfig`, `securityLevel: strict`, no `htmlLabels`) and the bundled-script URL, shared by both render paths so they cannot drift.
- `mermaid_web_renderer.dart` / `mermaid_web_renderer_stub.dart` — The web/io halves selected by conditional import. The web half loads the bundled `mermaid.min.js` via a first-party `<script src>` and calls `mermaid.render` through `dart:js_interop`; the io stub is never called (the WebView renders there).
- `miauw_compliance_analyzer.dart` — Scores each MIAUW EIS (Voldaan/Openstaand/Uitgesloten) from deck content + waivers.
- `miauw_eis_catalog.dart` — The bundled offline MIAUW EIS catalog (`MiauwEisCatalog`): all 88 testable EIS, parsed from the authoritative MIAUW workbook.
- `open_file_channel.dart` — Receives file-open paths from macOS for `.md` / `.ocideck` (Finder "Open With"), and opens the filterless native pick panel (`pickUnfilteredMacFiles`, with `pickUnfilteredMacFile` as the single-file wrapper) so a remembered UTI filter cannot grey out `.md` files. The panel takes a multiple selection when asked; the channel always answers with a list, so only one shape crosses the bridge.
- `privacy/privacy_checksums.dart` — Eleven-proof (BSN), Luhn and IBAN mod-97 with the country-length table.
- `privacy/privacy_allowlist.dart` — Known non-personal values: reserved domains, example IBANs, test cards, the official test-BSN range.
- `privacy/privacy_secret_rules.dart` — The secrets rule table: vendor tokens (AWS/GitHub/Slack/Stripe/…), PEM private keys, decodable JWTs, connection strings, Azure keys and SAS tokens, password hashes, TOTP seeds, plain-text passwords — plus the placeholder gate that keeps a how-to slide quiet, the Shannon-entropy fallback (`secret.entropy`, the only rule here with a context gate) and its exclusions for hashes, UUIDs and checksums.
- `privacy/privacy_checksums_eu.dart` — The European checksums: BE mod-97, DE ISO 7064 + digit-repetition, FR NIR, ES DNI/NIE, PT NIF, PL PESEL, IT codice fiscale, HR OIB, BG EGN, RO CNP, SE Luhn, FI mod-31, EE/LT mod-11, IS kennitala mod-11, LV personas kods mod-11 (a *different* scheme from EE/LT), LU matricule (Luhn **and** Verhoeff, both over the same eleven digits), CY tax code mod-26 check letter, UK NHS/NINO, NL legacy VAT (elevenproof over the nine embedded digits, which are the owner's BSN) — with embedded-birthdate validation where the checksum alone is too weak. Two entries deliberately have no checksum because none is published: the Maltese identity card number (the trailing letter encodes birth region and century, it checks nothing) and the Liechtenstein PEID (four to twelve bare digits). Both are context-gated in the rule table.
- `privacy/privacy_national_rule.dart` — `NationalIdentifierRule`: the shape of one national identification number (country, pattern, validator, context words, confidence). Lives apart from any country list because nothing about it is regional — it was called `EuIdentifierRule` until the US and Canada arrived and the name started to lie.
- `privacy/privacy_eu_rules.dart` — The European country packs as a data table of `NationalIdentifierRule`s: pattern, checksum, context words, confidence. Includes the six Dutch numbers beside the BSN (`nl.btw_id_legacy`, `nl.vnummer`, `nl.anummer`, `nl.big`, `nl.agb`, `nl.pv_nummer`); only the first may fire without a context word.
- `privacy/privacy_checksums_world.dart` — The non-European validators: US SSN/ITIN/EIN range rules, ABA routing mod-10, NPI/MBI/DEA, CA SIN/BN Luhn + RAMQ date + OHIP, AU TFN mod-11/Medicare/ABN mod-89, **Verhoeff** for Aadhaar, ZA Luhn + birthdate, BR CPF/CNPJ double mod-11. Note what is *absent*: the American numbers have no check digit at all, so "validation" there is a range check that removes a few percent where a mod-11 removes ten in eleven — which is why every one of those rules also carries a context gate.
- `privacy/privacy_world_rules.dart` — The non-European country packs, same table shape as the European one. Carries the GDPR-as-yardstick reasoning: rules exist here because a number identifies a person under an open norm, not because a local statute enumerates it — hence `us.ssn_last4` and `us.itin`, and hence a company's Indian PAN is filtered out rather than reported.
- `privacy/privacy_regions.dart` — Which country packs run. A rule is country-bound when its id starts with a known two-letter code, derived from the id itself rather than a second list that would drift. All packs ship **on**: every rule carries a checksum or a context gate, and protection should not depend on the author having found a checkbox. A pack exists only where a rule exists — six European ones were removed in July 2026 because a checkbox that is on and finds nothing lets "nobody looked" read as "nothing found", and returned days later once CY, IS, LI, LU, LV and MT each had a rule. The European packs now cover all 27 EU member states plus Iceland, Liechtenstein, Norway, Switzerland and the UK. `sharedRegionRules` lets one rule answer to two country codes (the rodné číslo under `cz` and `sk`, the Baltic personal code under `ee` and `lt`) rather than shipping a second id that would report the same number twice. `privacyRuleRegion` deliberately tests against a *complete* list of country codes instead of the enabled packs: a future `cy.` rule must fall inside the region gate, not outside every pack. Guard: `test/privacy_region_coverage_test.dart`, which fails in both directions.
- `privacy/privacy_special_rules.dart` — GDPR art. 9/10: multilingual keyword families, genetic notation (dbSNP/HGVS), the Dutch parketnummer, the co-occurrence escalator's definition of "identifies a person", and `statementSpan` — because a special-category datum is a statement, not a word, so redacting it takes the whole line.
- `privacy/privacy_lexicon_data.dart` — The bundled art. 9/10 lexicon: every term states how it wants to be matched, in which language, and how specific it is. The language coverage is deliberately uneven *and visible* — Dutch rich, English reasonable, DE/FR/ES thin, the other 25 interface languages none — because a green bar saying "nothing found" for a language where nothing *can* be found is worse than no check at all.
- `privacy/privacy_bulk_lexicon.dart` — The bulk lexicons (Orphanet conditions, EuroVoc convictions) as an asset laid over the compiled floor, same shape as `CweCatalog`: if the asset is missing or broken the floor keeps running. Carries a start-token index, because a linear `indexOf` walk over 62k terms never meets the 5 ms-per-slide budget — the work is made proportional to the slide instead of to the lexicon.
- `privacy/privacy_context_role.dart` — Whose role a criminal-law finding is reading: suspect, reporter/victim, or witness. Three-way with `unknown` as the default and deliberately no two-way fallback: if you must guess, the expensive mistake is not "I don't know" but calling a complainant a suspect. Triggers are scoped to the statement and cut at "maar"/"but"/"hoewel"; two roles in one statement yields no role. Changes only the wording of the notice — never the severity, the redaction or the export gate.
- `privacy/privacy_card_rules.dart` — Payment cards: the issuer-range table per scheme with each scheme's exact lengths, plus the CVV keyword pattern. Luhn alone is far too weak (one in ten random digit runs passes), so a number must also belong to a real scheme *at that scheme's length*; a Luhn-valid number belonging to no scheme is coincidence, not a card. The CVV never fires without a valid card number in the same fragment.
- `privacy/privacy_document_rules.dart` — The machine-readable zone of a passport or ID card (TD1/TD2/TD3), validated on every ICAO check digit including the composite one that runs over the others. No context gate — four interlocking check digits do not let ordinary text through. One wrong digit means no match: a deliberate preference for a missed scan over a scanner that shouts "passport!" at every table of capitals.
- `privacy/privacy_digital_rules.dart` — Digital identifiers: IPv4/IPv6, MAC, IMEI, ICCID, IMSI, advertising UUIDs and social handles. Skips the ranges reserved for documentation (RFC 5737/3849) and the version numbers that look just like addresses; a private range drops to `possible` — internal infrastructure is not a person, but an internal address plan on a public slide is still a leak. Two collisions are settled here: Amex prefixes are left to the card rule (both are 15 digits with a valid Luhn), and the checksum-less IMSI deliberately takes the 15-digit runs that *fail* Luhn, so the two never claim the same string. GitHub is absent from the handle list on purpose — in a technical deck that link is a repository, not a person.
- `privacy/privacy_plate_rules.dart` — Dutch licence-plate sidecodes and the international postcode patterns. The plate's context word is mandatory rather than recommended: `XX-99-99` is equally an article code or a version marker, so the pattern on its own excludes almost nothing. Letter groups the RDW never issues are dropped.
- `privacy/privacy_location_rules.dart` — Birthdates and coordinates: the two that point at a person without naming one. The birthdate's context word is not negotiable — a date is the most common number form in a business deck, and a rule that reports them all reports the calendar. The coordinate needs none: four decimals on both sides is about eleven metres, a shape ordinary prose does not produce.
- `privacy/privacy_phone_rules.dart` — Phone numbers: E.164 validated against the ITU calling-code list (the only form that earns `certain`), national trunk forms, the context-word gate for bare digit runs, and the reserved "drama" ranges that are the `example.com` of telephony.
- `privacy/privacy_contact_rules.dart` — Address, Dutch postcode and labelled person-name: a street-suffix word with a house number, the `1234 AB` pattern (hex colours excluded), and names only behind a salutation (`dhr.`) or a label (`naam:`) — no NER. Address and postcode are each `possible`; a street and a postcode within ~40 characters escalate both to `certain`, because postcode + house number pins one home address.
- `privacy/privacy_export_policy.dart` — The export gate: counts findings by disposition and decides whether to warn, block, or stay quiet.
- `privacy/privacy_own_identity.dart` — `OwnIdentity`: the author's own name/email/domain, which is the sender rather than a finding. Exact and domain matching only — no fuzzy match, which would silently suppress a real finding.
- `privacy/privacy_structural_rules.dart` — Structural leaks: user paths that reveal a name, tokens and personal data in URL queries, share links with built-in access, mailto links, unscannable data-URIs.
- `privacy/privacy_bulk_rules.dart` — Bulk personal data: a table header that names the column ("Naam", "BSN"), or one rule firing too often on a slide. One finding on top of the individual ones, not instead of them.
- `privacy/privacy_scanner.dart` — `PrivacyScanner`: reads a deck for privacy-sensitive data (email, phone, IBAN, BSN, EU numbers, address, postcode, name), with context gates where the checksum is too weak and proximity escalation where a postcode meets a house number. Findings inside an atomic document-timeline `customMarkdown` carrier resolve their exact GFM column header while retaining offsets into that same raw field, so projection both understands and redacts the actual exported source.
- `privacy/privacy_scanner_detectors.dart` — `part of privacy_scanner.dart`: the detectors for the families added after the first release — digital identifiers, the MRZ, the birthdate and coordinates — plus the address anchors and `fin.us_routing` that moved out of the scanner later. A `part` and not a library of its own, so `privacy_scanner.dart` stays under the thousand-line ratchet while these keep direct access to `_finding`, `_hasContextWord` and `ownIdentity`. Because they run through `_finding`, they inherit the own-identity and `[[…]]` suppression for free.
- `privacy/privacy_scanner_fragments.dart` — `part of privacy_scanner.dart`: which text the scanner walks — deck metadata, custom document fields, document header/footer templates and per-slide fields. Title/subtitle/author document fields share the existing metadata paths; custom fields are indexed separately. The list answers the question that decides the whole feature's reach: what is not scanned is not redacted either, because the projection only rewrites fields that carry a finding. An atomic timeline is scanned once through its exported `customMarkdown`; its decoded `tableRows` shadow remains available to bulk rules but is not scanned a second time.
- `privacy/privacy_timeline_context.dart` — Builds a one-pass cell-span index for an atomic timeline carrier, so every privacy finding receives its exact column-header context without rescanning or copying a potentially long table row.
- `privacy/privacy_marp_fragments.dart` — Adds deck- and slide-scoped Marp headers, footers, preserved directives and background references to the privacy scanner's fragment stream.
- `privacy/privacy_scan_memo.dart` — Per-slide memoisation of the scan. The provider used to re-scan the whole deck on every keystroke (~1.07 ms per slide, so 208 ms at 200 slides); one keystroke changes one slide. The cache key carries the scanner *object* — a settings change rebuilds it, so a different configuration is a different object and therefore a miss — rather than the settings fields, which would drift.
- `privacy/dismissal_codec.dart` — `DeckDismissals`, `PrivacyDismissal` and the `<name>.dismissals.json` codec: privacy findings the author has deliberately set aside (FILE_FORMAT §6.7, #651). Per deck rather than per user, because "that name belongs here" is a judgement about *this* document and has to travel with it to the second reviewer. Identity is rule id + `SHA-256(salt ‖ text)`, never the position — every position in `PrivacyFinding` moves the moment someone types a word above it, so a position-keyed set-aside would quietly expire. The commitment is not confidentiality against whoever holds the deck (they can read the slide); it stops this file becoming a second copy of a personal datum that outlives its deletion from the slide, and the per-deck salt stops correlating decks. `mergeDismissals` is a union keyed on rule + commitment where the later `at` wins; the `revocations` tombstones are load-bearing, because without them an undo vanishes on the next merge and the finding stays hidden — the quiet failure direction.
- `privacy/redaction_manifest_service.dart` — Builds and verifies the redaction manifest. Deliberately *outside* the projection: the projection runs on every render (preview, thumbnail, presenter) and so must stay pure and deterministic — a salt generated there would give a different answer each frame and the preview would rebuild forever. The manifest is therefore made once, at export. `redactedValues` is the single source of truth for both building and verifying, and it applies the same `isRedactable` gate as the projection plus a non-empty-span test: an entry for a redaction that is not in the document sends the recipient looking for a block that was never there.
- `privacy/privacy_preview.dart` — `audiencePreviewSlide` + `slideIsRedacted`: the author's own look at what the recipient gets. Projects **one** slide rather than the deck, because the projection scans and rescanning every slide on each keystroke in the editor beside it would make the preview unusable; for what the preview shows that costs nothing, since the scanner escalates within a slide and the deck fields are not on it. `slideIsRedacted` folds deck and slide disposition through `effectivePrivacyDisposition` so the notice appears exactly where redaction will happen.
- `privacy/privacy_quality_bridge.dart` — Maps `PrivacyFinding` onto `SlideQualityIssue` so findings surface in the quality panel.
- `privacy/privacy_projection.dart` — `AudienceDeck` + `PrivacyProjection`: the single boundary a source deck crosses to reach any receiving surface. Redacts `[[…]]` markers before rendering or export; the private constructor means no export path can hold the unredacted source. It projects the document-field map alongside deck metadata, using the title/description/author findings for the three standard fields and indexed `documentFields` findings for custom values, so Markdown, HTML and LaTeX consume the same audience-safe map. Media redaction erases the paths *and* sets the projection-only `Slide.mediaRedacted`, because an empty path alone cannot tell the renderer whether a photo was removed or never chosen. It also empties the path of every `![…](…)` in the body (2026-07-22) — those sit in no field, so a redacted slide used to travel to screen and export with its picture intact while the text beside it already showed blocks. Only the path goes; `![alt]()` stays, so the block keeps its place in the layout and the renderer draws the same black area it draws for a field. Its field list is written out by hand and must match the scanner's and the manifest's, or the export gate reports a finding that *Redact* cannot clear; `test/privacy_scan_redact_parity_test.dart` holds the three lists against each other.
- `export_readiness.dart` — `evaluateExportReadiness`: folds the four separate gates (needs-save, classification, privacy, quality) into the one `ExportReadinessStatus` the status-bar chip and the export dialog both read, in the order the user meets them. `readyPrivacyUnchecked` is the honest answer when `privacyChecksEnabled` is off: the scanner then returns an empty result, so "nothing found" and "not looked at" are indistinguishable from outside, and a green *Ready to export* would be a reassurance nobody gave. Only `needsSave` closes the export dialog; the rest let it open.
- `quality_export_policy.dart` — Gates export by slide-quality issues with warnings.
- `question_round_builder.dart` — `QuestionRoundBuilder.draw`: given the authored `QuestionSpec`, draws the randomised `QuestionView` a question slide shows — which wrong answers join, and in what order — so a viewer cannot replay the previous round. Pulled out of `_FullscreenPresenterState` (the presenter class had drifted over its size ceiling after #865/#872): drawing a round is pure computation over the model (no widget, no `setState`, no window channel), so it moved to a class that is testable on its own, and the presenter keeps only the timer, the rehearsal log and the audience windows. An unwinnable round — no correct answer to give, or too few options — comes back `answerable: false` with its countdown stripped, so it never blocks the presentation. The `Random` is injectable so a test can pin a draw; production keeps a fresh source per round, exactly as the presenter did.
- `rehearsal_controller.dart` — Unit-testable controller tracking elapsed/remaining/per-slide rehearsal timing, plus answered question attempts (`startQuestion`/`finishQuestion`). A round that is started and never finished is dropped, so a question you page past unanswered does not appear; `finishQuestion` without a running round does nothing, so a second verdict on the same attempt is not counted twice.
- `recovery_service.dart` — Auto-saves deck **and document** snapshots for crash/unsaved recovery. The last valid deck Markdown remains separately parseable while an optional `markdownDraft` carries even temporarily invalid, unapplied source-editor work and its deck/slide scope. A snapshot also carries the layers that are *not* in Markdown, including user notes and ink. A `RecoverySnapshot.kind` (presentation | document) records which kind the snapshot is, so recovery restores the right sort of tab; an older snapshot written without the key reads as a presentation (backward compatible). `available` is the honest platform answer (false on web: no application-support directory, so every call is a no-op). *(`kind` added 2026-08-08.)*
- `rich_text_chapters.dart` — Cutting a rich-text body up on its `#` headings into one slide per chapter (`splitRichTextIntoChapters`), with `richTextChapterHeadings`/`richTextChapterCount` for the editor's offer. In Marp `#` *is* a slide's title, so a `#` halfway down a body is an unclosed slide rather than formatting. Three deliberate limits: it never runs while parsing — a deck with headings in a body must come back unchanged, and restructuring during a read is silent data loss — it is not offered on `bulletsImage` (where the picture goes is the author's call, not a default), and a `#` inside a fenced block is source code. Only level 1; a `##` right under a chapter heading becomes that slide's subtitle, because the parser lifts it out on the next read anyway and the slide would otherwise look different after one save. The first chapter keeps the original slide id so annotations and notes stay attached. `DeckNotifier.splitIntoChapters` applies it as **one** mutation, so a single undo puts everything back.
- `rich_text_layout.dart` — Computes pagination and scaling for rich-text markdown bodies. `expandRichTextForRender` is the counterpart of `expandFindingsForRender` for surfaces that *enumerate* slides instead of paging through them (the export): every page becomes a full-size slide tagged with `Slide.renderPage`. Each copy keeps the whole body and the slide id — the page split and the shared font scale are properties of the body as a whole, so handing a renderer one page's markdown in isolation would size that page on its own. `freeMarkdown` is deliberately left out: `slideUsesRichText` counts it, but its preview has no notion of a page and always draws the whole body, so expanding it would yield identical copies rather than continuation pages.
- `rfc3161_timestamp.dart` — Builds a `.tsq` from the seal hash (with a fresh `Random.secure` nonce, minted at the edge so the builder stays a pure conversion) and reads a `.tsr` token far enough to compare its message imprint with that hash. That comparison is the whole check OciDeck performs: no CMS signature, no certificate chain, and no echo check on the nonce — the nonce is not retained, so the other half is gone after a restart. `timeStampEchoesNonce` exists for whoever holds both files. See the file header for why, and what it means for `genTime`.
- `scope_coverage.dart` — `deckScopeCoverageGaps`: flags in-scope objects with no test and no finding.
- `menu_blocks.dart` — the choice-menu slide type's blocks (#1162): `MenuBlock` (label + optional description + optional target anchor + optional image) with `parseMenuBlock`/`menuBlockToBullet` — a block is a plain link-bullet, a `[label]` link to the target slide's anchor, optionally followed by ` — description` (`kMenuDescriptionSeparator`) and an `![](mem:…)` image. Reading is tolerant where writing is exact: behind a link the whole tail is the description and a hand-typed `-`, `–` or `:` in front of it is stripped like the em-dash the writer emits (a linkless block has no brackets to go by and splits on the spaced em-dash only), and unrecognised markup becomes the label rather than an error. `MenuCategory` + `menuCategoriesFor`/`menuBulletsFrom` group those blocks under the existing group-heading bullets (`isGroupHeading`) — the same tussenkop the bullet editor uses, so categories cost no second list to keep in step and reordering, deleting and the file round trip all run over one set of bullets; blocks before the first heading form a nameless first category, and `menuHasCategories` decides whether a category bar is shown at all. `menuBlocksFor` flattens the same bullets, `menuBulletIndexForBlock` maps a block index back onto its bullet so one block can be rewritten without touching the headings around it, `menuGridColumns` is the one column ladder for the grid layout and `menuDiscFraction`/`menuRingRadius` the geometry of the circle layout — derived from the chord between two neighbours rather than guessed, which is what keeps the discs apart from about eight blocks on. All three live here rather than in the preview because the HTML export has to walk the same steps, or the exported slide drifts from what the author saw. Finally, `slidesWithMenuTarget` is the pure list computation behind `DeckNotifier.setMenuBlockTarget` (assigns the target a frozen anchor and points the block at it). The editor (`menu_editor.dart`), preview (`menu_preview.dart`), presenter, HTML export and Beamer export all read blocks through here, so the encoding lives in one place.
- `slide_anchors.dart` — stable in-deck anchors for the non-linear navigation (#1162): `slugifyAnchor` (ASCII-safe slug from a heading, `dia` fallback), `uniqueAnchor` (collision → `-2`, `-3`, …) and `slidesWithJump` (the pure list computation behind `DeckNotifier.setSlideJump` — assigns the target a frozen anchor and points the source slide's `nextAnchor` at it). Pure over the slide list; the presenter resolves an anchor with `_indexOfAnchor` and honours the jump in `_next`/auto-advance, retracing via a navigation stack on back.
- `finding_context_score.dart` — builds the deck's scope-object→CIA index and derives each finding's context (environmental) score / effective severity from it.
- `cockpit_layout.dart` — Flutter-free layout engine of the cockpit slide,
  shared by the painter and the HTML export (design:
  `docs/design/COCKPIT_LAYOUT.md`). `CockpitGridPlan` (columns 1/2/3/2/3/3 for
  one to six meters, an incomplete last row centred), `CockpitCellPlan` (wide
  cell → read-out window beside the dial, narrow cell → below it; dial side,
  bezel radius, window, label strip and the type scale as fractions of the
  cell), `cockpitLongestDigits` (one number size per slide, from the longest
  min/max/value), the character-count text cascade (`fitCockpitText`: fits →
  shrinks to a floor → wraps → only then an ellipsis; `wrapFirst` for units) and
  `cockpitReadoutLines` (number + inline short unit, unit lines, or the
  attitude/heading lines). Every decision is taken on character counts, never
  measured pixels, so the export — which cannot measure text — breaks lines
  exactly where the app does. Pinned by `test/cockpit_layout_test.dart`.
- `finding_pagination.dart` — Render-time pagination for `finding` slides (PENTEST_MIAUW §3.1). A finding stays one Markdown slide but is rendered across several when needed. Page 1 keeps the identity card and greedily adds whole sections while `findingHeaderFitScale` remains at or above the 0.80 readability floor; continuation pages use the same live-render measurement with the actual theme font and logo reserve. This replaces the approximate line budget that left usable space below compact headers and could create three slides where two balanced, readable slides suffice. Continuations keep only a compact `(i/N)` heading, sections remain whole and ordered, and single-section findings stay intact. `_headerCardCost` remains only as a historical calibration seam pinned by `finding_header_cost_test.dart`. The transform never changes the authored `.md`: `expandFindingsForRender` serves deck-enumerating surfaces, while `firstRenderPageSpec` gives thumbnails and the editor the correctly measured first page. Already-paginated pages are recognised through `findingPageMarker` and pass through unchanged.
- `sidecar_format.dart` — The version contract shared by every sidecar next to a `.md`: a file declaring a higher version is not loaded **and** not overwritten. Refusing to read while still saving over it is worse than reading half, not better — the deck holds nothing in memory, so the save reads as "there is nothing here".
- `slide_image_refs.dart` — The one definition of "which images does this slide use": `slideImageRefs`/`slideImagePaths` cover the image fields **and** every `![…](…)` in the rich-text body, and `rewriteSlideImagePaths`/`rewriteInlineImagePaths` rewrite that same set. Written because the answer was spelled out as `imagePath` + `imagePath2` in a dozen files, and stopped being complete the moment a body could carry pictures of its own — a gap is never cosmetic here: the privacy projection would leave a face on a redacted slide, the `mem:` sweep would free bytes still being drawn, and the pool's unused-asset cleanup is irreversible. The regex is deliberately not a markdown parser: every reference must be seen, including one in a line a parser would read as something else. Rewriting touches the path only, never the alt text, which is exactly what the callers (copy-to-project, pool, dedup) need. An empty path is not a reference, so `![alt]()` — what the projection leaves behind — is skipped.
- `secret_store.dart` — Manages secrets (WebDAV credentials, S3 secret access key, git token, AI API key) in the OS keychain. Also holds the privacy scanner's own-identity allowlist (`privacyOwnIdentityKey`): not a credential, but the only setting carrying personal data about a real person. `deleteSecretsOf` keys off the connections that exist rather than off a prefix scan of the keychain — a keychain belongs to the user, and a list we merely *believe* is ours is the list that wipes someone else's password.
- `slide_layout_metrics.dart` — Layout constants/helpers for text sizing, fonts, and fit scaling; `bulletFitCounts` measures how many bullets fit at natural size (the input to the "Split slide" page capacity). Re-exports `table_layout_metrics.dart` and `finding_header_metrics.dart`, so a caller needs one import for slide geometry.
- `finding_header_metrics.dart` — `findingHeaderFitScale`: the content-aware auto-fit for a `finding` header (#1282). Measures the paginated page's content (severity card + the sections on that page) against the slide height and returns a multiplier in `(0, maxScale]` on top of the fixed `kFindingBaseFontScale` (the #1163 down-scale), so a dense header reflows smaller until it fits instead of being shrunk-and-parked by the scaffold, while a sparse one is held at the ceiling. The target deliberately uses seven eighths of the available height: enough margin for a calm layout without reducing realistic finding text to thumbnail size. `kFindingHeaderTypeScale` keeps the supporting identity card a third smaller than the main prose. Kept out of `slide_layout_metrics.dart` so that file stays under the size ratchet; re-exported from it so callers keep one import.
- `table_layout_metrics.dart` — De maatvoering van een tabelslide: celgrootte (`tableCellFontMinimum`/`tableCellFontMaximum`/`tableFitCellSize`), kolombreedtes (`tableColumnWidths`) en blokhoogte (`tableBlockHeight`), plus de celmarges die `table_preview.dart` tekent. `tableFit` is de ingang die de preview gebruikt: de letter groeit tot ze de beschikbare hoogte vult, begrensd door het leesbaarheidsplafond én door `tableWidthCappedCellSize` (groeit ze door, dan eten de kolomminima de tabelbreedte op en breken de koppen alsnog); wat een korte tabel dan nog overhoudt gaat via `tableExtraRowPadding` naar de rijen, in plaats van als gat onder de tabel te blijven staan. De kwaliteitscontrole gebruikt diezelfde `tableFit` op een referentiekader (`tableReferenceWidth`/`tableReferenceAvailableHeight`) om te bepalen of de celtekst werkelijk op het minimum uitkomt — een dichtheidsformule over rijen en kolommen zei dat sinds het verticaal vullen niet meer. Meten en tekenen delen deze geometrie met opzet: liep dat uiteen, dan paste de gemeten hoogte niet bij de getekende tabel. `tableColumnWidths` geeft elke kolom eerst marge plus haar breedste ondeelbare kopwoord en verdeelt pas de rest naar `tableColumnFlexWeights` — puur op tekenaantal verdeeld kreeg een korte-maar-brede kop ("Systemen") minder ruimte dan het woord nodig heeft, brak hij letter voor letter af, en zakte de kolom onder haar eigen celmarge, dan tekende de tekst dwars over de tabellijnen heen.
- `bullet_pagination.dart` — Pure "Split slide" pagination (`chunkBullets`, `splitBulletsIntoPages`/`splitTwoColumnsIntoPages`): fills pages of a fixed size with the remainder last, never leaving a page under `kMinPageBullets`, and halves a list that already fits. Counts bullets and nothing else — measuring what physically fits used to collapse the page size and turn one slide into a stack.
- `split_run.dart` — Split-run logic shared by rendering and the quality check: `splitRunRange` finds the maximal group of same-type bullet slides joined by `continuesSplit`, `splitRunDrag` reports which pages of such a run render needlessly small because one page is far fuller (threshold `kSplitRunDragRatio`), and `canContinueSplitFrom` answers whether offering the editor's continuation switch makes sense. `SplitRunLayoutIndex` prepares every run boundary and shared minimum fit-scale in one linear pass; a weak cache keyed by slide-list identity plus immutable theme identity and font lets the rail, preview, presenter, audience window and rasterizer reuse that work. Normal deck edits replace the list; the live presentation paths explicitly invalidate after their exceptional in-place edits.
- `slide_quality_analyzer.dart` — Checks deck slides for accessibility and readability issues. The missing-media check walks the image fields per slide type *and*, outside that switch, every `![…](…)` in the body — any slide type can carry rich text, and a missing file is the same problem wherever the reference sits; the issue points at field `customMarkdown` (where the user has to go) and names the path (which image). Note that the split-run check (`_checkSplitRuns`, in the density part) runs *outside* the per-slide memo: whether a slide renders small depends on its neighbours, and that cache is keyed on slide identity. `_checkFindingSections` warns (categorie Inhoud) for every `## …`-sectie op een bevinding-**kop**-dia (rol `header`) waarvan de naam geen canonieke sectie of herkende alias is (`FindingSpec.unknownSectionTitles`): zo'n sectie staat wél in de `.md` maar rendert/exporteert niet, dus de melding vertelt de auteur dat hij de kop moet hernoemen — de stille datalek uit #1198. Een detail-/bewijs-dia draagt geen kopkaart en blijft ongemoeid. De thema-contrastreeks staat als top-level `_checkThemeContrast` naast de klasse (klasseplafond) en somt náást de dia-kleuren de drie paren op die alléén op het documentvlak bestaan: `documentHeadingColor` tegen `slideBackgroundColor` (grote tekst), `documentBandTextColor` tegen `effectiveDocumentBandBackgroundColor` (gewone tekst), en `accentColor` tegen diezelfde bandachtergrond — een link in de kop- of voettekst krijgt het accent als kleur, in de app én in de HTML-export. Alle drie alleen wanneer de auteur de kleur zélf zette; geërfd valt het paar samen met 'Thema bodytekst'/'Thema accent', die het al even streng of strenger meten. De accent-melding ankert bewust op `documentBandBackgroundColor` en niet op het accent zelf: dat is een gedeelde kleur die overal elders wél deugt.
- `quality_autofix.dart` — The engine behind **Fix all problems** (#915) and the live fix while presenting (#914). `fixAllStructuralQualityIssues` runs as a fixpoint — analyse, apply the first safe fix, repeat — over structural, reversible fixes that keep every word on screen: cut multi-sentence bullets apart, split overfull slides, detach a dragged split page. It never moves content off a slide; anything needing a human choice (alt text, theme contrast, privacy) is left in the panel. `safeFixForSlide` is the single-slide form the presenter's `F` uses, same order and thresholds so "fix" means one thing in the editor and on stage. Pure — no providers — so it is a plain unit test.
- `slide_quality/split_run_density_summary.dart` — `collapseSplitRunDensity` folds the *length*-driven density warnings that repeat across the pages of one split run into a single warning per kind per run (#1289). The exported set `kLengthDrivenRunDensityKinds` names them — text-density warning, high word count, long average bullet, multi-sentence bullet, deep nesting — because **Split slide** divides *how many* bullets share a page, not how long each one is, so these recur on every continuation page and multiply the count for whoever took the advice to split. It folds only a real run — a `continuesSplit` chain of two-or-more affected pages, delimited by `splitRunRange` — anchors the summary on the first hit, and tags it with `runPages` so `formatSlideQualityIssue` can say how many pages it covers. The critical (error) twins and the count-driven `bulletCountHigh` fall outside the set and stay per page, since a further split does relieve those. Pure and display-only: it runs on the way to `deckQualityProvider`, never in the analyzer that feeds **Fix all problems**, so the raw analysis keeps seeing every page.
- `slide_rasterizer.dart` — Renders on-screen slide previews to PNG for WYSIWYG PDF/PPTX export. Its precache list comes from `slideImagePaths`, so an image in a rich-text body is decoded before the frame is captured instead of arriving after it — that miss produced an empty box in the PDF with nothing failing.
- `text_measurement.dart` — `measureTextHeight`/`measureTextWidth` for rendered text dimensions, plus `measureTextWordWidth`: de breedte van het breedste ondeelbare woord, de ondergrens waaronder een tabelkolom haar kop niet meer heel kan tonen.
- `user_notes_codec.dart` — Serializes per-slide user notes with content fingerprints. Same version rule as `annotation_codec.dart` — it used to read an unknown version `3` as a `2`, which loaded half a file and then wrote that half back.
- `web_asset_store.dart` — In-memory afbeeldingsopslag (`mem:`-paden) voor de webversie; per-pagina levensduur. `retain` ruimt de assets op die nergens meer gebruikt worden; `TabsNotifier.sweepWebAssets` stelt de complete levende verzameling samen (alle tabbladen + ongedaan/opnieuw + klembord) en roept dat aan na dia-verwijdering en opslag.
- `s3/s3_sigv4.dart` — AWS Signature Version 4, written by hand rather than pulled from an SDK: an SDK brings its own HTTP stack and would connect around `NetGuard`, losing the socket pinning every other network source applies. Signing only, no network, so it is testable against fixed vectors (`test/s3_sigv4_test.dart`, cross-checked against botocore).
- `s3/s3_service.dart` — Talks S3 (and S3-compatible endpoints) over a pinned, redirect-free `HttpClient` with SigV4. Lists with a delimiter so prefixes behave as folders. Conditional writes use `If-Match`, but not every S3-compatible endpoint supports them — AWS only since 2024 — so an endpoint that refuses the condition yields `S3Error.conditionalUnsupported` rather than silently overwriting.
- `webdav_service.dart` — Talks WebDAV over a pinned, redirect-free `HttpClient`. Writes are guarded with `If-Match` so a file changed on the server surfaces as `WebdavConflictException` instead of a silent overwrite. `delete` (the one data-mutating verb, for §5.2 log compaction) runs through the same pinned, `followRedirects=false` path and is idempotent (404 = already gone).
- `net/transport_failure.dart` — Sorts a caught `dart:io` exception into what actually went wrong: certificate refused, host unreachable, connection dropped mid-flight, or something else. Shared by all three storage backends, because the question is the same for every one of them and the answer decides both what the user is told and whether a retry has any point. The *translation* into a backend's own exception type stays with that backend — the wording names the WebDAV server, the S3 endpoint or the forge, and those are not interchangeable.
- `presentation_search/presentation_source.dart` — `PresentationSource`: one searchable source of decks for *Slide zoeken*. Local libraries are scanned straight off disk; this abstraction covers the ones that need the network, so the finder can walk them uniformly and in parallel. A source may be slow and may throw — the finder reports per source, so one unreachable connection does not block the rest or the local search.
- `presentation_search/git_presentation_source.dart` — Searches every deck on a git repository's default branch. Reads each `deck.md` through the forge and puts it through the same safety gate as a normal open (scan → marp-sniff → parse), then resolves `repo:` images to in-memory `mem:` paths so previews work. An unreadable or refused deck is skipped, not fatal.
- `presentation_search/remote_presentation_source.dart` — The same for WebDAV and S3: walks the directory tree and reads both loose `.md` files and `.ocideck` packages, again through `FileService.openDeckFromContent`.
- `presentation_search/remote_file_client.dart` — `RemoteFileClient`/`RemoteFileEntry`: a listing detached from the WebDAV- and S3-specific types, so the tree-walking exists once instead of once per protocol.
- `presentation_search/storage_file_clients.dart` — The two adapters that put that interface on top of `WebdavService` and `S3Service`.
- `privacy/image_face_scan.dart` — de beeldcontrole: staat er een herkenbaar gezicht op een dia-afbeelding? Alléén tellen, nooit identificeren — de kop van dat bestand legt uit waarom dat het verschil is tussen een privacycontrole en een biometrische verwerking (EDPB 3/2019 §74-76). `image_face_scan_io.dart` is de native helft (YuNet via OpenCV, multischaal), `image_face_scan_stub.dart` de webhelft die eerlijk meldt dat ze niets kan. De native helft leunt op OpenCV-modules die `dartcv4` 2.x standaard uitsluit — `objdetect` (`FaceDetectorYN`), `dnn`, en de calib3d-keten die objdetect nodig heeft; die staan expliciet in de `hooks: user_defines`-sectie van `pubspec.yaml`, want zonder hen faalt de detector stil op een symbool-fout. Onder `flutter test` laadt de native laag niet, dus `test/image_face_scan_test.dart` bewaakt daar alleen het contract; dat de laag écht werkt (beschikbaar, katten/logo's nul, verminkte JPEG fail-closed) toont `integration_test/native_face_scan_test.dart`, die op een echt platform draait.
- `documentation_service.dart` — Loads the bundled Markdown documentation for the in-app reader. Only a curated subset of `docs/` is bundled (the user, licence/compliance and use-relevant technical docs — see `settings_dialog_docs.dart`); the developer-internal and design docs live in the repository and the reader links there. Locale-aware and forward-looking: for language `xx` it prefers `docs/NAME.xx.md` and otherwise falls back to `docs/NAME.md`, so a translated document can be added later without touching a single call site. A leading HTML/SPDX comment header (as `LICENSE.md` carries) is stripped so it does not show up as literal text. `loadDetailed` also reports whether the base version was served, which is what lets the reader say "this document exists only in English" — every bundled document does, while their titles are registered in 32 languages (#626). The base language is now explicit — `DocumentationService.baseLanguage` (`en`) — so the notice shows for *every* non-English reader that falls back to the base, Dutch included; before #1181 it read `!= 'nl'` on the mistaken assumption that the base was Dutch, which silently withheld it from exactly the Dutch reader. Translated variants are produced by `tool/translate_docs.dart` (`make translate-docs`): it machine-translates the bundled user docs into every app language with a visible "machine translation" banner, and never touches `docs/PRIVACY.md` or `docs/SECURITY_DESIGN.md` (a mistranslated promise is still a promise) — see `test/doc_translation_test.dart` (#1181). `bundledDocAssets` lists the shipped `.md` asset keys (translated `.<lang>.md` variants folded onto their base) from the asset manifest, so the reader can tell an internal link that points at a bundled document (open in the reader) from one that lives only on the repository (open the repository version). *(Corrected 2026-07-31: this said "all 24 bundled documents"; the reader now ships a curated subset, so a fixed count no longer fits — see the keep/repo split in `test/docs_registration_test.dart`.)*
- `duplicate_service.dart` — `DuplicateService`/`DuplicateInfo`: enriches an open-list entry with what else is on disk under that name — byte-identical copies (the same presentation in another place) and name-mates with *different* content. The two are kept apart on purpose: a name-mate is confusing but not a duplicate, and merging one away would destroy work.
- `slide_dedup_service.dart` — `SlideDedupService` + `SlideGroup`/`SlideFieldDiff`: finds slides that recur across decks and says per field where two copies differ. Flutter- and l10n-free (`SlideField` is an enum the widget layer labels), so the comparison is testable without a widget tree.
- `trash_service.dart` — Moves presentation files to the OS trash — never a hard delete, in line with the app's fail-safe line. macOS `~/.Trash`, Linux the XDG trash (`Trash/files` + `.trashinfo`); on Windows and web there is no safe equivalent without platform code, so `isSupported` is false there and the UI hides the clean-up action.
- `package_asset_resolver.dart` — `attachPackageAssetsToMem`: puts an `.ocideck` package's image members in the `WebAssetStore` and rewrites the slide paths that point at them to their `mem:` path. Only members that pass the image validation join, and a reference escaping the package root with `../` is not followed. Shared by opening a package in a tab and by searching packages on a remote source, so both apply the same containment and validation.

### `lib/services/import/` — presentation import (#772)

Reads a foreign presentation file (PowerPoint `.pptx`, LibreOffice Impress
`.odp` and Apple Keynote `.key`) and turns it into a real OciDeck
`Deck`. Deliberately
not a one-to-one copy: OciDeck's slide model is simpler on purpose, so
everything that does not fit becomes a visible "not converted" note slide
rather than disappearing. The whole subsystem works on bytes — no `dart:io` —
so it runs unchanged on web and desktop. Three layers: format-neutral models,
a per-format importer, and a pipeline that classifies and builds.

- `presentation_import_service.dart` — `PresentationImportService`, the single entry point. The heavy read — decode the zip once through the hardened `ImportBudget` helpers, detect the format from that same `Archive`, pick the importer from the registry, parse, and classify every slide — now runs off the UI isolate through `runImportTask` (`pipeline/import_runner.dart`, #875); only the light deck-build stays here on the main isolate, because it touches the process-global `WebAssetStore` and the l10n translator, and neither crosses an isolate boundary. `prepare`/`importBytes` take an optional `ImportCancelToken`; a stopped read returns a `PreparedImportResult.cancelled()` / `PresentationImportResult.cancelled()` with nothing built and nothing written. When a test injects its own `ImporterRegistry` (fake importers, which are not guaranteed sendable) the service runs the same core in-process via `runImportTaskInline` instead of spawning a worker. Decoding once and handing the `Archive` to the importer (`preDecoded`) is deliberate — the same file was unzipped twice before (validate, then import), and a second full copy is exactly the peak-memory regression #874 closes. A budget overrun ends here as tooLarge, before anything heavy runs. After building, the deck's final serialisation is run once more through the fail-closed `MarkdownSafetyScanner` (`scanDeckForUnsafeContent`) — the same gate that guards opening a foreign `.md` (#876). The import never ran that gate before, so a saved import with executable content was refused only on the *next* open; now it is refused at import (bulk/service → `unsafeContent`, the single-file UI → the same alarm as a foreign file). The build-side neutralisation already makes ordinary imports clean, so this only ever fires on genuine injection. Two ways in, because the per-slide decision (#812) falls *between* reading and building. `prepare` stops after classifying and hands back a `PreparedImport` — the `ProblemSlide`s to ask about, plus a `build(policies:)` that finishes the job — inside a `PreparedImportResult` in which exactly one of `prepared` or `failure` is set. `importBytes` is both in one step, for the callers with nothing to ask (the bulk queue, the tests), and returns a `PresentationImportResult` with exactly one of `deck` or `failure` set plus the `ProblemSlide`s the UI has to surface. The split exists so asking is not more expensive than doing: without it, a fifty-slide Keynote would have to be parsed twice to put one question on screen. An import that half-succeeds silently is worse than one that fails.
- `bulk_import_runner.dart` — `BulkImportRunner` (#772): the queue behind importing more than one presentation at once — convert each file through `PresentationImportService.importBytes` and write the deck to the chosen folder as its own `.md`. It passes no `SlideFailurePolicy` and so takes best-effort for every slide: the per-slide decision of #812 belongs to the single-file route, since a question per problem slide across ten files is not a queue any more. Two rules make it usable rather than brittle, and both are the kind that break silently: one failed file never stops the row (it is recorded and the next one runs, because stranding on the first unreadable file of ten is the worst possible answer), and every deck gets a collision-free name — two sources with the same title, or a name the folder already holds, may never overwrite each other. `safeDeckFileStem` mirrors `FileService`'s own sanitiser and adds two things, because this name becomes a path directly instead of passing a system dialog: a leading `-`/`_` goes (a name that reads as an option flag or a hidden file) and the length is capped. Free of `dart:io` like the rest of this subsystem: writing and the existence probe come in as seams (`DeckWriter`, `PathExistsProbe`) from the dialog. Takes an optional `ImportCancelToken` (#875): `shouldStop` still breaks the row between files, but the token also breaks off the file being read right now — the interrupted file counts as not-reached, and nothing of it is written.
- `deck_builder.dart` — `DeckBuilder`/`BuiltDeck`: the orchestrator of the build, on OciDeck's own object model rather than on Markdown text. Per source slide it computes the conversion loss (through `import_loss.dart`) and applies the `SlideFailurePolicy` the caller passed per source-slide index — best-effort (the slide plus its note), image-only (the pictures the source already held, the text dropped), or skip (no slide, only the note saying why) — building the slide(s) and the note through the `SlideFactory`, with the note slide's heading following the choice. Two guards live here rather than in the UI, because both fail silently if they are forgotten: the policy is only consulted for a slide with **real** (non-salvaged) loss, so a clean slide survives even a deck-wide "skip"; and image-only on a slide without a picture falls back to skip, since an image slide without an image is nothing. `analyse` runs the same loss calculation without constructing slides or materialising images, so the dialog can be raised before the deck exists. Holds exactly one `SlideFactory` per deck so its `mem:`-path cache stays consistent across every slide, and takes an `ImportTextTranslator` (default identity) that it hands to the factory so the note content is stored in the user's language (#806). *(Split into `slide_factory.dart` + `import_loss.dart` for #878; name corrected 2026-07-24 from `buildDeck`, and policy behaviour filled in for #812.)*
- `slide_factory.dart` — `SlideFactory` (#878): builds the real `Slide` objects for the classified type and materialises each `SourceImage` and embedded video into the `WebAssetStore` as a `mem:` path, de-duped by content hash through a cache it owns (one factory per deck). Also builds the "not converted" note and deck-note slides through the `UnconvertedTracker`. Every source text field is neutralised on the way in (`sanitizeImportedText`/`…Inline`, #876): the raw-serialised fields (title, subtitle, bullets, quote, section, timeline, free Markdown, chart labels, link text) through the full sanitiser, a table cell and a note through the inline variant that composes with their own escaper; captions and two-column bullets fold whitespace via `singleLine` and lean on the HTML-escaping serialiser plus the service's fail-closed backstop. `isUnsafeUrl` is a top-level function here, shared with `import_loss.dart`, so building a link and reporting a neutralised one use the same decision. `kImportedBulletLimit`/`kImportedTableRowLimit` live here and are re-exported from `deck_builder.dart` for the tests that read them.
- `import_loss.dart` — the pure conversion-loss analysis (#878), side-effect-free and shared by `DeckBuilder.build`/`analyse`: `conversionIssuesFor` gathers a slide's parse-time losses, the classifier's issues, the structural salvage losses (`salvageIssues`: audio, a table beside a chart), the field-drop losses of the chosen slide type (`droppedContentIssues`: a paragraph a bullet slide cannot carry, images past what the type shows) and the neutralised unsafe links (`neutralisedLinkIssues`, via `isUnsafeUrl`). `problemSlide` shapes a slide's real loss for the UI. Reporting loss that is not there is as harmful as hiding it, so each check only names what its type truly drops.
- `core/result.dart` — `Result`/`Ok`/`Err` plus `map`/`andThen`: a malformed source file is an expected outcome, not a crash, so importers report recoverable failures as a value and exceptions stay reserved for programming errors.
- `importers/importer.dart` — the `Importer` contract one format must satisfy: `format`, `displayName`, and `importBytes` with a progress callback, an `ImportBudget`, and an optional `preDecoded` archive (so the service's single unzip is shared rather than repeated). Robustness is part of the contract — a broken part yields a `ConversionIssue` on that slide and the import continues; only "no slide could be read" is an `Err`, and a budget overrun a `tooLarge`.
- `importers/import_failure.dart` — `ImportFailure`: a stable `ImportFailureReason` plus `args` (filename, format, size limit), the message the UI actually shows via `importFailureText`.
