The raw `message` stays as a Dutch technical string for the log — it is never shown to the user, so a French user no longer read "dit bestand is geen herkende presentatie" in Dutch (#806). `withArg` lets the service fill in `bestand`, which the importer deep in parsing does not know. `unsafeContent` (#876) is the reason a built deck is refused because its final serialisation still carries executable content — the fail-closed backstop's verdict. Same shape as `WebdavError`/`S3Error`/`GitForgeError`. (Note the name clash: `file_service.dart` has an unrelated `ImportFailure` enum for the *package* import; this is the *presentation* import.)
- `importers/odp/odp_importer.dart` — the LibreOffice Impress importer. Unzips once, reads the pages from `content.xml` in document order, and salvages the deck theme from `styles.xml`. Shares the whole pipeline with PPTX: it only has to produce `SourceSlide`s.
- `importers/odp/odp_context.dart` — `OdpContext`: the typed view on the unzipped ODF package — part lookup, the namespace-agnostic XML helpers, resolving picture references to their bytes in `Pictures/`, and `graphicProperties` — the `style:graphic-properties` behind a frame's `draw:style-name`, where Impress keeps mirroring and cropping. Parsed parts are cached, so `content.xml` is read once per import.
- `importers/odp/odp_slide.dart` — turns one `draw:page` into a `SourceSlide`: frames into title/outline/body or free positioned text, plus images, tables, charts and speaker notes. An image frame's geometry — `draw:transform` rotation, `style:mirror`, `fo:clip` measured against the image's natural size — is baked into the pixels via `bakeImportGeometry`.
- `importers/odp/odp_text.dart` — a `draw:text-box` into `BodyBlock`s and hyperlinks, including list nesting level (`text:list` depth) which is what becomes the bullet indent.
- `importers/odp/odp_table.dart` — `table:table` into a `SourceTable`, first row as header; spanned cells are flattened and recorded as an issue.
- `importers/odp/odp_chart.dart` — a chart sub-document (`draw:object`) into a best-effort `SourceChart`: type, categories and numeric series survive.
- `importers/odp/odp_theme.dart` — the ODF drawing page and master styles onto OciDeck's style profile (fill colour, text colour, font family), every field nullable with a fallback.
- `importers/pptx/pptx_importer.dart` — the PowerPoint importer. Unzips once, takes the slide order from `presentation.xml`'s `sldIdLst`, resolves each `r:id` through the rels part, and keeps hidden slides marked rather than dropping them. Deck title/author come from `docProps/core.xml`, the theme from `ppt/theme/theme1.xml`.
- `importers/pptx/pptx_context.dart` — `PptxContext`: the typed view on the unzipped OPC package — part lookup, relationship resolution (targets are relative to the owning part's folder), the slide size in EMUs, and the namespace-agnostic XML helpers (`descendantsLocal`/`childLocal`) the other PPTX files share.
- `importers/pptx/pptx_slide.dart` — `parseSlide`: turns one `ppt/slides/slideN.xml` into a `SourceSlide`. The orchestrator that walks the shape tree under a per-component error boundary (#877) and parses charts and tables itself; text shapes go to `pptx_shape.dart` and pictures/video/audio to `pptx_media.dart` (#878).
- `importers/pptx/pptx_shape.dart` — the text-shape reconstruction (#878): `parseShape` (a `p:sp` into a `ShapeText` carrying its paragraphs, font size and box), `resolveTitleAndSubtitle` (which free text box is the title or subtitle when there is no placeholder, scored by font size and vertical position) and `readNotes` (the speaker-notes slide).
- `importers/pptx/pptx_media.dart` — the picture and media reconstruction (#878): `parsePic` (a `p:pic` into a `SourceImage`, skipping logo-sized images and baking the shape geometry — `<a:srcRect>` crop, `flipH`/`flipV`, `rot` — into the pixels), `scanMedia` (the slide rels for the first embedded or linked video and any audio file name) and `picReferencesMissingMedia` (a picture whose bytes are gone — real loss, not a filtered logo).
- `importers/pptx/pptx_text.dart` — `parseTxBody`: a DrawingML `a:txBody` into `BodyBlock`s and hyperlinks, including bullet level and the largest font size (which is what lets the caller tell a title from body text).
- `importers/pptx/pptx_table.dart` — `parseTableXml`: an `a:tbl` into a `SourceTable`, first row as header. Merged cells are flattened here and recorded as an issue by the pipeline — OciDeck's GFM tables have no spans.
- `importers/pptx/pptx_chart.dart` — `parseChartXml`: a chart part into a best-effort `SourceChart`. Type, categories and numeric series survive; axes titles, trendlines and 3D effects do not.
- `importers/pptx/pptx_theme.dart` — `parseTheme`: the DrawingML colour and font scheme onto OciDeck's style profile (`accent1` → accent, `dk1` → text, major Latin typeface → font family). A partial theme is fine; every field is nullable and falls back to the defaults.
- `importers/keynote/key_importer.dart` — the Apple Keynote importer. Unlike PPTX and ODP there is no XML to read: a `.key` keeps its content in Snappy-compressed protobuf under `Index/`, so this file drives the IWA stack and records a deck-wide issue about what the format still holds back. Falls back to `preview.jpg` plus salvaged text when the object graph cannot be reconstructed, and refuses a non-zip up front — since archive 4 a corrupt file decodes to an *empty* archive, which is indistinguishable from an empty deck.
- `importers/keynote/key_context.dart` — `KeyContext`: the typed view on the unzipped Keynote package — part lookup by name, the hardened XML parse, and the entry list the importer needs to find `preview.jpg` and `Data/…`.
- `importers/keynote/key_plist.dart` — reads the deck title and author from `Metadata/Properties.plist`. Only the XML form of a plist is parsed; a binary plist (`bplist00`) yields nothing and the pipeline falls back to the file name, which is better than guessing.
- `importers/keynote/key_text_salvage.dart` — `KeyTextSalvage`: the last resort. Without Apple's runtime typeId registry no object can be *named*, but all character content is UTF-8 inside length-delimited protobuf fields, so every payload is walked and the text-like fields are collected in first-seen order. Noisy, and labelled as such.
- `importers/keynote/iwa/snappy.dart` — `SnappyDecompressor`: pure-Dart Snappy for iWork's framing variant, which carries no CRC32C. Caps both a single block and the whole stream (from the `ImportBudget`), so a crafted `.iwa` cannot claim gigabytes, and accumulates the stream in one `BytesBuilder` rather than a growing `List<int>` copied again into a `Uint8List` — one merge at the end instead of two full buffers (#874).
- `importers/keynote/iwa/proto_wire.dart` — `ProtoWire`/`ProtoMessage`: a schema-free protobuf wire decoder (field number → repeated values), the way `protoc --decode_raw` works. Keynote ships no `.proto` files, so decoding raw and interpreting per field beats maintaining a hand-reconstructed schema — and it is why this subsystem needs no protobuf dependency.
- `importers/keynote/iwa/iwa_archive.dart` — `IwaArchive`/`IwaObject`: splits a decompressed `.iwa` into records (`ArchiveInfo` + payloads), keeping each object's id, numeric type and `object_references` list.
- `importers/keynote/iwa/iwa_document.dart` — `IwaDocument`: the object graph, with the two-step lookup that turns a reference field (an *index* into the owning object's reference list) into the target object, plus data-file name resolution.
- `importers/keynote/iwa/slide_reconstructor.dart` — `SlideReconstructor` (#878): the assembler that reconstructs one slide's content (title, body, images, table, chart, media, notes) and builds the `SourceSlide`. Delegates slide ordering to `slide_order.dart`, text and drawable reconstruction to `drawable_reader.dart`, and tables/charts/media to their existing reconstructors.
- `importers/keynote/iwa/slide_order.dart` — `SlideOrder` (#878): recovers the slides in real presentation order via four routes tried in turn (`TSP.PackageMetadata` Slide components, a `TSP.ObjectContainer`, the SlideNode tree walked depth-first, else parse order), plus the structural "is this a slide" test. Message types are recognised by their field shape because the typeId — class map lives in the Keynote app, not in the file.
- `importers/keynote/iwa/drawable_reader.dart` — `DrawableReader` (#878): reconstructs the text and drawable structure of one slide — placeholder and note text, the text of an arbitrary drawable (walking the reference graph, avoiding cycles), flattening group drawables into their leaf children, and the images a drawable carries from `Data/`. Structural type detection, like the rest of the IWA stack. An image's geometry — the angle and the mirror flags in the `TSD.GeometryArchive`, and the visible window of the `TSD.MaskArchive` behind `ImageArchive` field 5 — is baked into the pixels via `bakeImportGeometry`; Keynote honours the EXIF orientation, so that geometry is measured on the upright photo.
- `importers/keynote/iwa/table_reconstructor.dart` — `TableReconstructor`: `TableInfo → TableModel → DataStore → Tile → row` down to the cell grid. Single-tile tables (a slide table, in practice) are handled; anything wider is recorded as an issue rather than half-read.
- `importers/keynote/iwa/table_cell_reader.dart` — `TableCellReader`: iWork's table cells are a custom binary format, not protobuf. Walks the V3/V4/V5 field bitmasks for text, numbers, dates, booleans, durations and errors; anything undecodable becomes an empty cell, never a crash.
- `importers/keynote/iwa/table_data.dart` — `TableData`: the string, error and rich-text lookup tables a `DataStore` points at, resolved once and offered as `valueAt(index)` to the cell reader.
- `importers/keynote/iwa/chart_reconstructor.dart` — `ChartReconstructor`: the `ChartArchive` extension on a drawable into a `SourceChart`. Exotic and 3D types are mapped to the nearest supported one and reported, so a chart never silently changes meaning.
- `importers/keynote/iwa/media_reconstructor.dart` — `MediaReconstructor`: a `MovieArchive` into a `SourceVideo` (embedded file, plain URL, or YouTube/Vimeo, recognised by host rather than by substring) plus its poster frame. Audio-only media has no OciDeck slide type and is returned as a file name for the "not converted" note.
- `models/source_format.dart` — `SourceFormat`: the formats the import recognises. The user-facing name per format lives in the UI layer with its translation, not here.
- `models/source_deck.dart` — `SourceDeck`: the parsed presentation — slides in source order plus deck-level title, author, theme, and the deck-wide issues that become one trailing note slide.
- `models/source_slide.dart` — `SourceSlide` and `PositionedText`: the format-neutral shape of one slide. Every salvageable feature has a field, which is exactly what makes the "not converted" note accurate; `PositionedText` keeps the raw geometry so the classifier can merge free-floating boxes in reading order.
- `models/body_block.dart` — `BodyBlock`/`BodyBlockKind`: one block of body text with its nesting level and reading order — the unit the classifier counts to decide bullets, quote or free Markdown.
- `models/source_table.dart` — `SourceTable`: the raw grid (header row plus data rows).
- `models/source_chart.dart` — `SourceChart`/`SourceChartSeries`/`SourceChartType`: the chart data. The type set mirrors `models/chart.dart` so a salvaged chart maps one to one; whether the numbers end up inline or in a `data/` file is the writer's decision, not this model's.
- `models/source_image.dart` — `SourceImage`: bytes plus extension, with a SHA-256 content identity so the same picture used on ten slides is stored once.
- `models/source_video.dart` — `SourceVideo`/`SourceVideoKind`: an embedded file, a plain URL or a YouTube/Vimeo reference, with an optional poster frame.
- `models/source_theme.dart` — `SourceTheme`: the colours, font, footer and page-number flag salvaged from the master. Every field nullable, so a half-read theme never breaks the style profile.
- `models/conversion_issue.dart` — `ConversionIssue`: one feature that could not be mapped, with what was salvaged of it (if anything). The record that makes the import lossless in the only sense that matters — the user is told.
- `models/slide_failure_policy.dart` — `SlideFailurePolicy`: what to do with a slide that carries real loss — `bestEffort` (keep it, with the note beside it), `skip` (drop the body, keep the note), or `imageOnly` (keep the pictures the source held, one or two, and drop the text). Lives with the models so it can be persisted without a models → pipeline dependency. *(Corrected 2026-07-24: this said "fall back to its first image", which understated it — a source with two pictures becomes a two-image slide.)* `imageOnly` was called `rasterize` until #812; the name is now what it does. The project this was ported from ran LibreOffice to render a source slide to a bitmap, and that is deliberately not available here — the import starts no subprocess and adds no external dependency — so a name promising a rendering was promising something absent.
- `pipeline/format_detector.dart` — `detectFormatFromBytes`/`validateFormatFromBytes`/`validateFormatFromArchive`: extension first, then the ZIP magic, then a format-specific marker entry inside the archive (`ppt/presentation.xml`, ODP's `mimetype`, Keynote's `Index/*.iwa`). Validating rather than guessing, so a renamed or truncated file is refused with a reason instead of parsed into nonsense. The `…FromArchive` form takes an already-decoded archive so the service can validate and import without unzipping twice (#874).
- `pipeline/importer_registry.dart` — `ImporterRegistry`: format → importer, with a `register` seam for tests and for the importers still to land.
- `pipeline/import_task.dart` — the serialisable import contract plus the shared parse core (#875). `ImportRequest` (bytes, filename, `ImportBudget`) goes in, a sealed `ImportTaskResult` (`ImportTaskParsed` / `ImportTaskFailed` / `ImportTaskCancelled`) comes out; `ImportProgress` carries a 0..1 fraction and the raw Dutch message the UI wraps in `l10n.d`. `parseAndClassify` is the lifted body of the old `prepare` steps — decode, validate, import, classify — now budget-parametrised and cooperatively cancellable: it checks `isCancelled` and the `maxDuration` deadline at each bounded work unit (before decode, before the parse, per slide) and yields the event loop between them. `ImportCancelToken` is the main-side cancel signal; it is deliberately *not* part of the const, isolate-copied `ImportBudget`, because an isolate shares no memory and a copied flag would never reach the worker. `ImportTaskFailed` sanitises `ImportFailure.cause` to a string so the failure is guaranteed sendable. `runImportTaskInline` runs the core on the calling isolate — the web path, and the path the service takes when a test injects its own registry.
- `pipeline/import_runner.dart` — `runImportTask`: dispatch to the right execution path per platform via a conditional import (`_io`/`_web`), the same idiom as `platform/platform_features.dart`. `debugImportTaskRunner` is a `@visibleForTesting` hook that forces the in-process path: a widget test runs under a fake-async clock and cannot drive a real isolate (a port message arrives on the real event loop, outside that clock, so `pumpAndSettle` would never see the import finish), so such a test sets it to `runImportTaskInline`.
- `pipeline/import_runner_io.dart` — the desktop/mobile path: spawn a worker isolate, stream progress and the one result back over a single `ReceivePort` (FIFO, so ordered), and settle exactly once behind a latch. Cancelling *kills* the worker — immediate and safe, because the worker owns no external resource and nothing is half-built (the deck-build runs after this, on the main isolate). Cooperative signalling could not reach the worker mid-parse (one work unit gives the event loop no turn), so killing is both stronger and simpler.
- `pipeline/import_runner_web.dart` — the browser path: no second isolate exists, so it runs `parseAndClassify` in-process. The shared core's yields and the real cancel flag keep an interrupt and a repaint coming through *between* work units; within one unit (the importer parsing the whole deck) web blocks, bounded by the budget's memory and time limits.
- `pipeline/slide_classifier.dart` — `classifySlide`/`ClassifiedSlide`: which `SlideType` fits a source slide, and what was recorded as lost while deciding. Deterministic and side-effect-free, so the mapping is unit-testable without any file.
- `pipeline/problem_slide.dart` — `ProblemSlide`: a slide with real loss, in the shape the UI needs — the 1-based number the user sees, the issue descriptions, whether there was an image (so the image-only fallback is only offered when it exists), and the suggested policy.
- `pipeline/parse_guard.dart` — `guardParse`/`guardParseVoid` + `classifyParseCause`/`IssueComponent`/`IssueCause`: the per-component error boundary (#877). Runs one sub-parse (a chart, table, media part, notes block or a whole slide) and, on any throw, records a typed `ConversionIssue` (component + cause) in the caller's sink and returns a fallback so the rest of the slide and every following slide survive — the importers used to parse a whole deck under one broad `try/catch`, so one malformed part failed the lot. Logs only the operation, component, cause and error *type*, never the error object: an XML/format error carries a slice of the parsed source in its message, and that can be personal data. The `ParseLogSink` seam (default `logError`) is the one place that privacy contract is testable. PPTX/ODP/Keynote share it, so their boundaries stay equivalent.
- `pipeline/unconverted_tracker.dart` — `UnconvertedTracker`: builds the free-Markdown body of the "not converted" note slides, per source slide and once for the deck. Takes an `ImportTextTranslator` (`String Function(String)`, default identity): the note is **content** written into the user's `.md` and kept there, so it is translated at import time and stored in the user's language — a Greek user otherwise kept Dutch paragraphs in his own file for good, travelling to every other Marp tool (#806). The service layer cannot import l10n, so the UI injects `l10n.d`. `{naam}` placeholders are filled *after* translating, so each language decides where the value lands. Guarded by `test/import_note_l10n_test.dart`, which pulls every note/progress source string from the AST and requires all 31 translations.
- `utils/import_text_sanitizer.dart` — `sanitizeImportedText`/`sanitizeImportedInline` (#876): makes attacker-controlled text from an imported presentation safe to land as *literal* text in the deck's `.md`. Neutralisation happens at the import boundary (a settled choice), so the `.md` format for the user's own decks is untouched. HTML-escaping `&`/`<`/`>` defuses every `<tag…` vector the `MarkdownSafetyScanner` knows (and the numeric-entity evasion, `&` first — the scanner decodes numeric but not named entities, so `&lt;` is a safe inert form); `[`→`\[` and `](`→`]\(` break link/image syntax and the scanner's `](javascript:` detection without touching prose parentheses; the full variant also folds newlines and escapes a leading block marker so a value can't become a heading, list, rule or table. The `…Inline` variant skips the folding, backslash-doubling and leading-marker steps for a context that already has its own structural escaper (a table cell's `encodeMarkdownTableCell`, a note's `-->` escaper), so the two compose without double-escaping. The contract is testable against the very gate it feeds: for any input the built deck must pass `MarkdownSafetyScanner`.
- `utils/import_budget.dart` — `ImportBudget`/`ImportBudgetException` (#874): one place that answers "where is the limit?" for every resource a foreign file may drive — source bytes, archive entries, per-entry and total uncompressed size, XML part length, Snappy block and stream, slides, IWA objects. The production `standard` is generous for a real deck and strict for a crafted one; `forTest` shrinks every axis so an overrun is reachable with a few bytes instead of a real gigabyte. Threaded through the zip gate, the Snappy decoder and the three importers, so the numbers live here rather than scattered across five files. Since #875 it also carries the `maxDuration` deadline, which the worker checks itself at each work unit (an overrun ends as `tooLarge` with `durationLabel`); the cancel *token* stays out of this const, isolate-copied object and is delivered out-of-band by the runner (see `pipeline/import_task.dart`). An overrun ends the import as `ImportFailureReason.tooLarge` with a readable `{limiet}`.
- `utils/archive_utils.dart` — `safeDecodeZip`: the zip gate, now driven by the `ImportBudget`. Caps the input size, the *entry count* (a million tiny entries is as harmful as one huge one), and — reading the declared uncompressed sizes from the central directory *before* decompressing anything — the per-entry and total uncompressed size, so a zip bomb is refused. A budget overrun is an `ImportBudgetException` (→ tooLarge); a missing zip header stays a `FormatException` (→ corrupt/not-a-presentation), because "too big" and "broken" are different answers.
- `utils/xml_utils.dart` — `parseXmlSafe`: caps the document length (from the `ImportBudget`) and strips any `<!DOCTYPE …>` block before parsing, so entity declarations are never processed at all and the "billion laughs" class of attack has nothing to expand.
- `utils/safe_extensions.dart` — `normalizeVideoFileName`: the source file decides what its attachments are called, so a Keynote video named `clip.command` would otherwise carry that name into the deck's `media/` folder. Only whitelisted video extensions survive; everything else becomes `mp4`. Images need no counterpart — they travel as bytes and are named by the builder.

### `lib/services/git/` — Git-repository storage (design: `docs/design/GIT_STORAGE.md`)

Reading, writing, concept branches, review PRs, merges and release tags, over
the forge REST plane (all platforms) and — on desktop, when `git` is present —
over a real partial clone. Three forges behind one interface. Cross-deck search
ships (`deck_search.dart` + its shell UI). Open: asset deletion (§6.2,
deliberately manual).

- `git_forge.dart` — The provider-agnostic `GitForge` interface (`probe` for the connection test, `listTree`, `readBlob`, `headSha`, `close`; the release surface `listBranches`/`createBranch`/`listTags`/`createTag`/`openPullRequest`/`mergePullRequest` (with a `deleteBranch` flag to prune the merged branch)/`pullRequestForBranch`) plus its value types (`BranchRef`, `TagRef`, `PullRequestRef` carrying head/base, `PullRequestMergeMethod`), `RepoProbe` (default branch, emptiness, push rights — what the settings dialog's *Test connection* reports), `GitForgeException` and the `listDecks` extension. Everything git itself has no notion of lives behind this. Also the optional `CodeSearchCapable` capability (a separate interface, not a `GitForge` method, because most forges cannot do this and the base contract must only promise what every adapter can keep): `searchDeckCodeDirs` returns the deck dirs a server-side search matched, or `null` when the forge/instance can't — index-based, so best-effort. Only `GitLabForge` implements it; Gitea/Forgejo has no endpoint at all, and GitHub's is word/token-based and would silently miss partial-word matches.
- `forge_http.dart` — `ForgeHttp`, the HTTP plumbing the three adapters share: `getJson`/`post`/`sendJson` over the transport, the status-to-`GitForgeException` translation, the JSON decode and the ref check, plus the `kForgeMax*` caps. Not a new layer — `GitForge` remains the contract upwards; this only stops the same code living in three files. What genuinely differs is supplied by the adapter: the URL shape (`apiUri`), the auth header (`headers`), the name the forge carries in an error message (`forgeName`) and whether a 409 means "the repository is empty" (`treats409AsEmptyRepo`, true for GitHub and Gitea, false for GitLab, which uses 409 for a collision).
- `gitea_forge.dart` — Forgejo/Gitea adapter (one adapter: same REST surface). The only place provider-specific knowledge may live. The release ops map onto the REST endpoints (`branches`, `tags`, `pulls`, `pulls/{n}/merge`) through the shared `ForgeHttp` skeleton over its own `apiUri`/`headers`. Gitea/Forgejo deliberately does **not** implement `CodeSearchCapable`: there is no REST code-search endpoint (go-gitea/gitea#31375).
- `github_forge.dart` — GitHub adapter (github.com and Enterprise). Differs from Gitea where it matters: a multi-file commit is four round-trips through the Git Data API (blob → tree → commit → non-forcing ref update), and that last step *is* the concurrency guard; the API host differs from the web host; auth is `Bearer`; an annotated tag is two calls; pruning a merged branch is a separate `DELETE`. Deliberately does **not** implement `CodeSearchCapable`: `/search/code` exists, but its index is word/token-based, so a partial word (`dekk` in `dekking`) does not match while the local scan searches substrings — as a pre-filter it would systematically skip decks the user meant. GitHub search goes through `git grep` or the full scan instead.
- `gitlab_forge.dart` — GitLab adapter (gitlab.com and self-hosted). One commit call again, but an `actions[]` list that must name `create`/`update`/`delete` per file, guarded by `start_sha`; a project is one URL-encoded `owner/repo` segment; auth is `PRIVATE-TOKEN`; a merge request is addressed by its per-project `iid`. Its tree listing carries no file size — recorded via the contract's `reportsBlobSize` flag rather than faked. Implements `CodeSearchCapable` via the project-scope `search?scope=blobs&ref=…` (any branch); needs Advanced/Exact Search on the instance, so an empty answer declines with `null`.
- `git_transport.dart` — The HTTP layer under the forge; carries no provider knowledge, not even the auth header (that differs per forge).
- `git_transport_factory.dart` — Conditional export: pinned `dart:io` on desktop, browser fetch on web.
- `git_transport_io.dart` — Desktop: `NetGuard.safeResolveTrusted` + socket pin + no redirects + byte cap.
- `git_transport_web.dart` — Web: browser fetch with the same-origin fetch-proxy as fallback, but never for a request carrying a token.
- `native_git_mirror_api.dart` / `native_git_mirror_io.dart` / `native_git_mirror_stub.dart` (+ `_factory`) — `NativeGitMirror`, the desktop working copy as a real partial clone (`--filter=blob:none`). Satisfies the `DeckMirror` storage contract (via a `FileDraftStore` over the clone tree) and adds `commitDeck`/`sync`/`prepareForOpen`/`history` (`git log -- <deckDir>`, each entry flagged pushed/unpushed): save is a real `git commit` + best-effort push (durable offline; a rejected push is kept local, not a lost update). `commitDeck` takes an optional `workBranch`/`forkFrom` (D3): it checks out that branch — `git branch` then `git checkout`, not `checkout -b`, since the hardened runner puts operands after `--end-of-options` — or creates it off `forkFrom`, then commits and pushes it; push and unpushed-tracking follow the checked-out branch. `mergeRemote` handles a rejected push (§8.6): fetch, `git merge-base` for the *real* common ancestor, let git merge the rest of the tree (pool blobs are content-addressed and just come along) and hand `deck.md`'s three versions to a caller-supplied resolver, then record a true two-parent merge commit so the next push fast-forwards — pushed only when the resolver came out clean. The resolver's answer is applied **additively**: `files` is written, `deletes` is removed, and the rest of the deck folder is left alone. It used to empty the folder first, which deleted every file the resolver had not thought of — the chart data on every merge, clean ones included (#670). The resolver is handed three `deck.md` blobs and a per-side reader, so that completeness was never his to promise; `commitDeck` keeps replacing the folder, because there the app *does* know the whole set. Token via `GIT_CONFIG_*`, never `.git/config`. The `_GitHistoryDialog` in `shell_actions_git_dialogs.dart` renders the timeline. Also `grepDeckDirs` — a fixed-string, binary-skipping `git grep -l` over the working tree (the needle rides as an operand behind `--end-of-options`; exit 1 "no matches" is an empty answer, not an error; a leading-dash term or a foreign branch declines with `null`) — which powers `NativeGrepShortlister`, the search accelerator (§9.3).
- `git_cli.dart` / `git_cli_io.dart` / `git_cli_web.dart` (+ `_factory`) — the hardened native-`git` runner (§10.2), the only place in the tree that may spawn a process. `NativeGitCli` (io) builds a shell-free argv (user data as operands after `--end-of-options`), a genuinely closed environment — `includeParentEnvironment: false` plus an allowlist carrying only what a process needs to start (plus `LC_ALL=C` and an empty `LANGUAGE`, so git always answers in English — `isPushRejection` reads that output, and a translated "non-fast-forward" would be classified as "offline" and silently queue the user's work instead of reporting the conflict), so no `GIT_TRACE_*` from the user's shell can write the token's `Authorization:` header to a trace and no `GIT_ASKPASS`/`GIT_SSH_COMMAND`/`GIT_CONFIG_PARAMETERS` can steer git (OQ-10), supplies the token via `GIT_CONFIG_*` never argv, caps output and enforces a timeout — and stops waiting on the output pipe shortly after git exits, because a helper process that inherited that pipe (a credential helper, `git-remote-https`) can outlive git and would otherwise hold the call open indefinitely, past its own timeout; `probe()` finds usable git (≥2.19) with the macOS xcode-select guard. The web half is an honest unavailable stub.
- `asset_pool.dart` — The shared content-addressed pool (`repo:assets/<sha256>.<ext>`): SHA-256 naming, fetch-once cache, and re-hashing of every fetched blob — a hash-named path from an untrusted forge proves nothing until checked. `refFor`/`existing` are the save side: hash bytes to a ref, skip blobs already pooled.
- `repo_asset_resolver.dart` — `resolveRepoAssetsToMem` and its counterpart `repoAssetBytes`. `resolveRepoAssetsToMem`: the other direction — every `repo:` image reference in a deck becomes an in-memory `mem:` path with the bytes from the pool. Shared by opening a git deck in a tab and by searching one (`presentation_search/git_presentation_source.dart`), so both see the same pictures. An asset that cannot be fetched or turns out not to be an image is skipped rather than fatal: the deck opens with a placeholder where the picture belongs, exactly like a package with a broken reference. `repoAssetBytes(projectPath)` is the write side: where an image's bytes come from when writing to a repo — the web store for a `mem:` path, otherwise disk, relative to the deck's project. It sat spelled out five times in the state layer, once per write path, and a sixth path that forgot it would write a broken reference without anything going visibly wrong.
- `deck_repo_sidecars.dart` — the sidecars of a deck inside a repository (#541/#651/#756): `deck.user-notes.json`, `deck.ink.json`, `deck.dismissals.json`, `deck.miauw.json` and `deck.seal.json`, the first four written indented next to `deck.md` so line-based diffs and merges outside OciDeck stay readable (the seal is compact: set in one act, never merged), each read back by its `withRepo…` function, and each guarded by one shared state check (`repoUserNotesState`/`repoInkState`/`repoDismissalsState`/`repoMiauwState`/`repoSealState` over `_repoSidecarState`): a file this build cannot read — conflict markers, a newer version, over the byte cap — is *untouchable*, in both directions, because overwriting it is as destructive as deleting it and it may be someone else's work. `writeRepoSidecar` applies the shared write/delete rule (delete only what we could have written). Split out of `deck_repo_serializer.dart` when that file hit its line ceiling; the serializer re-exports it, so importers see one surface.
- `deck_repo_serializer.dart` — `buildDeckRepoFiles`: turns a deck into its repo file set — `deck.md` bytes plus the missing image blobs, images rewritten `mem:`→`repo:`. The exact inverse of the open path's `repo:`→`mem:`; video/audio are reported, not written as broken refs. Chart data gets its own file next to `deck.md` at the path its `source` names (`chartDataFilesOf` writing, `withRepoChartData` reading) — deliberately not in the content-addressed pool, since a hash path turns every edited cell into a new file instead of a diff. The sidecars (notes, ink, set-aside privacy findings, since #756 the MIAUW disposition, and since #541's closing round the seal) travel too; their names, read paths and touch rules live in `deck_repo_sidecars.dart` (re-exported from here). `repoFileReaderFor` adapts a forge to the `RepoFileReader` contract — notFound becomes null, because "absent" and "unreachable" are different answers and reading every throw as "do not touch" meant no sidecar was ever written to a branch it did not already exist on. `mirrorDeckFiles` is the same set for the offline working copy, and what is missing there gets *deleted* on the next sync — hence one function with one list. `withRepoSidecars` re-attaches every layer in one step, and every read path must use it — including the native open path, which skipped it and so let the next save write a deck folder without them (#670). `chartDataPathsOf` names the data files a deck refers to *without* needing the numbers in memory, which is what the merge's refusal path needs to keep them. `resolveRepoDeckMerge` resolves a native three-way merge including those layers, and returns the deletes it wants rather than relying on the folder being cleared; `mergeIntoRemote` is the REST-plane equivalent after a rejected push — read their side, merge, and commit if it came out clean, returning their head so the tab can adopt it as its new base *even when it did not*. The `gitDeckOmissions` warning that used to live here is gone (#541): with media, chart data, notes, ink and the set-aside findings all travelling, it had no true line left. `gitRefusesSealedDeck` is gone too (#541, closing round): git is a filesystem here, not an enforcer — the seal travels as a sidecar, and what a seal *means* is guarded by the app keeping a sealed deck read-only (D13).
- `deck_mirror.dart` — `DeckMirror` interface + `DraftMirror`: the durable offline working copy a save falls back to. Text only, one draft per deck (`hasRealHistory == false`). `NativeGitMirror` is the other implementation: it makes real local commits (`hasRealHistory == true`) and serves the history dialog.
- `draft_store.dart` / `draft_store_factory.dart` / `draft_store_io.dart` / `draft_store_web.dart` — the mirror's storage: files on desktop (`FileDraftStore`), the browser key/value store on web (`PrefsDraftStore`), picked by conditional import.
- `offline_queue.dart` — `queueDeckSave` and `poolPendingDeck`: the two halves of working offline against a repo. The first parks a save — text to the working copy, intent to the outbox — and deliberately leaves the images as `mem:` references, because offline the blobs cannot go up anyway and their bytes are still in memory. The second unpacks it just before the commit and pools those references into `repo:` blobs (§8.3). They live in one file because that is where their contract is written down: what the first leaves unpooled is exactly what the second must pool, and nothing else states it. Both were private methods on `TabsNotifier` until #518; neither is state work — no tab, no Riverpod, no current selection, just a mirror, an outbox and a forge. What *was* state work stayed behind: invalidating the queue badge and carrying this one request's warnings.
- `outbox.dart` — `Outbox`/`PendingCommit`: the per-deck queue of not-yet-pushed saves, in `shared_preferences` so it survives restart. Carries the intent (deckDir, branch, message, `baseSha`, and an optional `forkFrom` so a work branch queued offline can be created on flush), never the bytes — the mirror holds those. Keyed per repository (`GitRepoConfig.storageSlug`): a repo is a trust boundary, and an unscoped queue meant two repos with a same-named deck overwrote each other's pending commit while a flush pushed the lot to whichever forge happened to be configured. `adoptLegacyEntries()` carries commits from the unscoped era over to the repo that was configured then — that work has reached no server yet, so leaving it behind would silently strand it.
- `sync_engine.dart` — `SyncEngine`: drains the outbox against the forge (`flush`/`flushDeck`), with `baseSha` conflict detection and content-based idempotency (a commit that already landed is skipped, not duplicated). When a queued commit carries a `forkFrom`, the flush creates its work branch (off `forkFrom`) if it is not there yet — so a review round that began offline still lands (D3). A `DeckFilePreparer` hook runs just before each commit — how `flushGit` pools an offline-added image into the reconnect commit; idempotency and deletes compare the deck-dir files only, not the pool blobs.
- `work_branch.dart` — `workBranchFor` and `roundBaseSha`: which branch a save lands on, and whether it forks there itself (D3: editing happens on a work branch, never straight on the default branch). Mid-round it stays where the tab already is; otherwise it starts or resumes today's round at `decks/<name>/<date>`. It decides two things at once, and the second is the subtle one: the commit the guard will collide against. An empty `baseSha` is a statement — *there is no ancestor* — and the caller refuses the save rather than writing over what is there. `roundBaseSha` then turns that choice into the commit the guard collides against: freshly forked, its own new head; already existing (a second round today, or a colleague who was quicker), deliberately **not** its head — taking that would satisfy the guard by definition and overwrite what is there.
- `deck_merge.dart` — `mergeDeckVersions`: a three-way merge of one deck against its common ancestor (§8.6). Per *slide*, not per line — a text merge would leave conflict markers in `deck.md`, and an unparseable deck is what you cannot show the user while they choose. Resolves what it can (one-sided edits, identical edits, both-deleted, reorders) and returns the rest as `SlideConflict`, each pointing at where its provisional choice sits in the merged deck so the dialog can swap the other side in. Two fail-safes: a conflict keeps *our* side provisionally, never silently theirs, and the deck TLP becomes the stricter of the two. The deck-level layers merge alongside the slides: notes and ink re-anchor to the merged deck, the set-aside findings merge via `mergeDismissals`, and since #756 the MIAUW disposition via `mergeMiauw` — union per EIS id, the decision taken last wins, a tombstone wins the tie.
- `version_diff.dart` — `diffDeckVersions`: what changed between two released versions (§9.5). A deck has no slide IDs, so matching is two-pass — first on content signature (identical slides find each other even after a reorder → *moved*), then on same-type similarity, so a reworded slide reads as one *edited* change instead of an addition plus a deletion. Leans on `SlideDedupService` for `signatureOf`, `similarity` and the per-field `diff`.
- `deck_search.dart` — `DeckSearch`: text search across every `deck.md` in the repo (§9.3), the text twin of `asset_index.dart`. Hits carry deck, slide index, slide title and a windowed snippet; slide attribution reuses the parser's fence-aware `splitSlideBlocks` (a naive `split('---')` would read a `---` inside a code block as a slide boundary and point at the wrong slide) without needing the deck to parse. Deliberately the opposite failure direction from the asset index: an unreadable deck shortens the answer and is reported alongside it, rather than refusing — every hit shown is true regardless. `truncated` means a real match went unreported, not that the list is exactly full. A `DeckShortlister` seam lets an accelerator pre-filter *which* decks to read (K instead of N) while the hit-building stays identical; `NativeGrepShortlister` (in `native_git_mirror_api.dart`) is the forge-agnostic, exhaustive one — `git grep` over the local clone — so on desktop only the matching decks are read. Without a clone (web), `ServerCodeSearchShortlister` uses a `CodeSearchCapable` forge's server-side search — only GitLab implements it — marked `bestEffort`. Can neither help, the shortlister is absent and the full scan runs. `DeckSearchResult.coverage` records whether the answer is `exhaustive` (grep or full scan) or `bestEffort` (indexed server search, which the dialog flags as possibly lagging).
- `asset_index.dart` — `AssetIndex`: the reverse index over the shared pool (§9.3). One pass over the repo — every `deck.md`, then `assets/` — inverted into asset → the decks that reference it, plus (with `includeReleases`) the release tags that still do. References are found by scanning the raw markdown rather than parsing it, because a slide type the parser skips would hide a reference and a missed reference marks an asset as unused. Two answers with deliberately different standards of proof: `decksUsing` may answer from an incomplete round (whoever is listed really is a user), `unusedAssets` may not — an unreadable deck or release could be the one user, and deleting is irreversible (P2), so it always scans releases too and throws when anything failed to read. Rendered by `_AssetUsageDialog`.

### `lib/services/improvement/stats/` — the statistics engine (design: `docs/design/PROCESS_IMPROVEMENT.md` §4)

The arithmetic of the Procesverbetering module, and only the arithmetic. One
library split into `part` files, the shape `lib/services/cvss/` already uses, so
the 1000-line file ratchet is served without scattering the subject. Pure Dart:
no Flutter, no network, no assets, no state, and no new dependency — there is no
statistics package worth the supply-chain surface. Four decisions run through
every file and are worth knowing before reading one: it **refuses rather than
guesses** (`StatsRefusal`, thrown when the data cannot support the number that
was asked for — a Cpk from four observations is arithmetically possible and
methodologically worthless); the **1.5σ shift is off by default and named in
every sigma-level result**, because "sigma level" means two different numbers in
the field and the shift is exactly where two people get two answers; **Cp/Cpk
never travels alone**, each capability result carries the Anderson-Darling
verdict beside it; and variance goes through **Welford**, regression through
**Householder QR**, because the naïve alternatives lose all precision on exactly
the data an improvement project collects — a tight spread far from zero.

- `stats.dart` — the library head: the design decisions above written down where they bind, the `part` list, `StatsRefusal` and its `_requireAtLeast` helper, and `improvementStatsFactorRows` for the module card on Settings → Uitbreidingen (the mirror of `cvss4LookupSize`: the screen shows a number instead of a promise).
- `descriptives.dart` — `Descriptives.of`: count, mean, variance, sd, skewness and kurtosis in one Welford pass, plus the order statistics (median, Tukey hinge quartiles, IQR) and `movingRanges`. The one-pass shortcut `Σx² − (Σx)²/n` is deliberately absent; `test/services/improvement/stats/nist_strd_test.dart` shows the data where it collapses.
- `constants.dart` — the control-chart factors (d2, d3, c4, A2, A3, B3/B4, D3/D4, E2) for subgroup sizes 2–25. Computed from their integral definitions rather than transcribed from a table, so the published values are the *test* and not the source; a size outside the table is refused.
- `control_charts.dart` — the charts themselves: I-MR, X-bar R, X-bar S, and the attribute family p/np/c/u, each returning per-point limits (a p chart's limits vary with the subgroup size, so a single triple would be a lie). Handles stages, where the limits are recomputed from a chosen point onwards.
- `rules.dart` — the run rules over a computed chart: the Nelson set and the Western Electric set, as two named families rather than one merged list, because they disagree about numbering and a violation must be able to say which book it comes from. A stage boundary breaks a run instead of continuing it.
- `capability.dart` — Cp, Cpk, Pp, Ppk and Cpm, with the within-subgroup sigma taken from the moving range when the caller does not supply one, and the Anderson-Darling verdict attached. Also the conversions `DPMO ↔ yield ↔ sigma level`, each carrying whether the 1.5σ shift was applied.
- `distributions.dart` — normal, Student-t, chi-squared and F: CDF, survival and quantile, over the shared special functions (`lnGamma`, the regularized incomplete gamma and beta, `erf`/`erfc`). Everything above that needs a p-value comes through here.
- `inference.dart` — the hypothesis tests: one-sample, two-sample (Welch and pooled) and paired t, one-way ANOVA, Levene, chi-squared goodness-of-fit and independence, the one- and two-proportion z tests, the F test on two variances, and `andersonDarlingNormality`. Each result carries its statistic, degrees of freedom, p-value and — where one exists — the confidence interval, since a p-value alone tells the reader nothing about size.
- `regression.dart` — least squares by Householder QR (never the normal equations, which square the condition number): coefficients, standard errors, t and p per term, R², adjusted R², the residual standard error and the ANOVA of the fit.
- `msa.dart` — `GageRr.crossed`: the measurement-system study by the ANOVA method, which unlike average-and-range can see a part × operator interaction. Pools the interaction when it is not significant, and says so. Reports the components as variance, as %contribution, as %study variation and — given a tolerance — as %tolerance, three numbers that differ and are routinely confused, plus the number of distinct categories.
- `doe.dart` — two-level factorial designs: the full 2^k in Yates order, and the 2^(k−p) fraction with the published minimum-aberration generators (a design the table has no entry for is refused rather than improvised). Computes the defining relation, the resolution and the alias structure, and `FactorialAnalysis` reads the effects back — with Lenth's pseudo-standard error for the unreplicated case, where there is no error term to test against. Phase 9 wires this into `mainEffects` / `interaction` chart types and the DOE design-table generator.
- `sampling.dart` — how many measurements are needed, and what a study of a given size can see: power and sample size for the one- and two-sample t, one and two proportions, and one-way ANOVA, over the noncentral t and F rather than the normal approximation that textbook formulas use. Also the margin-of-error sizing for estimating a mean or a proportion, with the finite-population correction.

### `lib/services/improvement/` — on top of that engine

- `chart_derivation.dart` — the one seam between a stored `ChartSpec` and the statistical chart types (PROCESS_IMPROVEMENT.md Phase 2/8/9): `deriveIndividualsChart`, `deriveHistogram`, `derivePareto`, `deriveBoxPlot`, `deriveProbabilityPlot`, `deriveMainEffects`, `deriveInteraction`, `parseDoeChartGrid`, `generateDoeDesignGrid` and the shared `chartPrimarySample`. Each returns a small view model — values, limits, bins, ranks, hinges, Q-Q points, main-effect lines, interaction panels — and `null` when the data cannot carry the figure, which is what lets both backends say "too little data" instead of drawing a line nobody can defend. **Nothing it computes is ever written back into the spec**: a limit stored beside data that can be replaced is a limit that can end up contradicting it. It exists so the canvas painters and the SVG exporter cannot drift apart — one derivation, two drawings — and so the arithmetic stays testable without a widget tree. A `StatsRefusal` from the engine below becomes a `null` here rather than an exception: refusing is a normal state for a chart, not an error.
- `improvement_analysis_helpers.dart` — pure parsing and thin wrappers for the Phase 8 analysis dialogs: Gage R&R table nesting (including from a chart grid), hypothesis tests and simple linear regression. Returns structured summaries or a `StatsRefusal` string; never decides significance.
- `matrix_spec.dart` — `MatrixSpec` / `MatrixColumn` / bundled templates (SIPOC, FMEA, RACI) and `derivedRpn`. Templates are NL/EN data, not `d()` strings: the column contract on disk is English.
- `matrix_slide.dart` — bridge between a `matrix` slide and the model: header contract, display columns (including derived), RPN-sorted display rows, and template remapping by column key.
- `matrix_layout.dart` — the matrix engine: `buildMatrixScene` turns a spec into a `Scene` once for both backends.
- `canvas_spec.dart` — `CanvasTemplate` / `CanvasRegion` / bundled templates (A3, charter, impact-effort, SWOT, board) and layout kinds (`regions`, `quadrant`, `board`). Templates are NL/EN data, not `d()` strings: the region contract on disk is English.
- `canvas_slide.dart` — bridge between a `canvas` slide and the model: `#` title + `##` region bodies, template id from `ocideck_template`, and remapping by region key when the template changes.
- `canvas_layout.dart` — the canvas engine: `buildCanvasScene` turns a template + regions into a `Scene` once for both backends.
- `tree_spec.dart` — `TreeTemplate` / `TreeLayout` / bundled templates (5× Why, CTQ, Ishikawa). Templates are NL/EN data, not `d()` strings.
- `tree_slide.dart` — bridge between a `tree` slide and golden-thread ids (`**X-01**`, `**Y-01**`), layout token, and starter bullets.
- `tree_layout.dart` — the tree engine: `buildTreeScene` turns nested bullets into a `Scene` once for both backends.
- `flow_spec.dart` — `FlowTemplate` / `FlowLayout` / bundled templates (process map, swimlane, VSM) and step attributes. Templates are NL/EN data, not `d()` strings.
- `flow_slide.dart` — bridge between a `flow` slide and the model: bullet steps with `::` attributes, template id, and layout token.
- `flow_layout.dart` — the flow engine: `buildFlowScene` turns steps into a `Scene` once for both backends.
- `gantt_dsl.dart` — pure-Dart tabel→Mermaid gantt-DSL converter: id-toewijzing, datum/duur/voortgang, milestones, FS-afhankelijkheden, sectiekoppen, auto-as-formaat. De DSL is afgeleid, nooit opgeslagen.
- `improvement_quality_bridge.dart` — golden-thread lint over improvement slides (orphan/undefined X-/Y-ids); feeds the quality panel when the module is on or the deck already carries improvement content (Phase 7).

### `lib/services/scene/` — a laid-out slide as data (design: `docs/design/PROCESS_IMPROVEMENT.md` §7)

- `scene.dart` — `Scene` and its sealed `SceneNode` family (`SceneRect`, `SceneLine`, `ScenePath`, `SceneText`, `SceneImageRef`): explicit positions in a fixed coordinate box, no style tree and no layout pass. The point is that a process-improvement engine (`matrix`, `canvas`; `tree` / `flow` later) does its placement maths **once** and hands the result to two thin backends, instead of writing the same layout twice for screen and export and watching the two drift. `sceneToSvg` is the export backend; `ScenePainter` is the screen one. Pure Dart with no Flutter import, which is what makes an engine's maths testable without a widget tree — hence `TextMeasurer`, the interface an engine measures text through, and `ApproximateTextMeasurer` for tests. `Scene.demo()` keeps the painter reachable for the dead-code gate.

### `lib/services/libreplan/` — LibrePlan REST connector (#1240)

Read-only import of a LibrePlan project snapshot as OciDeck slides. Off by
default, desktop-only, GET-only. Four layers: XML parser (pure Dart), REST
client (NetGuard + pinning), converters (models → slides), and an import
orchestrator that ties them together.

- `libreplan_xml.dart` — Parses LibrePlan REST XML (`order-list`, `resource-list`, `work-report-list`, `resource-worked-hours-list`) into Dart models (`LibreplanOrder`, `LibreplanResource`, `LibreplanWorkReport`, `LibreplanResourceHours`). Pure Dart, no network. Depth-limited (100) and size-capped (10 MiB) against pathological XML. Fail-closed: a parse error returns an empty list, not a crash.
- `libreplan_client.dart` — The REST client: GET-only, Basic Auth, via `NetGuard.safeResolve` (or `safeResolveTrusted` with the internal-server opt-in) + socket pinning + redirect refusal + response cap (10 MiB). Mirrors `ai_client_service.dart` with an injectable transport seam for testing.
- `libreplan_converters.dart` — Eight converters from LibrePlan models to OciDeck slide types: Order → gantt/tree/cockpit/timeline/flow, Resources → table, Work reports → table, Resource hours → chart. Plafonds: gantt 30 tasks, tree 50 nodes, tables 100 rows.
- `libreplan_import.dart` — `LibreplanImporter.import()`: orchestrates fetch + parse + convert. `LibreplanImportOptions` selects which slide types to produce. One failing endpoint produces a warning, not a crash — the rest of the slides are still produced.

## `lib/collab/` — real-time collaboration layer (design: `docs/design/COLLABORATION.md` §5)

The transport-agnostic core of live co-authoring, Phase 0 of COLLABORATION.md
(#975), plus the first networked transport, Phase 0.5 (#989). The core
(`deck_op`, `collab_transport`, `collab_session`) is pure Dart with no network
and no Flutter import, unit-tested through a loopback with no infrastructure. The
async WebDAV transport (§10) is written against a store seam for the same
reason, so it too is tested with no server; its one networked dependency
(`webdav_service`) is isolated in `collab_log_store.dart`. The real-time Matrix
transport (§6) plugs in later; the per-tab provider and the command-palette
actions now drive the layer (#1003), reachable through `TabInfo.collabSession`
(§5.7), `null` on every tab until a session exists. Owner-drop authority handover
(§5.3) is built (#1004): a `HandoverCoordinator` over the async transport flips
the version-assigning role to a stand-in when the owner drops and hands it back
when the owner returns.

- `collab.dart` — the module barrel: one `export` surface for the whole layer
  (model, transports, session, codec, store). The state layer imports it for the
  `CollabSession` seam on `TabInfo`, which keeps the transports and codec
  reachable through the module they belong to rather than each file singly.
- `deck_op.dart` — the typed operation model (§5.1). A `DeckOp` is one
  authoritative change to the in-memory `Deck`, ordered by `version`: the sealed
  ops `InsertSlide`/`RemoveSlide`/`ReorderSlide` and the field-level
  `SetSlideField`/`SetDeckMeta` over the `SlideField`/`DeckMetaField` enums. Ops
  act on the typed model, never on re-parsed Markdown (P5). `applyOp` is pure and
  *fail-closed*: an op for a missing slide, an out-of-range index or a value of
  the wrong type throws rather than silently no-op'ing, because a silent no-op
  would desync the stream (P3). The enums (not the design's raw `String field`)
  make `applyOp`'s switch exhaustive, so the compiler — not a test — guarantees
  every syncable field is handled. `copyWithVersion` lets the authority stamp the
  canonical version onto an intent before rebroadcast.
- `collab_transport.dart` — the `CollabTransport` seam (P6) and `LoopbackHub`/
  `LoopbackTransport`. A dumb pipe: it carries formed events between participants
  and neither orders nor versions them (that is the session's job). `LockEvent`
  carries a `forced` flag for the authority's `ForceUnlock` (§5.4).
- `matrix_client.dart` — the thin, pure-Dart Matrix client-server API client
  (design: `docs/design/SELF_ENCRYPTED_RELAY.md` §7.1, phase P-B): login/register
  (surfacing User-Interactive Auth typed via `MatrixUiaRequired`), `/sync` (reduced
  to the timeline/state/to-device events the relay reads, with `since` resume),
  send timeline/state/to-device events, room create/invite/join, logout. It knows
  nothing of ops, crypto or authority — it is the carriage `MatrixRelayTransport`
  (P-C) will ride on. Egress is an injected `MatrixHttpTransport` (mirroring the AI
  backend's `AiHttpTransport`), so this file opens no socket — the pinned/browser
  transport is built at the wiring seam (P-D) — which keeps it off
  `network_sink_guard_test` and fully testable against an in-Dart fake homeserver.
  It refuses to send its bearer token in cleartext to a non-loopback host and maps
  every non-2xx (incl. a refused 3xx) to a typed `MatrixException`. Pure Dart, so
  it runs under `flutter test` and on web.
- `matrix_http_transport.dart` — the conditional-export façade that picks the
  production `MatrixHttpTransport` for `MatrixClient`: `matrix_http_transport_io.dart`
  on desktop (an SSRF-pinned `dart:io` client — https-only so the token never goes
  cleartext, homeserver host resolved + refused if internal, socket pinned against a
  DNS rebind, redirects blocked, body capped; the only raw `HttpClient` in the
  collab layer, on the network-sink allowlist) and `matrix_http_transport_web.dart`
  on web (a browser-fetch client where the browser sandbox + page CSP are the host
  gate). `createMatrixHttpTransport(MatrixServer)` builds the right one (§11, P-D).
- `matrix_relay_transport.dart` — the realtime `CollabTransport` over a Matrix room
  used as an encrypted relay (design: `docs/design/SELF_ENCRYPTED_RELAY.md` §7,
  phase P-C). Composes `MatrixClient` (carriage, P-B) and `CollabCrypto` (seal/open,
  P-A) behind the same seam `LoopbackTransport`/`WebdavAsyncTransport` implement, so
  `CollabSession` drives it unchanged — only now events travel sealed through a
  homeserver that sees ciphertext (P4). `sendOp`/`setLock` seal and PUT; `syncOnce`
  pulls the next batch, drops own sends (by `sender_device`), opens the rest and
  emits to the op/lock streams. Ordering is Matrix's canonical order (§6.2), so no
  sequence bookkeeping. Fail-closed: a malformed/forged/un-openable event is logged
  and dropped, never applied. `resolvePeer` maps a sender device to its keys
  (backed by room device-state in P-D). Key establishment and presence handover are
  P-D/P-E; this carries the op/lock data plane with keys the session already holds.
  Two optional hooks (`onSystemEvent`, `onToDevice`) let the single sync loop feed
  non-op/lock room state and to-device messages to the key exchange (P-D).
- `collab_device_directory.dart` — the protocol-neutral device directory
  (design: `docs/design/XMPP_COLLAB_TRANSPORT.md` §5 brick 8, §7), extracted from
  `matrix_key_exchange.dart` so the Matrix and future XMPP key exchanges share one
  verified, pinned and capped peer-key store. `CollabDeviceDirectory` ingests
  `DevicePublicKeys` (rejecting any whose identity→agreement binding does not
  verify — §5.3), pins on first use (a known deviceId's identity fingerprint may
  not change — SA-F3, with a clean seam for the signed-rot bump in sub-plak 2),
  caps the pre-approval pin-store at ≥ the occupant cap (500 — NEW-2, so a public
  room's legitimate devices are not denied keying; the scarcity gate belongs on
  keying / the approved-set, not here), and bounds device-ids per peer address
  (SA-F2 — pinning stops overwrite, not creation). The peer address is an opaque
  string the transport supplies (a Matrix user id, an XMPP room/nick).
  `devicesForAddress` resolves the candidate sender devices for a blinded
  key-share (§5.1 N3 — the wrap carries no cleartext sender).
- `matrix_key_exchange.dart` — establishes the session's keys over the room
  (design: `docs/design/SELF_ENCRYPTED_RELAY.md` §4.3, §8, phase P-D), turning the
  relay from "keys pre-shared" into "keys established over the wire". Publishes this
  device's public keys as `nl.ocideck.device` room state; `CollabDeviceDirectory`
  ingests peers' device state (rejecting any whose identity→agreement binding does
  not verify — the relay-key-substitution defence, §5.3) and backs the transport's
  `resolvePeer`; the authority `distributeEpoch`s the epoch key to each member via
  an encrypted to-device key-share (the wrap is recipient-blinded, §5.1 N3 — no
  cleartext `to`/`from` on the wire), and a member installs the one addressed to
  it — the sender is resolved from the to-device event via
  `devicesForAddress`, and `installEpochKey` trial-verifies the signature against
  each candidate. The crypto (wrap/sign/unwrap) is `CollabCrypto`'s; this is only
  the Matrix plumbing. `handleSystemEvent`/`handleToDevice` wire to the transport
  hooks.
- `matrix_snapshot.dart` — delivers the session baseline over Matrix (design:
  `docs/design/SELF_ENCRYPTED_RELAY.md` §6.3, phase P-D). A `CollabSnapshot` is
  sealed once (its AEAD tag covers the whole baseline) and then chunked across
  `nl.ocideck.snapshot.chunk` events, because a full-deck snapshot exceeds the
  ~64 KiB event cap. `MatrixSnapshotChannel.sendSnapshot` (authority) seals + chunks;
  `handleSystemEvent` (wired to the transport's `onSystemEvent`) reassembles by
  chunk id, opens it (signature required — a snapshot is authoritative), and
  completes `firstSnapshot` — what a joiner awaits before starting its session so it
  adopts the authority's slide ids (§5.5). Fail-closed and bounded: a tampered
  (AEAD-caught), missing, malformed or oversized snapshot yields nothing, and the
  pending-chunk buffers are capped against a flooding server. `retryPending`
  re-opens a baseline that reassembled before its epoch key arrived (a joiner sees
  the chunks before its key-share).
- `matrix_session_launch.dart` — assembles a live Matrix session from the bricks
  (design: `docs/design/SELF_ENCRYPTED_RELAY.md` §6.5, phase P-D), the Matrix
  mirror of `collab_session_launch.dart`. `hostMatrixSession` publishes device
  keys, opens epoch 0, pushes the baseline and starts as authority;
  `joinMatrixSession` publishes device keys and returns a `MatrixCollabLaunch`
  whose `session` appears lazily once `syncNow` has received the key-share and
  opened the baseline (the guest then adopts the authority's slide ids, §5.5).
  `syncNow` drives one sync round plus its side effects — retry buffered presence,
  the host keys any newcomer (`MatrixKeyExchange.ensureKeyed`), a guest retries a
  buffered snapshot — handling the ordering hazard that a joiner sees the snapshot
  (and a peer's presence) before its key. `announcePresence`/`presencePeers`/
  `onPresenceChanged` expose the presence plane. `_wire` builds the directory, key
  exchange, snapshot channel, presence and transport with the single sync loop
  feeding them all.
  `MatrixCollabLaunch.sessionReady` completes when the session starts, so a
  provider can wire the deck controller without polling.
- `matrix_collab_launch.dart` — the app-level orchestration a provider calls
  (design: `docs/design/SELF_ENCRYPTED_RELAY.md` §6.5, phase P-D UX). `hostMatrixCollab`
  loads (or generates) this device's keys from `SecretStore`, creates a fresh
  private room, starts the session and returns a `MatrixCollabHost` with the
  `matrix.to` invite link to share; `joinMatrixCollab` parses that link, joins the
  room, and starts the guest session. The room model is "new room per session +
  shareable link" (decided 2026-07-31): the room id in the link is the access
  secret, content is E2EE by our own layer.
- `matrix_invite.dart` — `buildMatrixInvite`/`parseMatrixInvite`: the shareable
  `matrix.to` link a host shares and a guest pastes. Pure string work; the parser
  refuses anything that is not a room id or alias rather than probing an unknown
  link (bearer-like data, §7.1.3).
- `matrix_chat.dart` — `MatrixChat` + `ChatMessage`: the session chat (§6). Messages
  ride room timeline events (a conversation is history, so it accumulates), sealed
  like the deck content **and signed** — every member holds the same epoch key, so
  without a signature one member could forge a message attributed to another, and
  chat is where "who said what" must be trustworthy. Own messages echo locally on
  `send` and are skipped on receive; peers' messages that arrive before their key
  or sender is known are buffered and re-opened in `retryPending` (driven from
  `syncNow`), in arrival order.
- `matrix_presence.dart` — `MatrixPresence` + `PeerPresence`: the presence plane
  (§6, "iedereen ziet iedereen", decided 2026-08-01). `announce(slideId)` seals
  this device's current slide into a room **state** event keyed by device id, so
  its latest position replaces the previous one instead of piling up in the
  timeline; the homeserver sees only ciphertext. `handleSystemEvent` ingests
  peers', buffering any that arrive before their epoch key or sender is known and
  re-opening them in `retryPending` (driven from `syncNow`), the same ordering
  fix the snapshot channel uses. Not authoritative — sealed without a signature.
- `collab_participant.dart` — `CollabParticipant` (a device in a session: user id,
  device id, raw identity key, fingerprint, is-self, and its `TrustState`) and
  `deviceFingerprint`, the readable uppercase-hex-in-groups-of-four rendering of an
  Ed25519 identity key. The binding check already defeats a relay swapping *one*
  key before a peer enters the directory; the fingerprint is the human out-of-band
  compare that also catches a homeserver substituting *both* keys
  (SELF_ENCRYPTED_RELAY.md §4.3). `TrustState` is unverified / verified / mismatch.
- `collab_recovery_key.dart` — `encodeRecoveryKey`/`decodeRecoveryKey` and
  `RecoveryKeyException`: a device identity's two 32-byte seeds framed with a
  version byte and a CRC-16, rendered as grouped Crockford Base32 — the readable
  recovery key that carries the identity to another device (COLLABORATION Phase 2
  "Blok B"). No dependency; the CRC catches a typo before an identity is written.
- `collab_trust_store.dart` — `CollabTrustStore`, the trust-on-first-use pin store
  for device verification (COLLABORATION Phase 2 "Blok A"; SELF_ENCRYPTED_RELAY.md
  §5.3). Per local account (keyed on homeserver + user id in the keychain), it
  remembers which peer identity keys were pinned as verified, so a device compared
  once stays verified across sessions. `evaluate` returns the `TrustState`; a
  pinned device presenting a *different* key is a `mismatch`, the loud case. The
  pinned values are public keys, co-located in the keychain only so the collab
  layer keeps one persistence mechanism.
- `collab_device_store.dart` — persists a device's collaboration key material
  (design: `docs/design/SELF_ENCRYPTED_RELAY.md` §8, phase P-D). A device is
  defined by two random 32-byte seeds (`CollabDeviceSeeds`, from `Random.secure`);
  `CollabDeviceKeys.fromSeeds` rebuilds the identical identity from them, so storing
  the seeds equals storing the keys without exporting private material from
  `CollabCrypto`. `loadOrCreateDeviceKeys` reads the seeds from `SecretStore`
  (keychain) or generates + persists a fresh set; a changed/absent device id (a
  fresh login) regenerates. The seeds are secret and never touch the prefs domain.
  `CollabDeviceSeeds.recoveryKey()`, `readDeviceSeeds` and `importRecoveryKey`
  make the identity portable (Blok B): export the seeds as a recovery key, or
  restore them on another device — bound there to that device's own id, so the
  fingerprint and provenance-signing key carry over (`collab_recovery_key.dart`).
- `collab_crypto.dart` — the end-to-end encryption seam (design:
  `docs/design/SELF_ENCRYPTED_RELAY.md` §4, `XMPP_COLLAB_TRANSPORT.md` §5.1), the
  only file touching `package:cryptography`. A minimal group **key-wrapping**
  scheme, *not* a ratchet: each device has an Ed25519 identity key and an X25519
  agreement key (the latter signed by the former over `(deviceId, agreementKey,
  rot)`, so a relay cannot swap it and a `rot`-replay is unforgeable — §5.1 N2);
  the authority mints one random AEAD session key per **epoch** and wraps it to
  each member with an authenticated ephemeral-ECDH sealed box (the wrap carries
  no cleartext `to`/`from` — recipient-blinded by ECDH trial-decrypt, §5.1 N3);
  record payloads are sealed under the epoch key with XChaCha20-Poly1305, the AAD
  binding room/type/epoch/sender (room strings are structurally disjoint across
  Matrix/XMPP modes — §5.1 BW-1). `seal`/`open` fail closed (bad tag, wrong
  sender, AAD mismatch, missing-but-required signature, unknown epoch);
  `rekey`/`wrapEpochTo`/`installEpochKey` manage epochs (a bump locks out a
  removed member; a pure add reuses the epoch). Pure Dart, so it runs under
  `flutter test` and on web alike.
- `collab_session.dart` — the authority state machine and lock table (§5.3–5.4).
  Exactly one session is the authority (P3); it assigns the monotonic version to
  every op — its own edits and the intents it receives — and rebroadcasts the
  authoritative op, so every replica applying in version order reproduces the
  authority's deck. v1 has a follower edit round-trip (not optimistic under a
  lock). The authority role is now *mutable*: `becomeAuthority`/`stepDown` flip it
  after construction for owner-drop handover (§5.3), and version *resumes* from
  the current one, so a successor that took over at the highest observed version
  continues the sequence without a gap. This stays mechanism-neutral — it knows
  nothing of the beacon or who the owner is; the `HandoverCoordinator` decides
  *when* to flip it. A joiner from a re-baselined snapshot begins at a non-zero
  `initialVersion` (§5.2); `rebaseTo(deck, version)` jumps it *forward* to a later
  snapshot in strand-recovery (§5.2, forward-only so a stale read never rewinds);
  the apply and resume rules are otherwise unchanged.
- `collab_codec.dart` — the JSON wire (de)serialiser for `DeckOp`s and
  `LockEvent`s (§5.2, §5.6). JSON of the typed `Slide`/field model *by design*,
  not a Markdown round-trip: re-parsing would regenerate slide ids (§5.5) and
  re-derive escaping the op model exists to avoid (P5). Every decoder is
  fail-closed (mirrors `applyOp`): unknown discriminator, missing field, wrong
  type or unknown enum name throws rather than yielding a lossy op. The per-field
  value kinds are guarded by a test over `SlideField.values`/`DeckMetaField.values`;
  the full-`Slide` mapping (for `InsertSlide`) by an exhaustive round-trip.
- `collab_deck_diff.dart` — the editor→op seam (§5.1). The editor replaces the
  whole `Deck` on every edit, but the layer syncs field-level ops (P5), so
  `deckDiffToOps(before, after)` returns the ops that reproduce `after` exactly:
  field edits (keyed by slide id), then removes, inserts (carrying the whole new
  slide) and reorders. Exact for the syncable `SlideField`/`DeckMetaField`
  surface, leaving everything outside it alone as the op model does. Also the
  read counterparts `slideFieldValue`/`deckMetaValue` of `applyOp`'s setters. The
  v1 seam; the design's eventual model is the editor emitting ops under a lock
  (§5.4).
- `collab_session_controller.dart` — the bridge between a tab's editable deck
  and a session (§5.7). A local edit is diffed against the session and its ops
  submitted; a change the session applies flows back, *merged* onto the local
  deck so non-syncable fields (table rows, annotations) survive — the merge is
  `applyOps(local, deckDiffToOps(local, session))`. Two guards stop the echo
  loop: an `_applyingRemote` flag suppresses re-submission while writing a remote
  change, and, belt-and-braces, an echoed deck diffs empty. Riverpod- and
  Flutter-free (plain read/write callbacks), so the subtle part is unit-tested
  headlessly; the provider that owns one per tab is the remaining app glue.
- `collab_session_launch.dart` — the session lifecycle seam (§6.5). Both helpers
  return a `CollabLaunch {session, coordinator}` and each session gets a
  `HandoverCoordinator` that owns the poll loop (so `isCaughtUp` is read straight
  after its poll). `hostCollabSession` is *resume-aware*: fresh, it posts the
  baseline, becomes the authority and writes the initial beacon; resuming (a
  baseline already exists — a returning owner) it adopts that baseline, starts as
  a follower, and lets its coordinator reclaim authority once caught up, without
  resetting the baseline (§5.3 hand-back). `joinCollabSession` reads the baseline,
  rebases the local deck onto the authority's slides (adopting their ids, §5.5),
  catches up on ops and polls as a follower — a guest is never the owner. Both a
  joiner and a resuming owner start their session and transport at the *latest*
  baseline's `version` and `seq` (§5.2), so they fetch only the records posted
  since — never the whole log. Store-generic, so it runs over
  `InMemoryCollabLogStore` in tests and `WebdavCollabLogStore` in production —
  nothing here touches a network.
- `handover_coordinator.dart` — the owner-drop handover *policy* over the async
  WebDAV transport (§5.3), one per session (`CollabSession` is the mechanism, this
  decides *when* to flip its role). Each `syncNow` polls the transport then
  re-decides from the beacon — a last-write-wins `{authority, authorityIsOwner,
  tick}` blob beside the log — so every transition is reproducible by counting
  `syncNow`s with no wall-clock. A frozen `tick`, counted in polls, is how a
  successor infers the authority dropped (Phase 0.5 has no presence). Fail-closed:
  a missing, malformed or unreadable beacon, or a failed heartbeat write, never
  takes authority, and a claim is durable (the beacon write confirms) before the
  session acts as authority. A successor takes over only when caught up *and* the
  known maximum sequence has held steady (the WebDAV list-lag guard), after a
  deterministic per-participant backoff so successors do not all claim at once;
  the hand-back to a returning owner needs only "caught up". The beacon is
  *advisory* — persistence is gated on the launch role, never on it. It also
  drives §5.2 **re-baselining**: as the authority, after every `rebaseEveryOps`
  op versions it writes a fresh `CollabSnapshot` at the current version and
  `transport.lastSeq`, so a later joiner starts from it — ops-triggered, no
  wall-clock. It also owns both halves of §5.2 **log compaction**: as the
  authority it deletes (bounded per cycle) the records the *previous* durable
  baseline already subsumed — the delete window widens only after a snapshot write
  confirms, so a failed write can never strand a straggler; as any participant it
  recovers from a compacted gap by re-reading the latest snapshot and jumping the
  session and transport forward (forward-only, fail-closed). WebDAV-specific on
  purpose (it reads `isCaughtUp`/`knownMaxSeq`/`lastSeq`), so the Matrix step
  (§6.2) can replace it without touching `CollabSession`.
- `collab_snapshot.dart` — the session baseline (§5.2, §5.5). `CollabSnapshot`
  is the authority's slides at an op `version`, plus the log `seq` the baseline
  subsumes (so a joiner resumes just above it — §5.2 re-baselining; a missing
  `seq` decodes as `0`). A joiner adopts the slides so it shares the authority's
  slide-id space (`Slide.id` is regenerated on every parse, §5.5, so without this
  every op desyncs). It carries the slides, not the whole `Deck`: the durable
  content lives in the shared `.md` (P2), and changed metadata rides the op
  stream (`SetDeckMeta`). JSON of the model, fail-closed like the op codec.
- `collab_log_store.dart` — the append-only log the async transport reads and
  writes (§10). `CollabLogStore` is a numbered sequence of opaque records whose
  only guarantee is that a taken sequence number cannot be silently overwritten,
  plus two named slots beside the log: the snapshot (`readSnapshot`/`writeSnapshot`,
  §5.2) and the advisory authority beacon (`readBeacon`/`writeBeacon`, §5.3), both
  written unconditionally (last-write-wins, no racing writer to guard).
  `delete(seq)` removes a record for §5.2 compaction (WebDAV binding: a `DELETE`
  on the padded-seq path — only ever a numbered record). `InMemoryCollabLogStore`
  is the loopback-equivalent for tests, and `WebdavCollabLogStore` the production
  binding — one file per record in a sidecar directory, appended with a conditional
  `PUT` (`If-None-Match: *`), the snapshot and beacon sitting under non-numeric
  names (`snapshot.json`, `beacon.json`) so `listSequences` ignores them.
- `webdav_async_transport.dart` — `WebdavAsyncTransport`, the Phase 0.5 async
  transport (§10). Same `CollabTransport` pipe as the loopback, so the session
  drives it unchanged; sends append to the log and a poll delivers others'
  records strictly in sequence order, stopping at a gap (the version-order apply
  in §5.3 needs no out-of-order delivery). A participant never hears its own
  sends. Written against `CollabLogStore` so it is tested with no server. The
  deck `.md` is untouched (P2) — the log is a transient sidecar. `isCaughtUp`
  (the last poll left no record unseen), `knownMaxSeq` and `lastSeq` are the
  extra getters the `HandoverCoordinator` uses for a takeover and to stamp a
  re-baselined snapshot (§5.3, §5.2); a constructor `initialSeq` lets a joiner
  from a re-baselined snapshot start above the records it already subsumes, so it
  never replays the whole log, and `jumpTo(seq)` moves it forward past a compacted
  gap in strand-recovery (§5.2). WebDAV-specific, so they stay off the neutral
  `CollabTransport`.

## `lib/meetings/` — native calls: backend-neutral meeting interface (design: `docs/design/NATIVE_CALLS.md`)

The provider-neutral call contract (§1, §3): one call UI renders from these types, so a Jitsi and a (later) MatrixRTC session are the same experience — the UI never learns which backend is underneath. No dependency yet: pure-Dart contracts with a fake adapter behind them in tests (F1); real adapters (Jitsi over `lib/xmpp/` + `flutter_webrtc`, then MatrixRTC) arrive once the chain-review build-conditions are met.

- `meeting_participant.dart` — the provider-neutral media model the UI renders from: `MeetingParticipant`, a dependency-neutral `MeetingTrack` (an opaque handle, no native media type, so `lib/meetings/` carries no media dependency), `MeetingCapabilities`, `MeetingRole`.
- `meeting_event.dart` — the sealed `MeetingEvent` a session emits (participant joined/left/updated, connection, capabilities, chat, recording, error) plus `MeetingConnectionState`; the call UI is driven entirely by these.
- `meeting_provider.dart` — the `MeetingProvider`/`MeetingSession` interfaces plus `MeetingPreflight` (typed facts, never a bool; E2EE status incl. an explicit `unknown`), `MeetingLinkMatch`, `MeetingJoinRequest` and the support/failure enums. Adapters supply facts (recognition, capabilities, tracks, role), never their own screens.
- `meeting_media_core.dart` — the media-plane seam (F3, `flutter_webrtc`; `assurance/ketenkeuring-flutter-webrtc.md`). `MeetingMediaCore` is what an adapter drives; `mediaE2eeFor(platform)` is the pure, tested E2EE fact (off on iOS/macOS per Bevinding 6, else `unknown` — never a claim OciDeck can't verify). This file opens no network — it only reports facts and confirms the stack loads (`selfTest`); the bounded-egress policy and the connection/track operations arrive with the Jitsi adapter (F3.4b).
- `meeting_media_core_webrtc.dart` — the ONE real `flutter_webrtc` (libwebrtc) binding: `WebrtcMediaCore`.
