Kept deliberately thin (only direct libwebrtc calls, no branching) because it cannot run in a headless test VM — libwebrtc needs a device, so it is verified LIVE, not by a unit test, and is the sole "untestable native binding" entry in `tool/coverage_summary.dart`'s `uncoveredBaseline` (a user-approved new category, distinct from a platform half). Every testable decision lives in `meeting_media_core.dart`.

## `lib/xmpp/` — native calls: the from-scratch XMPP client (design: `docs/design/NATIVE_CALLS.md` §5)

OciDeck's own XMPP-over-WebSocket client (no fork — own code over a dependency), the single spine the Jitsi calls and the collaboration data-plane are meant to share (§5). F2 drives it only to SASL authentication — enough to prove an account works, which is what the module's "test connection" needs; MUC/Jingle/Colibri2 arrive with the Jitsi adapter (F3). Stanza parsing reuses `package:xml` (already a dependency) and is XXE-safe.

- `xmpp_stanza.dart` — the stanza wire model (`Stanza` for iq/message/presence) and the `xmppElement(...)` builder, parsed and serialised with `package:xml`; rejects DTDs, so a stored server frame can never pull an external entity (XXE-safe).
- `xmpp_sasl.dart` — the SASL mechanisms: `saslPlain`/`saslAnonymous` and a `ScramClient` (SCRAM-SHA-1/256, PBKDF2/`Hi` hand-rolled on HMAC), proven against the RFC 5802 §5 vector, with a constant-time server-signature compare.
- `xmpp_session.dart` — the live session state machine (`XmppSession.connect`, F3): open the stream, choose a mechanism (SCRAM > PLAIN only when `NetGuard.maySendReusableSecret` clears it > ANONYMOUS), run SASL, then restart the stream and bind a resource (RFC 6120 §7) — and STAY OPEN, routing inbound stanzas to `stanzas` and sending via `sendStanza` until `close`. Supersedes F2's one-shot `XmppConnection` (a session that closes right after `connect` is the old "test connection"). Returns a typed `XmppSessionResult`/`XmppSessionFailure` — a code, never prose, since this layer carries no l10n and the UI maps the code to a translated message. Refuses a `<see-other-host>` stream redirect (fail closed: it could point past the host check at an internal address). Also defines `XmppStanzaChannel` — the send/receive/bound-JID surface the layers above depend on, so they test against a fake channel; `XmppSession` implements it. With a `reconnectTransportFactory`, a stream drop on a live session triggers a supervised reconnect (§4): bounded exponential backoff (`xmppReconnectDelay`, capped at 30s), re-authenticate + re-bind on a fresh transport to the same host (never a new outbound host; `<see-other-host>` during reconnect is refused too), then `onRejoin` re-enters the MUC room(s) and `onReconnected` signals the transport layer to resync. The `stanzas` stream survives a reconnect — a consumer keeps its subscription across a drop→reconnect cycle. After `maxReconnectAttempts` (default 5) the session goes fail-closed (`stanzas` completes).
- `xmpp_muc.dart` — XEP-0045 Multi-User Chat over an `XmppStanzaChannel` (`XmppMuc.join`/`leave`, F3, `NATIVE_CALLS.md` §5.1): join by sending a presence, confirm on the self-presence (status code 110), and track occupants from presence (available adds/updates, `unavailable` removes) as a live `occupants` roster (`MucOccupant`: affiliation/role/realJid). XEP-0045 error presences map to a typed `MucJoinFailure`. The primitive both the Jitsi conference room and OciDeck's companion room (§5.1) ride — presence/roster only, no media or OciDeck control payload. `join(presenceExtensions:)` (sub-plak 5, §5 brick 10) lets the join presence carry extra children — the `<x xmlns="nl.ocideck.device">` device-key extension — so a newcomer publishes its keys at entry without a separate stanza.
- `companion_room.dart` — the deterministic companion-room derivation (`companionRoomLocalpart`/`companionRoomJid`, F3, `NATIVE_CALLS.md` §5.1): a one-way SHA-256 hash (behind a versioned salt) of the normalized conference URL into `ocideck-<hash>`, so two OciDeck clients holding the same Jitsi link compute the same companion MUC without a central registry (P1), and no one lacking the link can recover the conference from the room name. Normalization — lowercase host, drop the ephemeral `#config`/query, strip trailing slashes — makes the two clients agree.
- `companion_demux.dart` — the companion-MUC fan-out (`CompanionDemux`, `XMPP_COLLAB_TRANSPORT.md` §3, §5 brick 7): owns the one `channel.stanzas` subscription and routes each inbound groupchat stanza to the brick that registered for its `nl.ocideck.*` namespace — the transport (ops/locks/resync), the key-exchange, the snapshot-channel, presence, chat. Without this single owner, each brick would open its own subscription and compete over the same stream. Fail-closed: a stanza with a namespace nobody registered for is dropped (logged), never passed through — an unknown payload type is input, not an instruction.
- `xmpp_transport.dart` — the XMPP collab data-plane (`XmppTransport implements CollabTransport`, `XMPP_COLLAB_TRANSPORT.md` §3–5, §4 sub-plak 4): carries ops/locks sealed over the companion MUC on the same `CollabTransport` seam `collab_session.dart` already uses for Loopback and Matrix. What it adds over the Matrix relay (§4): a gap→resync path. Matrix's `/sync` delivers gap-free and resumable; a MUC does not — a briefly offline member *misses* messages with no cursor to redeliver them, and the follower rule in `collab_session.dart` only accepts at `version + 1`, so a gap freezes the deck. This transport detects a gap (an op with `version > expected+1`, or a reconnect signal) and emits a sealed `<resync>` request; the authority coalesces concurrent requests into one re-baseline (§4 N1: an unbounded request would force a deck-wide broadcast per occupant, amplified K× under simultaneous reconnects). Keys pre-shared in this sub-plak — the key-exchange (sub-plak 5) builds the admission-gated distribution; the crypto arrives already cooled. Sealing uses the existing `CollabCrypto.seal/open` (mode isolation is already in the room-AAD: `ocideck-<hash>@conference.<domain>` vs. Matrix `!local:server` are structurally disjunct). Serial send chain, own-echo suppression (`senderDevice == participantId`), deferred backlog for stanzas that arrive before key state, rate-limited resync emission, and coalesced resync handling mirror `MatrixRelayTransport`. `resyncApprovalGate` (sub-plak 5, NEW-1 §7) is the authority-side admission check: a departed member still holds an old epoch key and could seal a resync under it, so the gate tests *current-approved-set membership*, not decryptability.
- `xmpp_key_exchange.dart` — the XMPP key establishment (`XmppKeyExchange`, `XMPP_COLLAB_TRANSPORT.md` §5 brick 2 + Admission gate, §5.1, §7, sub-plak 5): the XMPP counterpart of `MatrixKeyExchange`, with three differences a MUC does not give for free. (1) Device keys ride as a **signed-rot presence extension** `<x xmlns="nl.ocideck.device">` — not power-gated room state; presence is unordered and ungated, so the §5.1 signed rotation epoch + pin-on-first-use + rot-monotonicity pay for what Matrix's ordered state gave. `handleDevicePresence` ingests with rot-monotonicity (a replay with same/lower rot is refused before the directory's same-identity update would accept it — NEW-5), and the directory's `ingest` refuses an identity swap (SA-F3) and an unverifiable binding (§5.3). (2) Keying is **deny-by-default admission-gated** — not the fail-open Matrix mirror that keys every known device unconditionally; `ensureKeyed` keys only devices in an explicit approval-set (`approve`, the existing TOFU/fingerprint flow), because the room is derivable from the (often public) Jitsi URL and keying-on-presence would hand the epoch key to anyone with the link (SA-F4). (3) The epoch key rides as a **recipient-blinded broadcast** `<keyshare>` — not a directed to-device; the wrap carries no cleartext `to`/`from` (§5.1 N3), each occupant trial-opens the one meant for it, and `handleKeyshare` resolves the sender from the stanza's `from` (room@conf/nick) through the directory. `devicePresenceExtension` builds the `<x>` child for `XmppMuc.join(presenceExtensions:)` so the join presence carries device keys at entry (§6). `keyedDeviceCount` exposes how many devices this epoch already received a keyshare, so the launch (sub-plak 6) can detect a newcomer just keyed and re-send the baseline (§6: a MUC gives no `/sync`-achtergrond). Crypto is `CollabCrypto` (+§5.1); fail-closed throughout.
- `xmpp_snapshot.dart` — the session baseline over the companion MUC (`XmppSnapshotChannel`, `XMPP_COLLAB_TRANSPORT.md` §5 brick 3, §4, §6, sub-plak 6): the XMPP counterpart of `MatrixSnapshotChannel`. A joiner needs the authority's `CollabSnapshot` — the slide list at a version — to share the slide-id space before any op (§5.5). An XMPP stanza is bounded (frame ≤ 512 KiB, §7), so the baseline is **sealed once, chunked after**: the whole snapshot is sealed as one `SealedEnvelope` (one AEAD tag over the whole baseline — a dropped or reordered chunk makes the reassembled blob unopenable, fail-closed), and only the ciphertext blob is split across `<snap>` stanzas. Each chunk-stanza carries plaintext routing-metadata (`id`, index, count) as JSON-text and one slice of the blob; the content itself stays ciphertext. Reassembly is per chunk-id, opened and signature-verified against the directory-held authority keys (a snapshot is authoritative, so the signature is required — P3 anti-impersonation). Two outlets: `firstSnapshot`/`hasSnapshot` for the join (a joiner awaits it before its session starts; a baseline that arrives before its epoch key is buffered and `retryPending` opens it once a key is installed — a joiner sees the chunks before its keyshare, §6), and `rebaselines` for the §4 resync re-baseline (after the first, each opened snapshot emits; the launch listens and calls `CollabSession.rebaseTo`, forward-only). Wire to the demux for `nl.ocideck.snapshot`; the authority calls `sendSnapshot`. Fail-closed and bounded: a malformed, incomplete, unopenable or too-large snapshot is logged and dropped, and the pending-chunk buffers are capped so a hostile server cannot exhaust memory by flooding chunks.
- `xmpp_presence_beacon.dart` — the slide-position beacon over the companion MUC (`XmppPresenceBeacon`, `XMPP_COLLAB_TRANSPORT.md` §5 brick 4, §3, sub-plak 7): the XMPP counterpart of `MatrixPresence`. Which slide each co-author is viewing rides **sealed** in a `<pos xmlns="nl.ocideck.presence">` on a `<message type=groupchat>` (§3) — the MUC sees only ciphertext, never *where* someone is. Presence is a current fact, not history, so the **latest per sender wins** (a second `<pos>` from the same device replaces, not accumulates) — exactly what Matrix's room-state did via the state-key, but without the power-gated ordering a homeserver gave for free (§2); the seal + the directory's pin-on-first-use pay for that. Like an op, presence is not authoritative (a wrong position misplaces a dot, nothing more), so it is sealed **without** a signature and opened without requiring one; a device trusts only a presence whose sender is a verified peer in the directory, and anything else is dropped fail-closed. A presence that arrives before its epoch key or its sender's device keys is buffered and `retryPending` re-opens it — a joiner sees a peer's presence on the same stream as (or before) its keyshare (§6). Own echo (the MUC reflects the sender's own message back) is suppressed on `senderDevice == deviceId`.
- `xmpp_chat.dart` — the session chat over the companion MUC (`XmppChat`, `XMPP_COLLAB_TRANSPORT.md` §5 brick 5, §3, §4, §8, sub-plak 7): the XMPP counterpart of `MatrixChat`, with one critical difference from §4. Messages ride **sealed + signed** in a `<chat xmlns="nl.ocideck.chat">` on a `<message type=groupchat>` (§3); a conversation is history, so it accumulates (unlike presence). **Signed**, like Matrix: every session member holds the same epoch key, so an unsigned message would let one member forge one attributed to another — and chat is exactly where "who said what" must be trustworthy — so a message is sealed with a signature and opened requiring one; a message that fails the check is dropped fail-closed. **Sealed-id dedup (§4)** — the difference: a MUC has no `/sync` cursor, so MAM/resync re-deliver a message, and chat (like `matrix_chat.dart`) has no own dedup, so each `<chat>` carries an **id = hash of its sealed bytes** (`sealed.toContent()`, which include a fresh per-seal nonce — a forced collision is infeasible and a replay is correctly idempotent; a *plaintext* hash would wrongly suppress identical text). The dedup set is **bounded** (FIFO, like the message list). The dedup decision falls **after** a successful open + signature verification: a forger without the signing key produces no valid message, so it cannot pollute the dedup set to suppress real messages; a replay of a valid message opens (valid signature), finds its id in the set, and is dropped. Own messages are echoed locally on `send` and skipped on receive. `ownUserId` (the room/nick) attributes the local echo.
- `xmpp_collab_launch.dart` — assembling a live XMPP collaboration session from the bricks (`XmppCollabLaunch`, `hostXmppSession`/`joinXmppSession`, `XMPP_COLLAB_TRANSPORT.md` §5–6, §8, sub-plak 6 + 7): the XMPP counterpart of `matrix_session_launch.dart`. The crypto, stanza channel + demux, data-plane, key exchange, snapshot channel, presence beacon and chat each stand alone; this is the thin seam that wires them into "host"/"join" so a caller does not have to know their internal order. Where it diverges from the Matrix launch: **push, not poll** — Matrix's `syncOnce()` polls `/sync`; XMPP is push (the demux routes stanzas as they arrive), so `syncNow` does not poll the transport but drives the reactive side-effects: the host keys any newcomer it has learned (`ensureKeyed`) and re-sends the baseline to a just-keyed newcomer (§6: a MUC gives no `/sync`-achtergrond, so the snapshot comes after the keyshare); the guest retries a buffered snapshot and, once one opens, starts its session on the authority's slide-id space (§5.5) and listens on `rebaselines` for §4 re-baselines. Presence and chat retry their buffered envelopes each round too — a joiner sees them before its keyshare (§6). **NEW-6: two recovery paths** — a MUC has no resumable cursor, so a drop is a gap; which gap depends on when the drop happened. *Before keying*: the guest holds no epoch key, so the transport's gap→resync emission fails silently (`seal` throws `no-epoch`), and the host's next `ensureKeyed` re-keys him on rejoin — no resync, no re-baseline. *After keying*: the guest holds an epoch key, so the transport emits a sealed `<resync>`; the authority's `onResyncRequested` re-baselines; the guest's `rebaselines` listener re-bases (`rebaseTo`, forward-only). The admission gate (`resyncApprovalGate`, NEW-1 §7) ensures a departed member who still holds an old epoch key cannot force a deck-wide broadcast — the gate tests current-approved-set membership, not decryptability. `hostXmppSession`/`joinXmppSession` take an `ownUserId` (the room/nick) threaded to `XmppChat` for the local echo; the launch exposes `announcePresence`/`presencePeers`/`onPresenceChanged` and `sendChat`/`chatMessages`/`onChatChanged` convenience methods mirroring the Matrix launch.
- `xmpp_frame_transport.dart` — the injectable frame seam (`XmppFrameTransport`: inbound frames, `send`, `close`) so the state machine is unit-tested against an in-memory fake; a real socket lives only behind the platform half. `XmppConnectException` is the single failure type it throws.
- `xmpp_frame_transport_platform.dart` — the conditional-import selector choosing the io half on desktop and the web half in the browser. `xmpp_frame_transport_io.dart` opens the one guarded `wss` WebSocket (raw `WebSocket.connect` so the egress-scan sees it; NetGuard-resolved, socket pinned to the validated address against DNS rebind, fail-closed on any scheme downgrade); `xmpp_frame_transport_web.dart` fails closed — unsupported, because the browser gives no socket pinning.

## `lib/state/` — Riverpod providers

- `collab_session_provider.dart` — the per-tab owner of a collaboration session (COLLABORATION.md §5.7), the app glue over `lib/collab/`. `CollabSessionNotifier.host`/`join` build a `WebdavCollabLogStore` from the tab's `webdavOrigin`, host or join a session, keep it on `TabInfo.collabSession`, own its `HandoverCoordinator`, and wire a `CollabSessionController` between the session and `deckProvider` (local edits out as ops, remote changes back via `DeckNotifier.applyCollabDeck`). It listens on the coordinator's `authorityChanged`: it re-drives any local edit the old authority never versioned to the new one, and refreshes `CollabSessionState.isTemporaryAuthority` (true when this *guest* is standing in as the authority after an owner drop, §5.3). `canPersist` gates saving — only the owner (host) may write the shared `.md`, so a guest, even one temporarily holding authority, cannot; the save path in `app_shell_main_layout.dart` reads it and the shell surfaces the transitions. Overridden per tab in `app_shell.dart`'s `_tabScope`, like `deckProvider`; the root is idle. `collabSidecarOpsDir` derives the sidecar folder beside the deck. `hostMatrix`/`joinMatrix` are the realtime counterpart (SELF_ENCRYPTED_RELAY.md §6.5): they need no WebDAV source, only the app-global Matrix account (`matrixAccountProvider`), the client (`matrixClientProvider`) and the keychain (`secretStoreProvider`); a host goes active at once and exposes `CollabSessionState.inviteLink` to share, a guest joins that link and goes active once the baseline arrives over the sync loop, and both reuse `_attachController` (no handover — that is P-E).
- `consent_provider.dart` — `ConsentNotifier` managing consent acceptance/revocation with persistent storage.
- `deck_provider.dart` — `DeckNotifier`: loaded deck, dirty state, undo/redo history, file path. Also `refreshEditorFields`, which bumps `DeckState.revision` without touching the deck: the editor's text fields cache their content in their own controllers and only re-read when that revision changes, so a change arriving from outside them (a table cell edited live while presenting) would otherwise sit behind stale text that the next keystroke writes back. `onChartDataWarnings` is the save-side counterpart of `onSweepWebAssets`: this notifier has no `Ref`, so it hands the chart-data complaints of a save up to `TabsNotifier`, which owns one.
- `document_provider.dart` — `DocumentState`/`DocumentNotifier`: the document counterpart of `DeckNotifier` — the loaded `MarkdownDocument`, dirty state, file path, and whole-source undo/redo with the same coalescing window as the deck (so typing collapses into one undo step). A document is the source itself, so there is no deconstruction to slides (docs/design/DOCUMENT_MODE.md §2). `markDirty` — the counterpart of `DeckNotifier.markDirty` — marks the document unsaved; crash recovery uses it so a restored document is not mistaken for already-saved. `requestFind` is the per-tab signal from the app shell to the document editor, so find/replace does not depend on focus already being inside the writing surface.
- `deck_provider_ai.dart` — `DeckNotifierAiAlt` extension: count/clear AI-generated image alt-texts.
- `deck_provider_auto.dart` — `DeckNotifierAuto` extension: `autoRenumberFindings` (P2-AUTO).
- `deck_provider_checklist.dart` — `DeckNotifierChecklist` extension: `generateScopeChecklists` (one checklist per scope object, feedback #8) and `clearAllChecklists`.
- `deck_provider_markdown.dart` — `DeckNotifierMarkdown` extension: generate/apply markdown for the whole deck or a single slide (per-slide markdown view).
- `deck_provider_miauw.dart` — `DeckNotifierMiauw` extension: set/withdraw MIAUW compliance waivers and confirmations. Each mutation stamps the decision with the current UTC time and a withdrawal leaves a tombstone (#756) — the git merge lets the latest decision win per EIS id, so the moment of the decision is part of the decision.
- `deck_provider_managementsysteem.dart` — the Managementsysteem module's top-level deck actions (in `deck_provider`'s library rather than an extension, so they keep private access to `_mutate` without counting toward the notifier's class-size ratchet). `generateManagementSystemOverview` rolls every `controlStatus` slide up into a `table` slide "Voortgang managementsysteem" (section · applicable · implemented · % implemented + a totals row). `generateManagementSystemChart` puts the burn-up chart (from `management_system_artefacts.dart`) down as a horizontal-stacked-bar `chart` slide; both are idempotent (refreshed in place — the overview on its slide title, the chart on its own spec title) and derived from the deck, so they never drift from the detail slides. `generateManagementReview` appends the ISO 9.3 management-review template as two `freeMarkdown` slides (input 9.3.2 + output 9.3.3), pre-filled with the current progress figures; an invisible marker (`kManagementReviewMarker`) on the first slide guards against a second copy on a re-run, so it protects the author's answers rather than overwriting them.
- `image_privacy_provider.dart` — De beeldbevindingen als kwaliteitsmeldingen. Eigen asynchrone provider naast de synchrone tekstscan, want dit is de duurste controle: decoderen plus een neuraal netwerk per afbeelding. Scheidt "nul gezichten" van "niet kunnen lezen" — HEIC valt in die tweede categorie, en iPhone-foto's zijn standaard HEIC.
- `deck_quality_provider.dart` — Computes accessibility/quality analysis for the loaded deck. `deckQualityRawProvider` keeps every finding (the badges and the popover need the accepted ones); `deckQualityProvider` drops the ones on an accepted slide (the panel and the export gate want the open work) and then folds the length-driven density warnings that repeat across a split run into one per kind through `collapseSplitRunDensity` (#1289), so the panel does not multiply them after **Split slide** — the raw provider stays uncollapsed, because the badges, the popover and the auto-fix engine each need the per-page truth. Both run through named top-level functions so the per-tab override in `AppShell` cannot drift from the original.
- `editor_provider.dart` — `EditorState`/`EditorNotifier`: selected slide, editor mode, Markdown buffer and its applied baseline; `hasMarkdownDraft` makes unapplied source participate in dirty-state and recovery handling.
- `tabs_provider_git_native.dart` — `TabsNotifierGitNative` extension: the native-git plane through the notifier — `openDeckFromGitNative` (read from the clone, tab `baseSha` = clone HEAD), `saveToGitNative` (resolve the round's work branch like the REST path → `commitDeck(workBranch, forkFrom)`; on a rejected push `_mergeNative` runs `mergeRemote` with a resolver that puts all three versions through the import gate and merges them with `mergeDeckVersions`), `syncGitNative`. Chosen over the REST path by `nativeGitMirrorProvider`.
- `tabs_provider_git.dart` — `TabsNotifierGit` extension: `openDeckFromGit` — fetch a deck from a repo, through the shared import gate, into a tab carrying a `GitOrigin`; `readVersionDeck` — read a deck at a release tag through the same gate without placing it (the read side under opening *and* under comparing two versions); `openVersionFromGit` — the same, placed **read-only** (a labelled snapshot, no `GitOrigin`, so the save path can never target it); `saveToGit` — the inverse, but landing on a dated **work branch** (`decks/<naam>/<datum>`, D3) rather than the default branch: the first save of a round creates it (lazily, off the default), later saves stay on it, and offline it queues with a `forkFrom` so the flush can create it. `openForReview` — evaluate the classification gate on `deckReleaseTlp` (max effective TLP, fail-closed, before any push) then `openPullRequest(work → default)`; `mergeConcept` — find the work branch's PR (`pullRequestForBranch`), merge it (optionally pruning), and re-base the tab onto the default branch; `tagRelease` — the same gate, then `createTag(releaseTag(deckName, version), target: default HEAD)`. `flushGit` drains the queue and re-bases any open tab whose deck landed. The "Concept mergen…"/"Versie vastleggen…" menu items live in `app_shell_menu.dart`; their dialogs, and the version-compare dialogs, in `shell_actions_git_dialogs.dart`.
- `git_provider.dart` — the forge-plane providers, every one of them a family keyed on connection id: `gitForgeProvider` (build the adapter from config + keychain token), `nativeGitMirrorProvider`, `draftMirrorProvider` / `outboxProvider` / `syncEngineProvider` (the offline queue), `gitDeckListProvider` (the browser's deck list), and `gitDeckTagsProvider` (a deck's release tags, newest first — the "Versies…" picker). Keyed on the id rather than the config so a corrected typo in the server URL does not detach an open deck from its source; `gitConnectionsProvider` and `primaryGitConnectionProvider` answer "which repos are there" and "which is the default". The "Versies…" menu lives in `app_shell_menu.dart`; `_GitVersionsDialog`, `_VersionComparePicker` and `_VersionDiffDialog` in `shell_actions_git_dialogs.dart`.
- `image_contrast_provider.dart` — Computes title-slide image-contrast issues asynchronously per deck.
- `theme_logo_provider.dart` — Deckbrede melding wanneer het logo van het actieve stijlprofiel niet gevonden wordt (`asset:`-sleutel niet in de bundel, verdwenen bestand, of een lege `mem:`-store na een herlaad). Asynchroon, net als `image_contrast_provider`, omdat het opzoeken van een `asset:`-sleutel de asset-manifest vraagt; gemerged in het kwaliteitspaneel en de export-gate (#1298).
- `info_safety_provider.dart` — The security-module enable/reveal state that gates the pentest features.
- `module_registry.dart` — the deliberately light module registry (#570): one enumeration of the optional modules (`ModuleId`) with each module's enabled/reveal gate as a concrete `Provider<bool>`. Each module keeps its own state and preference key — parameterising three working-but-different patterns would break them to unify them — but which modules exist, in what card order, and what their gates are lives in exactly one place, guarded by a test against the Extensions tab. Carries the shared contract: reveal when the content is already there. Includes `ModuleId.procesverbetering` (PROCESS_IMPROVEMENT.md Phase 0), `ModuleId.collaboration` (realtime samenwerken, SELF_ENCRYPTED_RELAY.md §6), `ModuleId.videoCalls` (native calls, NATIVE_CALLS.md §7) and `ModuleId.managementsysteem` (ISO-voortgang, ISO_MANAGEMENTSYSTEEM.md §5).
- `asset_rights_module_provider.dart` — Persistent, default-off gate for the Afbeeldingsrechten extension. Switching it off hides the menu and stops automatic scans without deleting repository assessment records.
- `procesverbetering_provider.dart` — the *Procesverbetering* module state (PROCESS_IMPROVEMENT.md Phase 0): optional authoring support for methods such as DMAIC, Kaizen and A3, off by default. Preference key `procesverbeteringModuleEnabled`. Reveal = enabled **or** `Deck.hasImprovementSlides` (a `matrix` or `canvas` slide already present). Discovery banner mirrors Informatieveiligheid (`improvementModulePromptProvider` in `tabs_provider.dart`).
- `collaboration_provider.dart` — the *Realtime samenwerken* module state (SELF_ENCRYPTED_RELAY.md §6), off by default. Two levels: the module master (`collaborationEnabledProvider`, key `collaborationModuleEnabled`) and a per-transport toggle — today Matrix (`matrixCollabEnabledProvider`, key `matrixCollabEnabled`, default on), with Jitsi/XMPP to follow. `matrixCollabActiveProvider` (module **and** Matrix on) gates the host/join actions; `collaborationRevealProvider` (active **or** an account already configured) gates the *Samenwerken* tab, so an existing account is never stranded after an upgrade.
- `meeting_session_provider.dart` — the optional *Videovergaderingen* module (default-off toggle, key `videoCallsModuleEnabled`) **and** the root-scoped active `MeetingSession` (`meetingSessionProvider`), held app-global so a call survives a deck-tab switch (`NATIVE_CALLS.md` §9, unlike the per-tab `collabSessionProvider`). `videoCallsRevealProvider` = enabled **or** a call is live. No in-app join flow yet (F3); the panel it feeds renders a fake session under test.
- `managementsysteem_provider.dart` — the *Managementsysteem* module state (ISO_MANAGEMENTSYSTEEM.md §5), off by default. Preference key `managementsysteemModuleEnabled`; `managementsysteemEnabledProvider` is the switch, `managementsysteemRevealProvider` the "module on" half that callers OR with `Deck.hasManagementSystemSlides` so a deck with a `controlStatus` slide reveals the type regardless. Simpler than the Procesverbetering notifier: the ISO index is bundled `const` data, so enabling only persists the preference — there is no catalog to warm.
- `improvement_provider.dart` — golden-thread quality issues from `improvement_quality_bridge.dart`, merged into the quality panel when the module is on or the deck carries improvement slides (Phase 7).
- `improvement_ai_provider.dart` — `improvementAiAvailableProvider`: AI assist on improvement slides requires both AI and Procesverbetering modules plus a configured backend (Phase 10).
- `import_module_provider.dart` — the *Importeren* module state (#772, decision B1): pulling material in from other systems as an extension, off by default. *(Updated 2026-08-03, #1158: since OpenKAT became its own integration, this module covers only presentation import — .pptx/.key/.odp — and `importerContentProviders` is empty.)* One module for every source, because "I bring something in from outside" is one thought to the user, not a list of products. **The extension point is `importerContentProviders`**: a new importer adds its own "do I already have content" provider there and inherits this project's fixed rule — reveal once the content is there, and switching off never strands existing work. Deliberately a list rather than an ever-longer `||`, so what counts as content lives in one place and no importer can silently fall outside it; today it is empty because the presentation import leaves nothing persistent behind. Unlike Online opslag the default is a hard off rather than a derived one: that module's content (connections) predated the module, this one's does not.
- `openkat_provider.dart` — the OpenKAT integration's state (#767, #772, #1158, live Rocky 2026-08-05): whether the connection is on (`enabled`, preference key `openkatIntegrationEnabled`, default off), the folder holding exports (`openkatReportDirectory`), and the list of connected Rocky installations (`openkatInstallations` — metadata only; Knox tokens per installation id in `SecretStore`). *(Updated 2026-08-03, #1158: OpenKAT gained its own switch on the Integraties tab.)* Providers: `openKatIntegrationEnabledProvider`, `openKatAvailableProvider` (always true — web shows a disabled card), `openKatDesktopCapableProvider` (folder/keychain paths), `openKatHasContentProvider` (folder *or* ≥1 installation), `openKatInstallationsProvider` and `openKatIntegrationRevealProvider` (on, *or* content).
- `integration_registry.dart` — the register of integrations (#1158): connections to other systems, each switchable on its own, with a control to flip them all at once. `IntegrationId` (today only `openKat`), `IntegrationEntry` (its availability, enabled and reveal providers plus a `setEnabled` bridge), and `integrationRegistry`. Derived providers `availableIntegrationsProvider` (what this platform can show — OpenKAT is listed on web too, disabled in the body), `anyIntegrationAvailableProvider` (whether the Integraties tab should exist at all), `allIntegrationsEnabledProvider` and `anyIntegrationEnabledProvider` (what the "alles aan/uit" control reads). Deliberately a sibling of `module_registry.dart` and not one shared framework: a module is functionality OciDeck brings itself, an integration is an outward connection with its own settings.
- `openkat_wizard_controller.dart` — `OpenKatWizardController`: the UI state machine for source check, scenario, needed inputs and review/build. It selects sensible source-derived defaults, has no scanner/engine calculation of its own, and calls the injectable wizard gateway.
- `online_storage_provider.dart` — the *Online opslag* module state (#570): WebDAV, S3 and git as an extension, off by default. One preference key, written only when the user flips the switch; without a stored choice the state *follows the content* (`onlineStorageEnabledProvider`), so an install that already has an online connection starts with the module on — a derived default instead of a one-time migration write, because prefs and the connection list load asynchronously and a migration reading too early would record "no connections" as "deliberately off". `onlineStorageRevealProvider` (enabled *or* has remote connections) is the single gate the storage surfaces check — deliberately only the add-connection kinds: menus, dialogs and the outbox all key on *existing* connections, which the reveal rule keeps intact, so switching the module off never strands configured or queued work.
- `local_cve_provider.dart` — `LocalCveNotifier`/`LocalCveState`: the local CVE database's status, build progress and cancellation, plus `localCveAvailableProvider` — which the CVE picker uses to search offline (and then deliberately *not* fall back online).
- `libreplan_provider.dart` — Riverpod providers for the LibrePlan-connector: `libreplanSettingsProvider` (reads from `SettingsProvider`), `libreplanRevealProvider` (module reveal state).
- `provider_warmup.dart` — `warmTabDerivedProviders`: keeps the tab's derived chain subscribed for as long as the tab lives, so a deck change schedules its refresh *before* the frame. Without it an unread chain goes dirty unnoticed and the first widget to read it flushes mid-build, which Flutter answers with "setState() called during build". Guarded by `provider_warmup_test.dart`.
- `save_progress_provider.dart` — `SaveTarget` (`local`, `webdav`, `s3`, `git`), `saveProgressProvider` and the `withSaveProgress` wrapper that clears the flag in a `finally` — a flag left standing after a failed save reports work that stranded long ago. App-wide rather than per tab: there is one user with one pair of eyes, and a second save running at the same time is exactly what you want to see.
- `privacy_provider.dart` — Runs the privacy scan for the active deck (per-tab scoped) and surfaces it everywhere the deck's quality is shown. The raw scan (`privacyRawScanProvider`) feeds two views: the panel/thumbnail issues (`privacyScanProvider` → `privacyQualityIssuesProvider`, which suppress already-handled slides) and the export gate's count (`privacyExportSummaryProvider`, which must *not* suppress them — a gate has to know how much was handled).
- `parts/settings_provider_connections.dart` — The file connections: add/update/remove/reorder, and the one-time migration out of the three older prefs keys (`libraries`, `webdavServer`, `gitRepo`). Those keys are left alone until the first change, so falling back to an older build costs nothing.
- `parts/settings_provider_privacy.dart` — The privacy switches (master, per-rule, own identity, export gate).
- `settings_provider.dart` — `SettingsNotifier`: app settings,
  theme/appearance profiles, cockpit schemes and the global authentic/classic
  cockpit look. `setDocumentDefaultStyle`/`setDocumentStyleEnforced`/`setDocumentChapterPageBreak`
  persist the document-mode style default, the house-style toggle and the
  chapter-page-break toggle (prefs keys
  `documentDefaultStyle`/`documentStyleEnforced`/`documentChapterPageBreak`); the
  three appliers live in `parts/settings_provider_document_style.dart`
  (DOCUMENT_MODE.md §13.5).
- `slide_clipboard_provider.dart` — Global slide clipboard for copy/paste across tabs.
- `tabs_provider_package.dart` — `_TabsPackageAssets` extension: the unpack path of an `.ocideck` opened in memory (web, or an import without a project folder). Images go to the `WebAssetStore` and slide paths are rewritten to `mem:`; chart data is inlined into the spec (it is text belonging in the spec, and on web there is no project folder for a separate file to sit in); the sidecars are re-attached as layers. All three refuse a reference that points outside the package root with `../`.
- `tabs_provider_recovery.dart` — `TabsNotifier`'s autosave/recovery helpers, kept top-level so they touch no field (only public getters) and stay off the notifier's class-size ratchet: `_deckRecoverySnapshot` builds a presentation tab's recovery snapshot, `_documentRecoverySnapshot` builds a document tab's (the raw `.md`, byte-faithful and including the style front-matter block, with no deck sidecars, tagged `kind: document` so recovery restores a document tab), and `_markdownDraftFor` is the unapplied per-slide source a deck tab may carry (a document has none — it edits its source live).
- `tabs_provider.dart` — `TabInfo` and the tabs notifier: open editor tabs, recovery, WebDAV origin. Also hosts the one-shot open-time signals the shell listens on, including `securityModulePromptProvider` — set once per open when a deck carries Informatieveiligheid slide types, driving the "enable the module" discovery banner. The signal carries only the tab id; which slide the banner points at is read from the live deck on every click, because slides can be deleted or moved while it is up. The shell takes the banner away as soon as its claim stops holding — another tab in view, the deck closed, or the last security slide deleted. The autosave tick writes the ink layer into the snapshot alongside the user notes, and `restoreRecovered` decodes it back; an unreadable ink payload is logged and skipped rather than allowed to block the recovery of the text. The same tick now also snapshots a dirty **document** tab (`_autosaveDocument`, with the same per-tab de-duplication as a deck — a byte-faithful source, no deck sidecars), and `restoreRecovered` puts a document snapshot back as a document tab, byte-faithful and marked unsaved, reusing `_createDocumentTab` so the recovered snapshot's key is kept — so a crash no longer discards an unsaved document. `chartDataWarningProvider` is the same one-shot shape, now carrying a `whileSaving` flag: reading and writing need different words, because a failed read leaves a chart empty while a failed write leaves the numbers nowhere but the open window.
- `webdav_provider.dart` — Providers for `WebdavService`, connection lookup and directory listings, all keyed on connection id. The listing key carries the connection too: two servers with the same folder name were otherwise served each other's contents from cache.
- `s3_provider.dart` — The S3 counterparts: `s3ServiceProvider` (bucket config + secret access key from the keychain, `null` when the connection is gone, half-filled or keyless), `s3ConnectionsProvider`/`primaryS3ConnectionProvider` and `s3ListingProvider`. Keyed on connection id for the same reason as WebDAV — a corrected typo in the endpoint must not detach an open deck from its source.
- `matrix_client_provider.dart` — the collaboration counterpart of `webdav_provider`/`s3_provider`: `matrixClientProvider` builds the app-global `MatrixClient` from the configured account plus the access token from the keychain, over the platform SSRF-pinned/browser transport, `null` when no account or no token. One client per app, shared across sessions and tabs (the CS calls are stateless on the client). `matrixAccountProvider` is a thin read of `settingsProvider.matrixAccount` — the one value the collab provider and a test depend on, rather than all of settings. `buildMatrixClient` is the transport-injectable, socket-free builder the provider calls (and tests call directly). Design: SELF_ENCRYPTED_RELAY.md §6.5, §11.
- `secret_store_provider.dart` — `secretStoreProvider`: the app-wide `SecretStore` as a provider rather than a bare `SecretStore()` at each call site, so a test can substitute a fake keychain and the collab session provider can load its device seeds (`loadOrCreateDeviceKeys`) under test. Defaults to the real keychain.
- `presentation_sources.dart` — Builds a `PresentationSource` per configured remote connection (git, WebDAV, S3) — the sources *Slide zoeken* sweeps beside the local libraries. The clients (with their keychain secret) are prepared here; the network traffic itself only happens in the finder, on the user's search.
- `provider_retry.dart` — `noAutoRetry`: the retry policy for providers whose failure is *shown* to the user. Riverpod 3 retries a throwing provider by itself, endlessly, and a retrying provider reads as `AsyncLoading` — so every screen with a careful `when(error: …)` explanation sat spinning forever instead. For these sources retrying is pointless anyway (configuration, sign-in, a blocked host), so retry belongs on a button the user presses.

## `lib/utils/` — small shared helpers

- `asn1_der.dart` — Minimal dependency-free ASN.1/DER encode + parse for RFC 3161 timestamping.
- `asset_destination.dart` — `resolveAssetDestination`: picks where an imported asset lands. On a name clash it compares contents — identical means reuse, different means a numbered suffix — so two pictures both called `screenshot.png` stay two pictures instead of silently becoming one.
- `archive_limits.dart` — `ExtractionLimitException`: het gedeelde signaal dat een uitgepakte entry voorbij zijn byte-budget groeit (decompressiebom-detectie), gedeeld door `file_service_package` en `cve_bulk_ingest`.
- `atomic_file.dart` — Atomic writes (temp file + rename) to prevent data loss on crash.
- `bullet_fixes.dart` — The deterministic one-click fixes behind the text-density quality reports: `splitSentenceBullets` cuts a multi-sentence bullet into one bullet per sentence (and copies the line as it was into the speaker notes, because the connection between those sentences lived in the full sentence), `trimBulletExplanations` moves the explanation behind a *label : explanation* bullet off the slide, and `splitBulletSlidePages` is the "split slide" page split itself — shared by the panel button, the fix-all engine and the live fix while presenting, so splitting means one thing everywhere. Next to an image the page target scales down with the narrower text column (`bulletsImageTextColumnFraction`, #1279), so the shared split-run font size fills the column instead of being pinned by an over-full page. Each fix has a `can…` twin so the panel offers an action only when it does something — and, for the sentence split, only while the result stays inside the readability threshold, since splitting adds bullets. Lines moved to the speaker notes are written with a `- ` in front (`_movedNoteLine`) so they read as a list there; a bare heading stays without one. `trimBulletExplanations` is gated on `visibleContentBulletCount(slide) <= kMoveToNotesMaxBulletCount`: above eight bullets a slide counts as *too many*, and there splitting is the only remedy offered, since moving text to the notes leaves the bullet count unchanged.
- `bundled_asset.dart` — `asset:`-schema voor méégebundelde logo's van ingebouwde stijlprofielen.
- `color_contrast.dart` — WCAG 2.1 contrast-ratio calculation, plus `tryParseHexColor`: the *strict* hex reader (six digits, always opaque, `null` when the input does not qualify) so a quality check can skip a pair instead of judging a colour it invented. The lenient counterpart, which always returns a colour, is `AppTheme.parseHexColor`.
- `content_hash.dart` — `sha512Hex` / `sha512HexOfText`: the seal hash, in the form `sha512sum` prints. A loose function rather than a service, because the seal and the markdown parser both need it and a shared service between those two would be an import cycle — and because this *is* the whole recipe: SHA-512 over the bytes, nothing around it that could add a step.
- `marp_style_values.dart` — Shared, dependency-free validation for Marp colour values and the bounded view of image filters that Flutter and HTML actually render; the full authored filter list remains available for round-tripping.
- `number_convention.dart` — Works out whether a file writes `1.234,56` or `1,234.56`, from evidence across all its values rather than per cell (`scanDecimalConvention`), and reads a value under a settled convention (`parseNumberUnder`). Deduces or refuses: what no value settles comes back as `undecided` for the chart import to ask about, never guessed from locale.
- `csv.dart` — RFC 4180 quoting for the two readers of CSV *files*, in two framings: `parseCsvRows` reads a whole document (a quoted field may hold a line break — MITRE's CWE export needs that) and `parseCsvLine` reads one already-split line, so a stray quote stops there instead of swallowing the file. Used by `models/chart.dart` and `tool/build_cwe_catalog.dart`. Also the scan behind `table_clipboard.dart` (spreadsheet paste). A fourth hand-rolled quote scanner fails `check_conventions.dart` — three had accumulated unnoticed before this was one file.
- `deck_markdown_dashes.dart` — Escapes standalone dash lines so the deck parser can't misread them.
- `file_extension.dart` — `withExtension`: voegt een extensie hoofdletterongevoelig toe via `p.extension`, zodat `Deck.MD` niet `Deck.MD.md` wordt. Vervangt vijf `endsWith`-checks die case-sensitive waren.
- `file_download.dart` — Het rauwe browserprimitief (blob + anker) achter een conditional import met stub. Exportpaden gebruiken het niet rechtstreeks maar via `services/download_delivery.dart`, dat één export ook echt als één download aflevert.
- `mem_asset_blob.dart` (+ `_stub` / `_web`) — `memAssetBlobUrl`: zet een `mem:`-pakket-media-asset om naar een `blob:`-URL zodat een `VideoPlayerController` pakket-video/audio op web kan afspelen (#854). Beeld tekent rechtstreeks uit de bytes; video/audio hebben een URL nodig. Web-only — de stub geeft buiten de browser null, waar een `mem:`-pad ook nooit langskomt.
- `image_focal.dart` — Maps a normalized image crop focal point (0..1) to the `Alignment` used to reposition a cropped/cover image.
- `image_limits.dart` — Caps decoded image dimensions to prevent OOM; the `CappedImage` provider only downscales over-cap images so within-cap animated GIFs/WebP decode natively and keep animating.
- `image_dimensions.dart` — Lightweight image dimension reader: reads only the file header (PNG, JPEG, GIF, BMP, WebP) without a full decode. Used by the callout clip check (#1853) to determine whether a target falls outside the visible image area under cover/zoom.
- `image_resize.dart` — `resizeLongestEdge`: deelt de resize-stap (langste zijde naar maxEdge met average interpolation) die `html_image_embedder` en `image_alt_ai_service` gemeen hadden. Decode-cap, EXIF-strip en encodeerkeuze blijven per aanroeper. Daarnaast het geometrierecept van de import: `jpegExifRotationDegrees` leest de EXIF-orientatie uit de rúwe bytes (`img.decodeImage` bakt hem in en wist de tag, dus achteraf is hij niet meer te zien), `bakeImportGeometry` bakt de `ImportImageGeometry` van het bronprogramma — uitsnede, spiegeling, rotatie — in die volgorde, voor alle drie de formaten. Bij PPTX en ODP telt die geometrie vanaf de rúwe pixels en gaat de door de decoder gebakken EXIF-hoek er eerst weer af; bij Keynote telt ze vanaf de rechtgezette foto en blijft die staan (`sourceHonoursExif`). `imageNaturalSizeCm` geeft de maat waartegen ODF `fo:clip` afmeet: pixelformaat gedeeld door de eigen pHYs-/JFIF-resolutie, met 0,2 mm per pixel als standaard.
- `media_fetch.dart` (+ `_io` / `_web`) — `guardedNetworkImage`: the image provider for a deck-supplied remote URL. On desktop it fetches the bytes itself over a socket pinned by `NetGuard.connectPinned`, so `NetworkImage` never re-resolves the host and there is no DNS-rebind window between check and fetch. On web the browser (CORS, mixed content) and the page CSP are the gate — `dart:io` pinning cannot run there.
- `image_luminance.dart` — Computes average image colour, cached by mtime/size.
- `markdown_files.dart` — What OciDeck counts as an editable text file (`kEditableMarkdownExtensions`: `.md`, `.markdown`, `.txt`; `isEditableMarkdownFile`) and how such a file is named without opening it (`firstMarkdownHeading`, front-matter- and fence-aware). Shared by the folder scan, the disk-wide scan and the open surfaces: with the list in three places a `.txt` would show up in one search and not in the other, which reads as "search is broken".
- `library_scan_limits.dart` — Shared upper bounds (`kLibraryScanMaxDepth` 32, `kLibraryScanMaxFiles` 20000) for a recursive on-disk library walk, used by both the deck file scan (`ImageReferenceService.findDeckFiles`) and the image picker (`ImageLibraryScanner`) so a mounted or pathologically large folder caps both paths the same way (#1049).
- `jaro_winkler.dart` — `jaro`/`jaroWinkler` similarity plus `normalizeAnswerText`, `bestAnswerMatch` and `bestAnswerSimilarity`, behind the open-text question kind: a typo should not be wrong, a different word should be. Normalisation lowercases and collapses whitespace but keeps punctuation, since punctuation is sometimes part of the answer and the threshold absorbs a stray one. `bestAnswerMatch` returns the winning answer *and* its score, not just the number — with several accepted answers the viewer has to be shown which one theirs was measured against, and that is rarely the first in the list; `bestAnswerSimilarity` is the score-only shorthand over it. Hand-written rather than a package — it is twenty lines of arithmetic, and every dependency is one more thing to weigh, scan and account for.
- `json_depth_guard.dart` — `jsonDecodeGuarded`: a pre-scan that counts bracket/brace nesting depth (string-aware, so a `[` inside a JSON string value doesn't trip it) and refuses input deeper than `kMaxJsonNestingDepth` before `jsonDecode` can overflow the stack. Called at every trust boundary — sidecars, recovery files, chart data, style profiles — so a corrupt or hostile file throws a catchable `FormatException` instead of crashing the app with an uncatchable `StackOverflowError`.
- `inline_markdown.dart` — The widget-free half of the inline markdown: `parseInlineRuns`/`stripInlineMarkdown`/`inlineRunStyle`/`buildInlineSpans`. Text and its styling, not a widget tree, so the headless services (`text_measurement`, `slide_quality_analyzer`) can measure and analyse exactly what the render will show without importing the UI layer. One exception is inline `$…$` math (a run flagged `math`, its text the raw TeX, gated to content with a LaTeX command so currency like `$5` stays text): the widget half renders it as a real `Math.tex`, but this widget-free side can only measure it as its source text — a safe over-estimate of the width, so a line never under-measures and slides under the logo (#948).
- `log.dart` — Fail-soft logging to DevTools without exposing sensitive data (`logError`/`logWarning`). The rule is that a message carries an operation description and the caught error, never deck or file *contents*; `test/log_no_content_test.dart` scans `lib/` for the shapes that break it (a collection joined, taken from or sliced into a message), which is how a chart warning holding real cell values was found.
- `lru_cache.dart` — Fixed-capacity LRU cache backed by `LinkedHashMap`.
- `marp_emoji.dart` — Compact offline Marp/GitHub shortcode-to-Unicode table shared by Flutter and HTML rendering; unknown aliases remain literal and no emoji CDN is contacted.
- `markdown_caret_map.dart` — `MarkdownCaretMap`: translates a caret position back and forth between the Markdown source and the plain text of the visual (rich-text) editor, so switching between Visueel and Bron leaves you where you were (#1566). It maps per line, because that is how the text sits on both sides: the visual editor puts every block on its own line, drops blank source lines, and replaces a table or thematic break with a single object character. Within a line the source and the visible text are walked together — the visible text is always a *subsequence* of the source, since markup only removes characters — which keeps the column right even in the middle of `**bold**`. The reverse direction lands *just before* the visible character, not at the first position with the right count, so typing does not start inside a marker.
- `markdown_paste_cleanup.dart` — Cleans pasted website markdown and normalizes rich-text quirks.
- `html_to_markdown.dart` — Zet klembord-HTML om naar Markdown (koppen, geneste lijsten, nadruk, links, code, tabellen). Stijl, class, script en `javascript:`/`data:`-URL's vallen weg; de HTML zelf wordt nooit gerenderd (#1595).
- `clipboard_markdown.dart` — Kiest wat een plak in de documentbron wordt: spreadsheet-tabel uit platte tekst, anders HTML→Markdown, anders opgeschoonde platte tekst.
- `markdown_quill_codec.dart` — Round-trip conversion between markdown and Quill documents. GFM-tabellen, de `<!-- toc -->`-marker en voetnoten gaan als embed (`x-embed-table` / `x-embed-toc` / `x-embed-footnote-ref` + `-def`) heen en weer, zodat ze de visuele modus niet naar brontekst dwingen.
- `source_patcher.dart` — Past bewerkingen uit de visuele editor toe op de originele bron, in plaats van de hele round-trip-uitvoer te overschrijven. Vergelijkt drie versies (origineel, baseline = round-trip zónder bewerkingen, current = round-trip mét bewerkingen) en diff't baseline → current om de echte bewerkingen te isoleren. Die diff toegepast op het origineel levert de byte-getrouwe versie op (#1613).
- `timeline_table_embed_syntax.dart` — Quill block syntax and embed carrier that keeps a recognised timeline marker immediately adjacent to its raw GFM table during rich-text round-trips.
- `markdown_visual_compatibility.dart` — Detects raw HTML/directives and escapes that the Quill bridge cannot yet round-trip; those notes remain in protected source mode instead of being flattened. Uitzonderingen die er wél doorheen komen omdat ze als embed reizen: de GFM-tabel (`x-embed-table`, inclusief **al zijn regels** — de `<br>` van een celregeleinde en de `\|`/`\\` van `encodeMarkdownTableCell` zijn de schrijfwijze van dat blok, niet rauwe HTML of een ontsnapping van de rijke-tekstlaag), de inhoudsopgave-marker `<!-- toc -->` (`x-embed-toc`), de voetnoot (`x-embed-footnote-ref`/`-def`) en de afbeelding (`x-embed-image`) — telkens wierp één ingevoegd element anders het hele document terug in de brontekst. Andersom levert een tabelregel die *niet* bij zo'n blok hoort juist `looseTableLine` op: `_normalizeQuillOutput` laat de escapes op elke pijpregel bewust staan, dus buiten een echt tabelblok blijven Quills `\-` en `\.` in de tekst van de gebruiker staan.
- `net_guard.dart` — SSRF guards (host/address checks, `safeResolve`, `resolveConfigured` — which names *why* a host was refused, so a typo is not blamed on the trusted-internal setting — and the media resolve gate) against DNS rebind, plus `connectPinned` — the only supported way to build a pinned `connectionFactory`, because setting one makes the factory solely responsible for TLS (a plain socket meant no encryption at all).
- `pinned_http_client.dart` — `buildPinnedClient`: het gedeelde recept dat een `HttpClient` bouwt met `connectPinned` + `pinnedCertCheck`. Voorheen gekopieerd in vijf transports (cve, git, matrix, webdav, s3); één helper houdt de pinning op één plek. De scheme-check, resolve, body-cap en error-types blijven per transport.
- `secure_compare.dart` — `constantTimeEqualsBytes` / `constantTimeEqualsHex`: constant-time vergelijking voor hashes en HMACs, om timing-side-channels te vermijden bij seal-, timestamp- en redactieverificatie (#1366).
- `footnotes.dart` — De voetnoot-ontleding van een document: `documentFootnotes` (label, tekst en het volgnummer in leesvolgorde), `stripFootnoteDefinitions` (de definitieregels uit de lopende tekst), `nextFootnoteLabel` en `insertFootnoteIntoSource`. Pandoc-syntaxis (`[^1]` / `[^1]: …`), dus een document mét noten blijft een gewone `.md`. Een verwijzing zonder definitie is tekst en een definitie zonder verwijzing krijgt geen nummer — allebei zoals Pandoc het leest, en samen de reden dat `[^abc]` in een technische tekst geen merkteken wordt. Gedeeld door de weergave, de vellen en beide exports, zodat een noot overal hetzelfde nummer krijgt.
- `footnote_embed_syntax.dart` — `EmbeddableFootnoteRef` (`x-embed-footnote-ref`, inline) en `EmbeddableFootnoteDef` (`x-embed-footnote-def`, blok) plus hun markdown-syntaxregels: voetnoten als embeds in de Quill-brug. Twee stuks en niet één, zodat de definitie blijft staan waar de auteur hem in het bestand zette; met één embed die tekst én merkteken droeg zou hij naar de verwijzing verhuizen. Hierdoor is een voetnoot geen `markdownVisualLimitations`-terugval meer.
- `pentest_block_embed_syntax.dart` — De twee Quill-bloksyntaxen en de gedeelde `EmbeddablePentestBlock` (`x-embed-pentest-block`) voor de pentest-enveloppen (PENTEST_DOCUMENT.md §5.5). Van een bevinding reist alleen de **kop** atomair — markerregel, kopregel en de veldregels tot de eerste sectiekop, waar de `<br>` van de hertestnotitie zit; de sectieteksten blijven bewerkbare Markdown. De andere vier enveloppen reizen in hun geheel. De grensregels staan niet hier maar in `services/pentest_blocks.dart`: de syntaxen stellen er per regel een vraag aan (`pentestFindingHeadEndsAt`, `pentestBlockEndsAt`) in plaats van de extent-lus te kopiëren — het verschil met `TimelineTableSyntax`, dat zijn lus deelt met de brug en de scanner zonder dat iets die drie bij elkaar houdt. Eén embeddable voor alle vijf: de soort staat in de bloktekst zelf (de markerregel is de eerste regel van `data`), dus een tweede administratie zou alleen uit de pas kunnen lopen.
- `image_embed_syntax.dart` — `EmbeddableMarkdownImage` (`x-embed-image`, inline) + `MarkdownImageSyntax`: een afbeelding reist met haar **hele** markdown als embed door de Quill-brug. De standaardweg van `markdown_quill` maakt er een `image`-embed van die alleen de bron draagt, en dat kostte twee dingen: het schrijfvlak viel om (geen bouwer voor dat type — `UnimplementedError` tijdens het tekenen van de regel) en de terugweg schreef `![](bron)`, dus de alt-tekst was weg. `parse` splitst alt en bron voor wie de afbeelding wil *tonen*; de opgeslagen waarde blijft de letterlijke markdown.
- `toc_embed_syntax.dart` — `EmbeddableToc` (`x-embed-toc`) + `TocMarkerSyntax`: de `<!-- toc -->`-marker als blok-embed in de Quill-brug. De marker is HTML-commentaar, en rauwe HTML dwingt de visuele modus naar brontekst; als embed reist hij byte-getrouw heen en weer. De blokregel staat vóór de HTML-blokregel van de markdown-parser en wikkelt de embed in een `p`, omdat `MarkdownToDelta` alleen dán een eigen regel afsluit — een kale embed erfde de blokopmaak van de volgende kop.
- `mermaid_embed_syntax.dart` — `EmbeddableMermaid` (`x-embed-mermaid`) + `MermaidFenceSyntax`: de ```mermaid-fence als blok-embed in de Quill-brug. Anders dan bij de inhoudsopgave en de voetnoot was dit géén `markdownVisualLimitations`-terugval: de fence-scanner slaat alles binnen een fence bewust over, dus de visuele modus ging gewoon open en toonde het diagram als monospace brontekst terwijl lezer, voorvertoning, Pagina's, PDF en HTML-export het wél tekenden (#1920). De embed draagt de héle fence, beide ```-regels inbegrepen, en de terugweg schrijft die verbatim weg — `DeltaToMarkdown` escapet leestekens, en een `%%{init}`-directive of een `<br/>` in een label is geen proza. De blokregel staat vóór de standaard-fenceregel en wikkelt de embed in een `p`, om dezelfde blokopmaak-lekkage te vermijden als bij de andere blok-embeds.
- `user_facing_error.dart` — Caught exception → short, actionable, translated message. One table per storage kind (`webdavErrorMessage`, `gitForgeErrorMessage`, `s3ErrorMessage`), each an exhaustive switch so a new error kind cannot be forgotten; tests assert no two kinds share a message. The raw service message stays for the log. `importFailureText` does the same for the presentation import (#806): an `ImportFailureReason` → a localised message with the `{bestand}`/`{formaat}` placeholders filled from `args`. Its `other` branch, carrying a caught write error from the bulk queue in `cause`, delegates back to `userFacingError` so "disk full" still reads properly. Each `l10n.d(...)` here is a single-line literal on purpose: the translation gate reads one literal per `.d(`, so an adjacent-string split would leave only the first fragment guarded.
- `page_scoped_notes.dart` — Per-page speaker/user-notes parsing and storage.
- `password_generator.dart` — Cryptographically strong random passwords (`Random.secure`) for encrypted packages.
- `password_strength.dart` — Entropy-based password-strength estimate (warn-only) for the encrypt dialog.
- `project_path.dart` — Path resolution with project containment and symlink checking.
- `export_link.dart` — Gedeelde fail-closed schema-allowlist voor klikbare links in audience-exports: alleen web, e-mail en interne ankers; onbekende, relatieve en bestands-URL's blijven platte tekst.
- `sanitize_svg.dart` — Keeps only the SVG elements and attributes `flutter_svg` actually reads, and drops the rest with a log line. The allow-list is read off `vector_graphics_compiler`, so it is never stricter than the renderer: what it refuses would have been discarded anyway.
- `safe_filename.dart` — `sanitizeFilename`: deelt het `[\w\s-]`-recept om vrije tekst tot een veilige bestandsnaam-stam te maken, met een fallback-parameter per aanroeper. Voorheen gekopieerd in `file_service_package`, `shell_actions` en `file_service_style_profile`.
- `physical_control_shortcut.dart` — `ControlHActivator`: Flutter-vangnet voor Ctrl+H-varianten die macOS als Backspace doorgeeft.
- `shortcut_label.dart` — `shortcutLabel`/`labelWithShortcut`: the one place a keyboard shortcut becomes visible text. A shortcut is part translation and part identifier — the key letter is bound to a `LogicalKeyboardKey` and never changes, the modifier does (German reads `Strg`, French `Maj`) — so only the modifier goes through `d()` and the notation is composed here. Keeps a new shortcut free of 31 translations, and stops the same shortcut being spelled two ways in one screen.
- `table_dates.dart` — `parseIsoDateCell`/`isPastDateCell`: recognises a table cell that is a bare ISO date and whether it has passed. Strict `yyyy-mm-dd` only, and it rejects dates that do not exist (`DateTime` silently rolls 31 February into March). Drives the `table-overdue` marking, which is derived against the day the deck is shown rather than stored.
- `table_clipboard.dart` — Recognises whether clipboard content is a table and which separator it uses (the part that is genuinely about a paste); the field scanning is `csv.dart`. Parses tabular clipboard content (TSV, CSV, markdown tables).
- `table_cell_navigation.dart` — Headless cel-navigatie voor tabel-editors: Tab/Shift+Tab/Enter over cellen plus `tableArrowTarget` voor de pijltjes, gedeeld tussen de bouwer, de documenttabel en de presentatiemodus. Tab op de laatste cel maakt een rij. De pijltjes doen wat een rekenblad doet: ←/→ verplaatsen eerst de cursor door de celtekst en springen pas aan de rand naar de buurcel, ↑/↓ gaan een rij op of neer zodra de cursor de boven- of onderste regel van de cel heeft bereikt, en niets loopt door voorbij de rand van de tabel.
- `table_number_format.dart` — Taalbewuste getalnotatie voor tabelcellen: `formatTableCellNumber` formatteert een getal volgens de deck-taal (`1.234,5` in nl, `1,234.5` in en), niet-parseerbare cellen blijven ongewijzigd. Render-time, de ruwe celinhoud staat in de .md.
- `table_pagination.dart` — Splitsing van lange tabellen over meerdere dias: `splitTableSlidePages` deelt rijen in pagina's van hooguit `kTableRowsPerPage` met de koprij herhaald, hetzelfde patroon als `splitBulletSlidePages`.
- `text_diff.dart` — `diffText` plus `leftSide`/`rightSide`: the classic longest-common-subsequence over runes, splitting two short texts into `same`/`onlyLeft`/`onlyRight` segments. Behind the correction shown after a typed question answer, so "wrong" can say *where*. Case-insensitive when matching but it hands back the original casing, because capitals do not count towards marking an answer right either and pointing at them would mislead. `textDiffMaxLength` (400 runes) caps it: the work is quadratic, and past that length the two texts come back whole and unmarked rather than slowly.
- `text_search.dart` — Case-insensitive text search/replace with match tracking.
- `title_contrast.dart` — Evaluates title contrast and recommends WCAG fixes.
- `url_launcher_util.dart` — Opens external links with scheme and SSRF validation.
- `document_front_matter.dart` — De byte-chirurgische front-matter-helper van de documentmodus: `splitDocumentFrontMatter`, `documentFrontMatterValue` en `withDocumentFrontMatterKey` beheren uitsluitend structurele sleutels op kolom nul. `documentFrontMatterMetadata` scant de kop eenmaal en wordt door `MarkdownDocument` bij bodywijzigingen hergebruikt. `documentFields`/`withDocumentFields` lezen en vervangen geordende, eenregelige vrije velden met sleutels `[a-z][a-z0-9_-]*`, maximaal 100 velden en 4096 tekens per waarde; dubbelingen blijven zichtbaar totdat de auteur ze oplost. De structurele sleutels en `ocideck_*` zijn gereserveerd. Onbekende, geneste en commentaarregels blijven verbatim, LF/CRLF inbegrepen.
- `markdown_blocks.dart` — De gedeelde ` ```chart `-fence (`chartFencePattern`) van de documentmodus. Eén bron in `utils/` (dat zowel widgets als services mogen importeren) voor het editor-scherm (de fence vervangen bij bewerken), de HTML-export (`MarpHtmlService`, de fence → inline-SVG) en de grafiek-hydratie — voorheen droeg elk een byte-getrouwe kopie van de regex. GFM-tabellen wonen bewust in `services/markdown_table_codec.dart`: sinds de office-tabellen deelt de documentmodus die app-brede tabelcodec (per-kolomuitlijning, meerregelige cellen via `<br>`) met de rapportagedia's, de import en het klembord.
- `image_search_paths.dart` — Zoekwortels voor de afbeeldingscarrousel: `deckImageSearchPaths` (project/`images` eerst, dan bibliotheken) en `documentImageSearchPaths` (bibliotheken, open presentaties, recente `images`/`logos`, zonder de ouder-map van een los `.md` zodat Desktop de bestandsgrens niet vult).
- `doc_link.dart` — Classifies a link tapped in the documentation reader (`resolveDocLink`): an external link (opens in the browser), an internal `.md` document link resolved relative to the current document (a bundled target opens in the reader, a repo-only target opens the repository version in the browser), or a `#anchor` (scrolls within the current document). Pure and fully unit-tested, so the routing is decided without widgets or I/O. Also `headingSlug` (the GitHub-style anchor slug of a heading, matched against the anchors the docs themselves use) and `firstHeadingText` (a document's first `#` heading, used as the title when a link opens it).
- `zip_encryption.dart` — Detects whether a `.ocideck` zip is password-encrypted (header inspection, no password needed).
- `display_path.dart` — `displayFolder`: the readable *whereabouts* of a file for open-lists, not the whole path — the filename is already the title above it. A full path is unusable in a narrow list: the shared prefix eats the space and the ellipsis swallows exactly the distinguishing end. Shows the full path in a tooltip and the short form here.
- `error_snackbar.dart` — `showErrorSnackBar`: an error the user can **copy**. The SnackBar carries a copy action putting the exact text on the clipboard, so a failure can be forwarded without retyping it, and it lingers a little longer than a normal notification so there is time to read it. Takes the `ScaffoldMessengerState` the caller already holds, so it is safe after an async gap.

## `lib/platform/` — platform abstraction (conditional imports)

- `launch_files.dart` — Launch-argumenten (Windows/Linux-bestandsassociaties) en de `?deck=`-deeplink-parser.
- `native_window.dart` — Export selector for platform-specific window configuration.
- `native_shortcut_channel.dart` — Ontvangt sneltoetsen zoals Ctrl+H die het macOS-venster vóór de tekstinvoer heeft onderschept.
- `native_window_io.dart` — Initialises native desktop window options (size, title, focus).
- `native_window_stub.dart` — No-op window stub for web (part of `native_window`).
- `platform_features.dart` — Feature detection: desktop, dual-screen, local projects.
- `platform_features_io.dart` — Desktop feature availability (part of `platform_features`).
- `platform_features_web.dart` — Web platform: no desktop features (part of `platform_features`).
- `presenter_fullscreen.dart` — Export selector: volledig scherm voor de presentatiemodus (desktop-venster of browser-API).
- `presenter_fullscreen_io.dart` — Desktop: nativeapi fullscreen (part of `presenter_fullscreen`).
- `presenter_fullscreen_web.dart` — Web: browser-Fullscreen-API (part of `presenter_fullscreen`).
- `unsaved_work_guard.dart` — Export selector: `setUnsavedWorkGuard(bool)`, called by the shell whenever the dirty state of the open tabs changes.
- `unsaved_work_guard_io.dart` — Desktop/mobile: a no-op. `windowManager`'s `setPreventClose` + `onWindowClose` already hold the window and the shell asks its own question there; two confirmations in a row teach nobody anything.
- `unsaved_work_guard_web.dart` — Web: registers a `beforeunload` listener while unsaved work is open, and removes it again. Both `preventDefault()` and a non-empty `returnValue` are set because browsers differ in which they honour; the wording is the browser's own and cannot be chosen. Registered only when needed — an always-present listener keeps some browsers from putting the page in their back/forward cache. The one listener is kept in a variable, since `removeEventListener` matches on identity.
- `clipboard_html.dart` — Leest de HTML-variant van het klembord. Gevel met io/web-split: Windows en web via `pasteboard`, macOS en Linux via het eigen `ocideck/clipboard`-kanaal (`NSPasteboard` / GTK `text/html`). De bytes gaan naar `html_to_markdown.dart`, nooit naar een renderer (#1595).

## `lib/theme/`

- `app_theme.dart` — Material 3 theme builder with brand colours and appearance profiles. Also home to `AppTheme.parseHexColor`, the single hex→`Color` reader for everything that renders a stored theme colour (previews, presenter, dialogs): it accepts `#RRGGBB`, `RRGGBB` or `AARRGGBB` and falls back rather than failing. Four private copies of that logic had accumulated elsewhere before this became one function. `_inputDecorationTheme` is the look of every text field in the app: an *outlined* field the way Material 3 means it — a border, and no fill. The fill used to be there, and a floating label sits half above the field's top edge and half inside it, so a colour change always ran straight through the letters (the gap in an `OutlineInputBorder` breaks the *line*, not the surface). Invisible in the light profile, a hard rule through the letters in the dark one (#811). The border carries the field on its own now, so it moved from `outlineVariant` (1.58–1.92:1 against what sits underneath) to `outline` (4.18–5.61:1) — past the 3:1 WCAG 1.4.11 asks for the boundary of a control.
- `appearance_scope.dart` — `AppearanceScope`: sets `AppTheme.isDark` from the chosen profile and, on a mode flip, marks every element below it as needing a rebuild. The second half is the point. `AppTheme.isDark` is a static flag — deliberately, because a slide has to render identically in a headless export isolate where no `BuildContext` exists (PENTEST_MIAUW §11) — and a static flag tells nobody it changed. The scope itself did rebuild; what stopped there was the tree below it, because `Element.updateChild` skips a rebuild when the new widget is identical to the old one and two `const` instances of the same thing are identical. So the slide-quality panel stayed dark green on a light interface until a restart (#780), along with every other surface colouring itself from `AppTheme` — 776 sites in 139 files (#814). Marking keeps every `State` alive, which is why it is marking and not a key on the mode: keying disposes `DeckNotifier` with the tab and takes the user's unsaved deck with it (tried and reverted in #780). The cost is one full rebuild per mode switch, and the trigger is the *mode*, not the profile — tuning one colour in a custom profile goes through `ThemeData` and propagates by itself.
- `appearance_contrast.dart` — `appearanceContrastFindings` / `appearanceContrastProblems`: the nine colour pairs in an app appearance profile that have to stay legible, measured. It builds the profile's real `ThemeData` and reads the resolved values rather than comparing the eight colour fields, because four of the nine pairs are not fields at all — the text-button foreground, the interactive colour, the tick on it and the primary button's label are derived in `AppTheme.fromProfile`, and that is exactly where #744 lived. One calculation, used both by the warning in the appearance editor and by `test/app_theme_contrast_test.dart`; two sums over the same question drift apart, and then the test no longer guards what the screen promises.
- `brand_logo.dart` — `BrandLogo`: the three bundled brand marks (OciDeck, LibreKAT, Vigilis), each paired with the variant for a dark surface, and `assetKey` to pick between them on `AppTheme.isDark`. The pairing exists because `ocideck-logo.png` carries its white plate baked in as opaque pixels — no tint can separate the plate from the drawing, so the dark variant is a second file with a transparent background and light ink. `ocideck-logo-eu.png` has no entry: it only sits on surfaces that are dark in both modes. The theme-profile logo in `models/settings.dart` deliberately stays the light asset — that one renders on a slide, and a slide is a white canvas in both modes.
- `finding_severity_palette.dart` — `FindingSeverityPalette`: severity → colour tokens for finding cards and CVSS badges, following the FIRST rating bands (PENTEST_MIAUW §11). With a `ThemeProfile` the bands come from its severity tokens (so the built-in security profile or a tuned deck restyles findings); without one, the deterministic `const` `AppTheme` tokens. Every token resolves through `AppTheme.parseHexColor` with the `const` default as fallback, so a finding renders identically on screen and in a headless export isolate. A filled score badge in the finding editor (small 12px label) draws its label colour from `AppTheme.labelOn(band)`, not a hardcoded white: on the light orange/amber bands (High/Medium) white is 3.2-3.6:1, under AA for normal text. On a slide the same badge is large (3:1 suffices) and stays white — that path is measured in `app_theme_contrast_test`; the editor path in `finding_severity_palette_test` (#821).
- `image_picker_palette.dart` — `ImagePickerPalette`: the deliberately dark chrome of the image picker (coverflow/grid). A palette of its own rather than `AppTheme` tokens, because this is a self-contained dark surface and not part of the light app theme; the raw-`Color(0x…)` conventions ratchet exempts this file for that reason. That exemption also kept it outside every contrast measurement until #779, when `textDim` turned out to colour both icons and text while clearing the 4.5:1 text bar on none of its own surfaces. It is now `iconDim` — the name carries the rule, because a `textDim` that may not colour text is a trap for the next caller — and `test/standalone_palette_contrast_test.dart` holds both bars.
- `presenter_palette.dart` — `PresenterPalette`: the same idea for full-screen presentation mode (presenter view, overlays, ink). Measured by the same test; its ink was already comfortable (9.0–15.7:1), but nothing was keeping it that way. `textMuted` en `outline` kwamen erbij in #780: tien plekken in de presenter improviseerden een tweede tekstniveau met `Colors.white24/30/38` — 2,06 tot 3,60:1, en géén daarvan zichtbaar voor de ruwe-kleurratchet, die alleen `Color(0x…)` matcht. Er is bewust geen derde tekstniveau: op deze oppervlakken haalt het tweede al net 4,5:1, dus een niveau daaronder kán geen tekst zijn — en zolang het bestaat, wordt het gebruikt.

## `lib/l10n/` — localization

- `app_localizations.dart` — `AppLocalizations` class + delegate; assembles the lookup maps from per-language files. Re-exposes the language set as `languageNames` from `language_registry.dart`.
- `language_registry.dart` — Flutter-free constants for build tooling: `kLanguageNames` (the canonical interface-language codes → display names) and `kDocsBaseLanguage`. Lives apart from `AppLocalizations`/`DocumentationService` so `dart run tool/translate_docs.dart` can read the language set without compiling Flutter's `material`/`dart:ui` graph (which the standalone VM cannot). Guard: `test/language_registry_test.dart`.
- `export_block_localization.dart` — De weigering van het classificatiebeleid als zin, in de taal van de gebruiker. Staat hier en niet in de dienst omdat de vertaallaag op letterlijke brontekst sleutelt: een zin die een TLP-niveau interpoleert heeft geen sleutel, en bereikte de gebruiker daardoor altijd in het Nederlands (#576).
- `page_size_localization.dart` — `pageSizeLabel`: a `PageSizeSpec` as a sentence, in the user's language. Lives here for the same reason `export_block_localization.dart` does — the translation layer keys on literal source text, and `"A4 (liggend)"` has no key of its own. The model supplies the language-neutral half (`sizeName`, `"A4"`) and this function interpolates it into the one translated string, so the paper name stays untranslated (it is a standard, not a word) while the orientation follows the interface language.
- `slide_quality_localization.dart` — Localized formatting for slide-quality issues and export summaries. `slideQualityFieldLabel` names the spot a finding sits in; given the slide it turns a table finding into "table row 4, column 2" by dividing the scanner's running cell index by the widest row — the same stride the scan used — and leaves it at the bare "Table" when it cannot, since no location beats a wrong one. `formatSlideQualityIssue` appends a run-scope sentence when a finding carries `args['runPages']` — the tag a folded split-run density summary leaves behind (#1289) — so the single entry can state how many slides of the run it stands for.
- `slide_quality_navigation.dart` — Routes a quality issue to the relevant editor field. `kDeckInfoFields` + `issueBelongsToDeckInfo` split the deck-wide issues in two: a contrast complaint is about the theme and opens the colour settings, while a privacy finding on a front-matter field (author, organisation, keywords, version, date, standards, tools, the two MIAUW motivations) opens *Presentation info* instead — it used to send the user hunting for a colour field that does not exist.

### `lib/l10n/translations/` (each `part of app_localizations.dart`)

- `lib/l10n/translations/*.dart` — **A declared group** (see *What this map covers*). One file per language, 32 in total. `nl.dart` is the source language; the other 31 carry the translations and are kept in step by `make add-l10n` / `make l10n-check`: `en` (English), `de`, `fr`, `es`, `it`, `pt`, `pl`, `uk`, `el`, `da`, `sv`, `fi`, `cs`, `sk`, `sl`, `hr`, `hu`, `ro`, `bg`, `et`, `lv`, `lt`, `ga`, `mt`, `tr`, `id`, `fy` (Frisian), `pap` (Papiamento), `gsw` (Swiss German), `la` (Latin) and `tlh` (Klingon).
