No line each: they carry the same 2,150 keys and differ only in the language, so a per-file line would say the same thing 32 times.

## `lib/widgets/` — UI

- `app_shell.dart` — Main application shell: layout, file IO, and dialog coordination. Also the listeners for the one-shot signals: `_listenChartDataWarning` (two texts, since a data file that could not be *read* leaves a chart empty while one that could not be *written* leaves the numbers only in this window — the second is shown as an error) and `_listenUnsavedWork`, which passes the dirty state to `setUnsavedWorkGuard` and, where `RecoveryService.available` is false, says once that a crash recovers nothing here. That notice waits for the first edit rather than firing at startup: a warning about losing work when there is no work yet reads as noise. `_tabScope` gives a document tab its own light `documentProvider` scope and a `DocumentEditorScreen`, instead of the deck's derived-provider chain and `_TabContent`.
- `document_editor_screen.dart` — `DocumentEditorScreen`: the full-screen editor for a document tab, bound to `documentProvider`. A **Visueel\|Bron\|Pagina's** toggle, an insert palette (chart/table/Mermaid/image/**page break**/paste), a **Style** picker (`_DocEditorToolbar._styleMenu`), an outline rail of the headings, undo/redo, export and convert-to-presentation live in `_DocEditorToolbar`; the page-break item (`_insertPageBreak`, `l10n.d('Pagina-einde')`) inserts a plain `---` thematic break — continuous on screen, a new sheet on print/PDF/LaTeX export (DOCUMENT_MODE.md §13); every keystroke flows straight to the `DocumentNotifier` (no "Apply" wall), and undo/redo drive the source from outside the editor while the controller re-syncs. The text surface is bound to the document **body** (source minus the style front matter); each write-back puts `frontMatter` in front again so `document.source` stays byte-faithful, and the Style picker sets/clears the `theme:` key byte-surgically via `MarkdownDocument.withStyleName` (locked to a house-style chip when the settings enforce a style). The resolved style profile (`resolveDocumentStyleProfile`, `document_style.dart`) colours the writing surface font and drives export; conversion and export read the body, so the style never travels as text (DOCUMENT_MODE.md §12). **Bron** puts the raw source beside a live `DocumentMarkdownView` preview (double-click a chart/table to edit it); `_DocumentSourceField` in `parts/document_source_field.dart` draws a non-editable line-number gutter over the left padding so the numbers never enter the `.md`. **Visueel** always mounts the shared `MarkdownNotesEditor`, which owns the WYSIWYG↔source degradation itself: rich-text where the bridge can round-trip, and otherwise the *editable* source text with the full formatting toolbar and a `markdownSourceModeHint` warning — never a read-only wall (owner decision 2026-08-08; the earlier `_renderedVisual` read-only fall-back is gone, DOCUMENT_MODE.md §4.3). **Pagina's** (added 2026-08-16) hands the same source to `PagedDocumentView` with the page size, the margins and any bleed from `effectiveDocumentPageSetup` — the document's own front matter when it carries one, otherwise the settings (changed 2026-08-17; the same resolution feeds the writing view's sheet lines and the export call): a reading and checking view, not a third writing surface — typing happens in Visueel or Bron. Insert/edit write portable Markdown blocks in place via the pure top-level helpers `insertBlockIntoSource`/`replaceNthChartBlock`/`replaceNthTableBlock`. The palette also carries one whole-document rewrite, **Hoofdstukken op nieuwe pagina** (`_applyChapterBreaks` → `applyChapterPageBreaks`, added 2026-08-17): it writes a `---` before every `H1` but the first, so "new chapter on a new page" lives in the file instead of in `AppSettings.documentChapterPageBreak` — idempotent, front matter untouched, and heading/break recognition borrowed from `DocumentMarkdownView.chapterHeadingLines`/`isThematicBreakLine` so a `#` or `---` inside a fenced block never counts (DOCUMENT_MODE.md §13.6). The same toolbar now carries the document-wide TLP picker and **Document · Eigenschappen**; that properties window edits fixed `title`/`subtitle`/`author` fields plus validated custom fields as one undoable, byte-surgical source change. All three views receive the same field map and resolve the style's header/footer placeholders through `DocumentChromeBand`.
- `parts/document_fields_dialog.dart` — The document-properties window: fixed title/subtitle/author controls and removable custom name/value rows. It exposes hand-written duplicates as separate rows, rejects invalid, reserved or duplicate names, and enforces the shared 100-field/4096-character limits before returning one ordered field map; empty values are omitted.
- `parts/document_export_pdf_support.dart` — Wat de PDF-export uit de schil nodig heeft: het gebundelde terugvalfont via `rootBundle` (zonder dat blijft de PDF bij Latin-1), de vertaalde teksten voor de lagen die zelf geen vertalingen kennen, en de zin die de gebruiker leest als er tekens ontbreken. Staat los van `document_editor_screen.dart` omdat `lib/services/pdf/` bewust Flutter-vrij is — een asset laden en een vertaling opzoeken is juist wél schilwerk.
- `theme_profile_logo.dart` — `ThemeProfileLogo`: one bounded logo renderer for style-profile surfaces. It resolves bundled `asset:` logos, web-memory logos and trusted local files, and is shared by the style chooser and both document-editor views so the selected logo does not silently differ between them.
- `document_page_chrome.dart` — `DocumentChromeBand`: the shared document header/footer row used by settings previews and all document-editor views. It combines logo, resolved `{field}` template text, inline Markdown, colours, accent rule and page label, and repeats the document-wide TLP marking in both bands when classified — even without a style profile.
- `mermaid_render_host.dart` — `MermaidRenderHostLayer`/`MermaidRenderHost`: the offstage WebView that `MermaidRenderService` renders its diagrams in, mounted only after the first diagram is requested.
- `asset_origin_badge.dart` — `AssetOriginBadge`: makes visible what happens to a slide's media once the presentation is passed on. Says what the origin *means* rather than where the file sits, with the consequence and the way out in the tooltip. Deliberately confined to the editor — the rendered slide is also what the audience and the export see, and a work instruction does not belong there.
- `privacy_badge.dart` — `PrivacyBadge`, the bare `PrivacyKatMark`, and the `privacyKatSvg` mark: the non-blocking marker (with an explanation on hover) for a spot where personal data is pointed at or something leaves the device. Used by the status bar's remote-origin badge, the export-readiness chip's privacy warnings, and the Security tab's online-CVE switch.
- `privacy_statement_content.dart` — Privacy/license content shared by the consent and settings dialogs.
- `collab_verify_banner.dart` — `CollabVerifyBanner` (provider-driven) and `CollabVerifyBannerView` (its look): the slim "not every device is verified yet" prompt above the workspace during a live realtime session, one tap opening the fingerprint comparison (COLLABORATION Phase 2 "Blok A"). Self-hiding — renders nothing unless a Matrix session is active and still has an unverified or mismatched peer; disappears once every device is pinned (`CollabSessionState.trustRevision`).
- `language_flag.dart` — `languageFlag`/`languageOptionRow`: the small flag beside a language in the pickers. Most languages get a platform flag emoji; Frisian a bundled image of the Frisian flag (a regional flag, not a protected mark); Klingon a neutral letter badge with its ISO 639-3 code — the Klingon trefoil is someone else's emblem, and reproducing it as artwork in every distributed binary is not nominative use.
- `duplicate_badges.dart` — The shared badges and `formatModifiedDate` for the duplicate lists: which copy is the newest, and which entries are name-mates rather than duplicates.
- `document_signature_view.dart` — `DocumentSignatureView` plus the decoder for an embedded `data:…;base64,…` signature image. The decoded bytes are memoised per URI so every caller gets the *same* `Uint8List`: `cappedMemoryImage` then compares equal across the export precache and the preview, which is what lets a drawn signature paint on the very first frame the rasteriser captures.
- `signature_draw_dialog.dart` — `SignatureDrawDialog`: a freehand signature pad (mouse, trackpad, touch or stylus) rasterised to a transparent PNG and returned as the same embedded `data:` URI `DocumentSignature.imagePath` already expects — so a drawn signature round-trips in the deck and is covered by the seal like the rest of the attestation. Cancelling returns null, so the caller keeps the typed signature.
- `resizable_dialog_box.dart` — `ResizableDialogBox` + `DialogResizeHandle`: a width-resizable replacement for the fixed `SizedBox(width:, height:)` that dialogs wrap their content in. The user drags the handle to widen the dialog so long file paths fit on one line (#1211, #1217). The builder pattern lets each dialog place the handle where it fits (footer, last row).
- `searchable_language_picker.dart` — `SearchableLanguagePicker`: a compact language selector (flag + name in an outlined field) that opens a dialog with a search box filtering by language code and display name, diacritics folded via `AppLocalizations.sortKey`; selecting a language calls `onLanguageChanged` at once. Replaces the scroll-through-31-languages dropdown in the consent gate (#1228).

### `lib/widgets/shell/` (`part of app_shell.dart`, except the ones marked below as a library of their own)

- `ai_actions.dart` — `_MainLayoutAiActions`: the bulk "wipe AI alt-texts" safety action.
- `app_menu_bar.dart` — **A library of its own, not a `part`.** `AppPlatformMenuBar` wraps the whole app in a `PlatformMenuBar` on macOS and returns its child untouched everywhere else (`PlatformProvidedMenuItem` throws off macOS, so the menus are not even built there). `buildAppMenus` is public and pure — a test walks the tree without opening a window — and takes an `AppMenuActions` (new, open, settings, user guide, shortcuts) plus a nullable `AppDeckMenuActions`; `null` means "no deck open", which greys the items out rather than hiding them. Cut/copy/paste/select-all go through `PlatformMenuItem.onSelectedIntent` so they reach the focused field: this bar replaces the standard macOS menu, and adding a menu bar must not take text editing away.
- `shell_deck_commands.dart` — **A library of its own, not a `part`.** `ShellDeckCommands` + `shellDeckCommandsProvider`: the workspace publishes what it can do (present, export, save, find, properties, palette, undo/redo and their enablement) so the menu bar — which hangs above the whole app, including the welcome screen — can reach actions that live inside the per-tab editor layer. `sameEnablement` keeps the publish out of every frame: the closures are rebuilt each build but close over the long-lived `State`, so only a changed on/off state has to travel.
- `provenance_actions.dart` — the provenance-signing UI actions (COLLABORATION Phase 2 "Blok C"): `runProvenanceSigning` (guard on a finalised, saved, clean deck → load the account's device keys, `signDeckProvenance`, apply, save so `.seal.json` carries it) and `provenanceSignCommands` (the "Herkomst ondertekenen" palette entry, shown only for a finalised deck with a Matrix account). Both top-level so the heavy logic does not count toward `_MainLayoutState`'s or the palette extension's size ratchets.
- `command_palette_actions.dart` — `_MainLayoutCommandPalette`: builds and shows the Ctrl/Cmd+K command list (incl. undo/redo, find, properties, the user guide and the shortcut sheet, and the security-module actions). `collabPaletteCommands` adds the collaboration entries — WebDAV host/join when the deck lives on WebDAV, realtime Matrix host/join when a Matrix account is configured (§6), and, once a Matrix session runs, copy-the-invite, open/close chat (`collabChatOpenProvider`), verify-participants (the fingerprint dialog, §4.3) and leave. `listenMatrixCollab` surfaces a Matrix session's outcome on its phase transition (the host's invite dialog, a guest going live, or the localised failure), because a guest only goes active once the baseline arrives well after the action returns.
- `shell_actions.dart` — File-IO helpers for deck import/export and the WebDAV source, plus the shared `requestCloseTab` helper. Draagt ook de twee gedeelde ingangen `_openFromConnection`/`_saveToConnection` (één "Openen uit…" en één "Opslaan naar…" voor alle opslagsoorten) en `_saveToOrigin`, waarmee de gewone opslaanknop teruggaat naar de plek waar het deck vandaan kwam in plaats van lokaal te landen. Also `emptyAudienceReason`, the one sentence shown when presenting or exporting is left with no slides at all: it names skipping and TLP withholding separately, and both together, because the old single line ("all slides are skipped") sent the user to a button that cannot undo a classification. `_reportOpenFailure` turns a failed "Openen" into a snackbar, and when the file the user picked was actually a presentation (`presentationOpenRescueSnackBar`, keyed on the extension) it carries a way out instead of the dead-end "OciDeck opens Markdown": an **Importeren** action that starts the import on the already-picked file when the module is on, or a link to **Instellingen** when it is off (#1175). On the desktop path the `onImport` closure reads the bytes for the chosen path so the user need not choose the file again.
- `shell_actions_present.dart` — `presentDeck` (and `_presentFromSlide`): the one shared entry the toolbar, the play-only screen and the command palette all funnel through, so slide filtering, the audience projection, annotation wiring and the fullscreen transition are identical everywhere. Split off from `shell_actions.dart` when it hit the 1000-line ceiling. Removes any lingering `MaterialBanner` (e.g. the Informatieveiligheid enable offer) before pushing the presenter route — otherwise the shared `ScaffoldMessenger` re-parents it onto the fullscreen Scaffold and the bar hangs over the audience screen.
- `shell_actions_connections.dart` — Which file connection carries out an action, and `saveDeckWithDestination`: a deck saves back to where it came from (WebDAV, S3, git) rather than silently landing on the laptop. `shouldAskDestination` is public and pure — it decides whether the destination dialog has anything to offer. It does not, when no library is configured: the list is then empty and the two remaining buttons both lead to the same system dialog, which was the "three dialogs deep to save a file" of #646, on the one path everyone starts from. With libraries it earns its place, so it is skipped rather than removed.
- `document_save_actions.dart` — **A library of its own, not a `part`.** `saveDocumentWithDestination`: the document counterpart of `saveDeckWithDestination`, and the single way every document save route lands — the app-wide `Ctrl/Cmd+S`, the document editor's own shortcut and save-on-quit all funnel through it. Byte-faithful back to the file's path when it has one and something changed; otherwise (a new document, or a path that can no longer be written) it falls back to *Save as…* so work is kept as a copy rather than dead-ending, and remembers the file in the recent list. Returns `false` when nothing was written — the picker was cancelled or the write failed — so save-on-quit can hold. Before it, `Ctrl/Cmd+S` on a document tab reached the deck-only save and did nothing useful; in Visual mode, where the editor's own shortcut never receives the keystroke, a document could not be saved at all. *(Added 2026-08-08.)*
- `shell_actions_git.dart` — The `…`-menu handlers for the git plane: open/save, sync, flush the outbox, history, versions, compare, resolve a merge conflict, open for review, merge the concept, tag a release, and the pool overview. Each one gates and reports; the dialogs themselves live next door. The save path reports its outcome through `_reportGitSaveResult`; the sealed-deck refusal it used to gate on is withdrawn (#541, closing round — the seal travels as a sidecar instead). *(Corrected 2026-07-23: this entry still described `_confirmGitOmissions`, the blocking "not everything travels" dialog counting `gitDeckOmissions`. That dialog was removed with #541 — once media, chart data, notes, ink and later the set-asides and the MIAUW disposition all travelled, it had no true line left.)*
- `shell_actions_git_dialogs.dart` — The git dialogs: browse, history, the version list and its compare picker, the version diff, the merge-conflict chooser, save, review, merge and tag.
- `shell_overlays.dart` — `_DropOverlay` and `_ResizableDivider` chrome.
- `openkat_import_action*.dart` — the platform-split entry for the desktop OpenKAT scenario wizard: its IO half owns only the folder picker and opens the wizard with its controller/gateway; after a successful build it opens a new tab or replaces marked generated views in the active OpenKAT deck. The web half is deliberately empty because this route scans a local folder; omit it rather than showing a disabled action. Menu, welcome screen and Integrations use this one action.
- `presentation_import_action.dart` — **A library of its own, not a `part`.** `importPresentation` (#772): the menu action behind *Presentaties importeren* — warning, file picker, conversion through `PresentationImportService`, and the result opened in a new tab. For a single file the conversion runs in two halves with the decision between them (#812): `prepare`, then `ImportDecisionDialog.ask`, then `PreparedImport.build` with whatever came back. A `null` answer means the user cancelled, and then nothing is built at all — the tab is only opened once there is a deck to put in it. Works on the picked bytes, so it exists on web too, unlike the OpenKAT action next to it. The deck lands unsaved: where an import belongs on disk is the user's call, and the warning suggests keeping it apart. `presentationImportLabel` is the shared menu label, so the menu and the dialog cannot drift. The picker takes several files at once, and *more than one* branches off to `PresentationImportQueueDialog` — one folder, one row, no ten tabs. Exactly one file keeps the original route unchanged: the queue's questions (order, destination) only exist from two upwards. `fileOverride`/`filesOverride` and `targetDirectoryOverride` exist for tests, like `directoryOverride` next door: the static FilePicker cannot be driven under `flutter test`. `presentationImportExtensions` is the single source for the picker's allowed extensions and for `isImportablePresentationName`, which lets a failed "Openen" recognise a presentation by its name; `presentationOpenRescue` turns that into the message-plus-action the open path shows (#1175). `importDroppedPresentations` is the drag-drop counterpart (#1175): the two drop handlers (`_onFilesDropped` in `app_shell.dart` for desktop paths, `_onWebFilesDropped` in `shell_actions.dart` for web `DropItem`s) read a dropped presentation to bytes and hand the batch here — with the module on it goes straight to `importPresentation` (one file opens, several queue), with the module off it shows the same pointer to **Instellingen** rather than importing silently.
- `openkat_import_summary.dart` — `OpenKatImportOutcome` (loaded, skipped, updatedDeck, failed) and `openKatImportSummary`, the sentence that goes with it. Deliberately outside the platform halves: it touches no dart:io, the web shell has to be able to name the same shape, and a second copy of this text in that shell is exactly how the two would drift apart. One sentence for two surfaces so that starting the import from Integrations reads the same as starting it from the menu.
- `status_bar.dart` — `_DeckStatusBar`: save state, file info, TLP classification, and the remote-origin privacy badge (`_RemoteOriginBadge` + `remoteOriginTooltip`, rendered with the shared `PrivacyBadge`) shown when a deck was fetched from a URL. The save chip is `_SaveStatusAction`: normally saved/unsaved, and during a save a spinner with the destination from the public, pure `saveProgressLabel` — a WebDAV, S3 or git save takes long enough that an unchanged screen is indistinguishable from a hung app.
- `tab_bar.dart` — `_AppTabBar`/`_TabChip` multi-deck tab management; `_TabContent` picks welcome / play-only / editor per deck.
- `module_prompts.dart` — (part of the `app_shell` library) the module discovery banners: a deck already carries Informatieveiligheid or Procesverbetering slide types while that module is off, so OciDeck offers to switch it on instead of leaving the slide inert. One subject, two modules, the same pattern. An `extension _ModulePrompts on _AppShellState` because the banner's lifetime is the shell's and its state lives in two fields there — a banner that survives a tab switch makes a claim about the wrong deck. Split out 2026-07-29 so `app_shell.dart` could shrink under its size ceiling.
- `welcome_screen.dart` — `_WelcomeScreen`: recent files and new/open/import actions, plus the two things a first-time visitor needs — one line saying what OciDeck is, and a direct button to the user guide beside *Settings* (`_footerLinks`, split off to keep the column under the length ceiling). *(Corrected 2026-08-19: a `_visibleTemplateCount` helper also printed how many templates waited behind *New presentation*. The helper and its source string are gone: a count is not what someone needs at the one place where there is a single thing to do, and the picker shows the templates themselves.)* With the OpenKAT integration revealed (`openKatIntegrationRevealProvider`, #1158) it also carries the OpenKAT import, behind the same gate as the menu item and under the same source string: whoever works from OpenKAT exports comes back to this screen most often, to refresh the overview. Outside Dutch and English, the *New presentation* button carries the same example-slides-are-English notice as the picker in `new_deck_dialog.dart` (`_withTemplateLanguageTooltip`, which wraps the button now that the count line it hung on is gone). On desktop, once library folders are configured, it also offers a **Manage images** button (`_imageLibraryButton`, gated on `supportsLocalProjectFolders` **and** a non-empty library set) whose handler `_manageImageLibrary` opens the carousel in its `manageOnly` mode (#1108) — maintenance without an open deck — searching the configured libraries and reusing `_imageUsages`/`_replaceImageUsages` so a clean-up still respects decks open in other tabs.
- `welcome_screen_chrome.dart` — (part of the `app_shell` library) the shared chrome of the welcome screen: one button shape plus the primary and secondary styles (`_widePrimaryButton`, `_wideSecondaryButton`), the Vigilis credit and the template-language tooltip. The two ways to start — *New presentation* and *New document* — share the primary style deliberately: they are equal routes, and an outlined button next to a filled one read as the lesser of the two. Split off when `welcome_screen.dart` hit the 1000-line ceiling.
- `play_only_screen.dart` — `_PlayOnlyScreen`: locked view for `Deck.playOnly` decks (first slide + Play + Close).

### `lib/widgets/panels/`

- `editor_panel.dart` — Routes slide edits to type-specific editors with toolbar/notes/timing controls.
- `editor_panel_slide_settings.dart` — The per-slide settings block (logo, footer, timing, table editing, audio, TLP, privacy disposition, and the *Weergave beperken* view-limit editor from #672, which shows totals and warns when a sort column holds no numbers). One row shape throughout, grouped into cards by the question you are actually asking; the cards sit side by side when the column is wide enough, so a label and its control stay within one glance instead of a thousand pixels apart. Collapsed, the header badges what deviates from the default — including the redaction disposition, which decides what the recipient gets.
- `preview_panel.dart` — Zoomable slide preview with rich-text page navigation. On a slide whose effective disposition is *redact* it shows the notice that says what will be left out (findings blacked out **and** all of that slide's media), with an *audience view* toggle (`audiencePreviewProvider`, off by default) that runs the current slide through `audiencePreviewSlide`. Off by default because the author has to see his own text to be able to edit it; the switch is there to check, not to work in.
- `slide_list_panel_clipboard.dart` — Copy-slide-as-image: an egress path, so it runs the same classification gate and privacy projection as a real export.
- `slide_list_panel_bars.dart` — The bars above the list (`part of slide_list_panel.dart`): `_SkipBanner` (how many slides are skipped, with *Alles tonen* to clear the marks), `_WithheldBanner` (how many are held back by their TLP) and `_BulkActionBar` for a multi-selection. Two separate banners on purpose: withholding is not a per-slide choice the author made and cannot be cleared by a button, so the withheld bar carries no action — raising the deck's level belongs with the TLP chip, not with a tidy-up in a sidebar.
- `slide_list_panel.dart` — Searchable, reorderable thumbnail list with import/paste/add controls. During a live Matrix session each thumbnail overlays `SlidePresenceDots` for the co-authors on that slide (§6 presence); the overlay renders only when a session is active, so the list is unchanged — and the goldens untouched — the rest of the time.
- `slide_presence_dots.dart` — `SlidePresenceDots`: the little coloured, initialled markers on a slide showing which co-authors are viewing it (§6, presence). A stable colour per device, a tooltip of the user id, and a "+N" once the row is full. `slideWithPresence` wraps a thumbnail with the overlay (or leaves it bare outside a session).
- `collab_chat_panel.dart` — `CollabChatPanel`: the session chat as a non-modal right rail (§6), shown while a Matrix session runs and the user opens it (toggled from the palette, `collabChatOpenProvider`). Renders `CollabSessionState.chatMessages` — own right-aligned, peers left with a presence-coloured dot and their user id — and sends what the user types. Editing continues alongside; it just takes width.
- `call_panel.dart` — `CallPanel` + `callRail`: the workspace call rail, revealed while the *Videovergaderingen* module is on. Renders the active `MeetingSession` backend-agnostically — participant tiles plus capability-driven mic/camera/screen/leave controls — with a placeholder until a call is live. Video frames are not drawn until `flutter_webrtc` (F3) maps a real track onto a tile.
- `slide_quality_panel.dart` — Slide accessibility/quality checks with issue filtering. Carries the **Fix all problems** button (#915), shown only when something is actually auto-fixable; it runs `DeckNotifier.fixAllStructuralIssues` (one undo step) and reports back that what remains needs a choice.
- `slide_quality_actions.dart` — `SlideQualityAction`: the concrete *do it* button beside a quality finding ("split this slide", "raise the contrast", "add alt text"), so the assistant does not only point at a problem but offers the fix.

### `lib/widgets/dialogs/`

- `add_slide_dialog.dart` — Selects a slide type when adding a slide. Below the grid sits the help text (`slideTypeHelpText`) of whatever the mouse or the keyboard focus points at — the same sentence that used to appear only *after* inserting, behind a collapsed hint — and it rides along as the card's screen-reader hint, because the strip below the grid is not something a screen reader passes. Its height is bounded in line heights rather than pixels, so it grows with 200% interface text instead of making the dialog jump.
- `command_palette.dart` — Searchable command overlay (Ctrl/Cmd+K); filters actions, keyboard-navigable.
- `consent_dialog.dart` — Initial consent/welcome dialog (privacy and license).
- `cvss_builder_dialog.dart` — Reusable per-metric CVSS 4.0 builder (`CvssBuilder`) + modal wrapper (`CvssBuilderDialog`) with a base/context score read-out; shared by the finding wizard and editor.
- `maswe_picker.dart` — `MaswePicker`: doorzoekbare kiezer over de MASWE-lijst voor het MASWE-veld van een bevinding. Toont de 78 zwakheden op id-volgorde met categorie en CWE; sinds de herbouw van MASWE zijn ze alle uitgeschreven, dus er is geen concept-markering meer.
- `cwe_picker.dart` — Searchable picker over the offline CWE catalog (finding editor / wizard).
- `cve_picker.dart` — Gated online CVE lookup dialog (by id pattern) returning the chosen id to the finding editor / wizard.
- `export_dialog.dart` — WYSIWYG export dialog for PDF/PPTX/HTML.
- `document_export_dialog.dart` — `DocumentExportDialog`: the compact export dialog for a plain-Markdown **document** — it picks the profile (Volledig / Geredigeerd) and the format (`.md` / HTML), states plainly that the result is a redacted copy for a recipient rather than the byte-faithful master (which only Save writes), and hands off PDF by telling the user to print the HTML from their browser. Pure presentation: the actual build-and-write runs through the injected `onExport` (docs/design/DOCUMENT_MODE.md §11.2).
- `convert_to_document_dialog.dart` — `ConvertToDocumentDialog`: the confirmation before presentation → document. It reassures that a **copy** opens in a new tab (the original is untouched) and lists what is dropped — the slide structure, the per-slide `_class`/theme, and the seal, which never travels because a sealed copy would be a false integrity claim (docs/design/DOCUMENT_MODE.md §11.3).
- `convert_to_presentation_dialog.dart` — `ConvertToPresentationDialog`: the confirmation before document → presentation. It shows the proposed slide count (the zero-loss deconstruction itself, not a guess) and the drop-list — a thematic `---` becomes a slide break, and the two forms are not a perfect mirror (docs/design/DOCUMENT_MODE.md §11.3).
- `find_replace_dialog.dart` — Full-text find-and-replace across all slides.
- `finding_template_picker.dart` — Searchable picker over the reusable finding-template library.
- `finding_wizard.dart` — The guided finding wizard (title → scope → CVSS builder → CWE → CVE → sections → emits a group); stores the base vector, context score derived from the scope object's CIA.
- `openkat_report_wizard/openkat_report_wizard.dart` — `OpenKatReportWizard`: modal desktop shell for the source gate, three wizard stages, preview, update/new decision and hand-off of a built report to the platform action. Its dialog surfaces and outline come from the active appearance profile.
- `openkat_report_wizard/openkat_recipe_picker.dart` — family-first recipe selector: shows the four evidence-gated report families, recommended recipes and the expandable **Meer rapportvragen** list without reimplementing scenario availability.
- `openkat_report_wizard/openkat_wizard_preview.dart` — source-derived preview pane for the selected recipe, including report facts, planned blocks and typed diagnostics, styled with the same appearance-profile surfaces, OciDeck accent language and radii as the wizard shell.
- `openkat_report_wizard/openkat_wizard_steps.dart` — source chooser, scenario/input/review steps and the explicit choice between updating the marked OpenKAT deck and making a new one.
- `openkat_report_wizard/openkat_wizard_strings.dart` — scenario, block and diagnostic wording projection for the wizard; input is typed IDs/codes rather than parsed display text.
- `openkat_installation_wizard.dart` — `OpenKatInstallationWizard`: ≤3-step dialog to add or edit a Rocky installation (name, URL, LAN flag, token, connection test before save). Token empty on edit means unchanged.
- `openkat_server_report_dialog.dart` — `OpenKatServerReportDialog`: pick installation → organisation → aggregate report; tries REST `/json/` (path A), otherwise guides JSON file/folder pick (path B) into the existing import wizard.
- `image_carousel_picker.dart` — Image-library carousel (grid and coverflow modes). A `manageOnly` flag (#1108) drops the picking half — the *Choose*/*Browse* buttons, the Enter/double-click confirm, and the choose-oriented hint — leaving a management screen with only the maintenance actions (dedupe in the footer, delete per selection in the preview). The start screen uses it to open the library without a deck; the deck editor keeps the default picking mode.
- `import_security_alarm_dialog.dart` — Hard-stop alarm screen for a rejected unsafe presentation.
- `import_rename_dialog.dart` — `ImportRenameDialog`: the post-import prompt to confirm or adjust the deck title. The deck is not yet on disk, so this is the moment the user sees that saving is still ahead; the deck is marked dirty afterwards so the tab dot and status bar keep showing it.
- `presentation_import_progress_dialog.dart` — `PresentationImportProgressDialog` (#875): the small cancelable progress window for a single-file import. It drives its own task — starts it in `initState`, updates the bar on each `ImportProgress`, and pops itself with the outcome the moment the task ends (parsed, failed or cancelled). The single-file read used to run with no window at all: the UI simply waited, and a large or hostile file froze it for seconds with no way to stop. Now the read runs on a worker isolate and this window shows the progress with a *Stoppen* button that cancels the `ImportCancelToken`; `PopScope(canPop: false)` keeps it from being dismissed out from under the running task. Modelled on the queue dialog's progress row and reusing the existing `Stoppen` string.
- `import_presentation_warning_dialog.dart` — `showPresentationImportWarning` (#772): the one-off warning before a presentation import — the conversion is best-effort, not a one-to-one copy, and importing into a separate folder keeps foreign material apart from your own. Being clear up front is the point: telling someone afterwards what was lost is not the same promise. Dismissible for good via a preference key that must never be renamed.
- `oversized_import_warning_dialog.dart` — `showOversizedImportWarning`: the per-file warning when a single presentation exceeds the 512 MiB soft source-size limit. Shows the actual file size and the limit, and lets the user proceed with a raised `maxSourceBytes` budget — the hard limits (uncompressed total, archive entries) stay enforced, so a confirmed large file is not a confirmed zip-bomb. No "don't show again": the consequences (slow, memory) vary per device and per file, so the warning belongs every time someone crosses the line.
- `import_decision_dialog.dart` — `ImportDecisionDialog` + `ask` (#812): what should happen to each source slide that carries real loss — carried over as completely as possible, its pictures only, or skipped. Raised between `prepare` and `build`, and only for a single-file import: the bulk queue asks nothing, because ten files times a question per slide is not a route. `ask` is the whole rule and the reason this is a static on the widget: no problem slides or an earlier *Niet meer vragen* returns an empty map (everything best-effort) without showing anything, a cancel or a dismissed barrier returns `null`, and `null` means the entire import is abandoned rather than quietly built anyway. Each row shows the reasons alongside the buttons — without knowing what went wrong the choice is a guess — and starts on `bestEffort`, because the explanation above the list promises exactly that for anything you do not touch; a preselected `imageOnly` would have thrown away the text of the very slides the dialog is asking about. Image-only is left out for a slide without a picture rather than shown and ignored. The apply-to-all row is a `Wrap` and not a `Row` on purpose: a label plus three buttons does not always fit on one line, least of all in the languages that need more words for them, and an overflowing row makes the last button unreachable rather than untidy. The *Niet meer vragen* preference key must never be renamed; it is written only on confirm, and an unreadable preference store means asking rather than deciding silently.
- `presentation_import_queue_dialog.dart` — `PresentationImportQueueDialog` (#772): the queue shown when *more than one* presentation is picked. The user orders the list (up/down, remove), points at one target folder, and watches the row run file by file; afterwards a summary counts what succeeded, what failed and how many slides need attention, and names the folder — the decks deliberately do not open in tabs, so without that path nobody knows where their work went. The separate-folder advice repeats the warning dialog's wording verbatim, on purpose: there it is general advice, here it is about the folder being pointed at right now. Drives `BulkImportRunner` with `FileService.saveDeck` and a real existence probe. `initialDirectory` is the test seam, like `directoryOverride` elsewhere: the static `FilePicker` cannot be driven under `flutter test`.
- `import_slides_dialog.dart` — Scans directories for presentations to import slides from.
- `management_summary_dialog.dart` — Shows the derived management summary (severity counts, coverage, standards).
- `miauw_compliance_panel.dart` — The MIAUW compliance overview panel with per-EIS status and waivers.
- `new_deck_dialog.dart` — Creates a new presentation with a title; the searchable template picker + `templatePickerIcons`, hiding templates gated by `requiresInfoSafety` (MIAUW and selected information-security starters) or `requiresProcesverbetering` until their module is revealed, then marking them with that module's badge. The same badge labels participate in search, and an empty result disables creation. A template's title and description run through `l10n.d()`; its example slides come from the per-content-language documents in `assets/templates/` via `services/template_content_service.dart`. That is a choice, not an omission — template content is deck content, and translating it via interface l10n would make a document's text depend on the menu language it happened to be created in. `_templateLanguageNotice` appears only for a language without its own content document (currently Klingon) and explains the English fallback.
- `improvement_project_setup_dialog.dart` — The optional Y-01 metadata step after choosing a DMAIC, DMADV, Kaizen, A3 or 8D template. It records the metric, unit, limits, target, baseline and goal without reintroducing a separate framework picker; cancelling aborts creation.
- `open_presentation_dialog.dart` — Full-text searchable file picker with directory scanning. Lists presentations *and* plain documents from one walk (`FileService.scanMarkdownFiles`), because the document side edits ordinary `.md` files and a decks-only list hid half of what OciDeck opens. Every row carries its kind (icon plus badge) and the *Alles · Presentaties · Documenten* filter narrows to one; the counts come from one search pass, so the filter never re-runs the query. A hit inside a presentation jumps to its slide; a hit inside a document opens the file, because there is no slide to jump to. Rows take the same multi-select handle as the slide list — Shift for a run, `Ctrl/Cmd` to add or drop one, a plain click opens that file straight away — and *Openen (n)* returns every chosen path in `OpenSearchResult.paths`; the selection is kept per path, because the visible list shifts with every keystroke in the search field.
- `open_kind_chrome.dart` — The shared "what kind of file is this" chrome for the open surfaces: `OpenKindFilter` (all/presentations/documents), `markdownKindIcon`/`markdownKindLabel`, `MarkdownKindBadge` and `OpenKindFilterBar`. One place, so the folder search, the disk-wide search and the recent list on the welcome screen say the same thing.
- `open_preview_pane.dart` — The optional rendered preview beside an open list (setting `showOpenPreview`, default off): the first slide of a presentation via `SlidePreviewWidget`, or the opening of a document via `DocumentMarkdownView`. It loads through exactly the same fail-closed gate as opening (`openDeckDetailed` / `openDocumentDetailed`), so it can never be a back door that renders content the app refuses to open; a 250 ms settle delay keeps a mouse sweeping the list from reading twenty files.
- `asset_usage_dialog.dart` — `AssetUsageDialog`: the shared image-pool overview — per image who references it, in three states (a deck uses it / only a released version still does / found nowhere). The cleanup section shows what could not be read instead of a candidate list when the round was incomplete, and calls a complete list a proposal rather than a verdict: another branch may still use what looks orphaned here. A dialog of its own rather than a `part` of the shell library, so that rule can be driven with a snapshot on its own.
- `asset_rights_dialog.dart` — Repository-wide review queue for possible image-rights issues; accepting an exact signal removes its active warning while retaining the audit record, and rejected signals remain visible.
- `settings/asset_rights_module_card.dart` — The Settings → Uitbreidingen switch card for the optional, default-off image-rights scanner, including its local-only and non-legal-conclusion boundary.
- `settings/video_calls_module_card.dart` — The Settings → Uitbreidingen switch card for the optional, default-off *Videovergaderingen* module (`NATIVE_CALLS.md` §7); the copy says plainly that joining a meeting arrives with the provider adapter in a later step. When enabled it exposes the XMPP-connection test and the media preflight.
- `settings/media_preflight_tile.dart` — `MediaPreflightTile` (F3.4a): the module card's honest media disclosure — the media-E2EE status for this platform (`mediaE2eeFor`) and, on demand, whether the native WebRTC stack loads on this device (`WebrtcMediaCore.selfTest`, which reaches no network). The media core is injectable, so the widget test drives it without a real stack.
- `hex_color_dialog.dart` — `HexColorDialog`: typing a custom colour as hex, with a swatch that follows the field. `normalize` is public because it is the whole rule: the hash may be omitted, case and spaces do not matter, but a half colour (`#33FF`) is not a colour and must not reach the theme profile. A dialog of its own for the same reason as `git_search_dialog.dart`.
- `git_search_dialog.dart` — `GitSearchDialog`: the cross-deck search UI. A button rather than search-as-you-type, since each round reads N files over REST. Picking a hit returns its deck dir to `_searchDecks`, which opens it through the ordinary `_openFromGit` path. A dialog of its own rather than a `part` of the shell library, so it can be driven with a `DeckSearch` on its own.
- `recovery_key_dialogs.dart` — `showRecoveryKeyDialog` (shows the identity's
  recovery key to save, with a copy button and the plain warning that losing it
  means losing the identity on a device switch) and `promptRecoveryKey` (takes a
  key to restore). Decoupled from the settings panel so both are widget-testable
  (COLLABORATION Phase 2 "Blok B").
- `matrix_collab_dialogs.dart` — The dialogs of a realtime Matrix session (§6.5): `showMatrixInviteDialog` shows the host's shareable link with a one-tap copy; `promptMatrixInvite` asks a guest to paste one; `showMatrixParticipantsDialog` lists the session's devices with their identity-key fingerprints for out-of-band comparison (§4.3). The link is the room secret, so it carries session access — but not content access, which stays E2EE by our own keys.
- `xmpp_test_connection_dialog.dart` — `XmppTestConnectionDialog` (F2/F3, `NATIVE_CALLS.md` §5): the first consumer of the XMPP client, opened from the *Videovergaderingen* module card. Server/JID/password fields and an optional conference-URL field; the test button opens a guarded `wss` stream, authenticates and binds a resource (`openXmppFrameTransport` + `XmppSession.connect`), and — if a conference URL is given — derives its companion room (`companionRoomJid` on `conference.<domain>`), joins that MUC (`XmppMuc`), reports which OciDeck users are present, and leaves; then closes the session. Shows the negotiated mechanism, the bound "Sessie actief als" JID, the derived companion room and its occupants, or a translated session/join failure. Stores nothing — it only proves the account (and optionally the companion pairing) works. The connection call is injectable, so the widget test drives the whole flow without a socket.
- `package_encrypt_dialog.dart` — Optional password protection when exporting a package: strength meter, generator, copy.
- `package_password_dialog.dart` — Prompts for the password when opening an encrypted package (with wrong-password retry).
- `presentation_info_dialog.dart` — Edits title/author/organization/description metadata. `editPresentationInfo(context, ref)` is the dialog *plus* the handling of what comes back (the `updateInfo` call and the app-wide style profile), lifted out of the shell because two routes lead here: the Properties menu item and a privacy finding on a front-matter field, which sits in the quality navigation and cannot reach a private shell method.
- `scan_library_dialog.dart` — Scans well-known locations for editable Markdown files. Presentations (OciDeck theme first) come before documents, each row carries its kind, and the same kind filter and optional preview as `open_presentation_dialog.dart`.
- `scope_coverage_dialog.dart` — Shows the scope-coverage gaps (in-scope objects with no test/finding).
- `seal_timestamp_dialog.dart` — RFC 3161 timestamp workflow: export the `.tsq`, import/verify the `.tsr`.
- `settings_dialog.dart` — Sidebar settings (theme colours, fonts, cockpit,
  Opslag, Licentie en Privacy, Beveiliging, Checklists, and an "Over OciDeck"
  screen); tab bodies live in `parts/settings_dialog_*.dart` (the Checklists tab
  managing user checklist templates is `parts/settings_dialog_checklists.dart`;
  the table house style is split in two by kind — its colours sit with the other
  colour pickers in `parts/settings_dialog_colors.dart`, its shape controls
  (border style, cell padding, zebra and accent-header switches) in
  `parts/settings_dialog_table_style.dart`).
  Which tabs exist, in what order, with which icon and label, is the
  `SettingsSection` enum in `parts/settings_dialog_sections.dart` — one list, on
  name. It replaced four index-aligned lists whose numbers silently drifted apart
  (the git tab was once inserted at index 8 without renumbering the search index,
  so "checklist" jumped to Git-repository). Nothing addresses a tab by number any
  more.
  The Cockpit tab owns two independent global choices: its Display dropdown
  selects `CockpitVisualStyle` (authentic by default, classic retained), while
  the named colour-scheme editor manages semantic instrument colours.
  The four network sources own their editing state instead of scattering it
  over the dialog: `settings/webdav_form.dart`, `settings/s3_form.dart`,
  `settings/git_form.dart` and `settings/ai_form.dart` each hold their
  controllers, their init and their save. Deliberately no shared base class —
  they differ for real (git has a forge/owner/repo, WebDAV a subfolder, S3 an
  addressing style, AI a mode) and forcing one type yields a base class with
  holes. Only the part that was literally identical is shared:
  `settings/keychain_secret.dart` (`KeychainSecret`), which answers "must this
  secret be written?" — the two silent failures being a save that lands before
  the async keychain read (blanks the secret) and an identity change that leaves
  it under the old key.
  Those four live outside the `part` scope on purpose (#631). Everything under
  `parts/` shares one private scope with `_SettingsDialogState`, so a change to
  the CVE panel could syntactically reach the S3 credential fields; no gate sees
  that and the compiler gives no signal. `lib/widgets/dialogs/settings/` is the
  half that has been pulled out of it.
  The dialog's own shell — sidebar, nav items, branded footer, content header,
  footer bar — is `parts/settings_dialog_chrome.dart`. It is the one block that
  reads only the selected section and writes it back, touching none of the
  settings, so it can be read without holding the rest of the state in mind.
  Integrations is one tab of switch-cards, one per connected system:
  `settings/integrations_panel.dart` (`IntegrationsPanel`, #1158) reads the
  register and draws a card per available integration — each with its own on/off
  switch and the OpenKAT logo (`assets/images/openkat-logo.png` — the logo, not
  the mascot photo from the About tab: there stand Brenno's cats, here stands the
  brand we connect to) — plus an "alles aan/uit" control that flips them all at
  once. Below the switch, once the connection is on or a folder is already set,
  it shows the integration's own body — for OpenKAT that is
  `settings/openkat_integration_panel.dart` (`OpenKatIntegrationBody`). Both are
  standalone widgets and not a `part` (#631): the dialog class sits against its
  ceiling and neither panel needs anything from it. The tab hangs off
  `anyIntegrationAvailableProvider` (`navItems`): since #1158 the switch lives on
  the tab itself, so the tab must be there as soon as an integration is
  available — on web the only integration (OpenKAT) falls away with its folder
  picker, and an empty "Integraties" tab is not information but noise. A second
  integration is one entry in `integration_registry.dart`; the tab and the bulk
  control follow by themselves.
  Storage is one tab and one list: `parts/settings_dialog_storage.dart` renders
  the file connections as a single reorderable list — folders, WebDAV servers,
  S3 buckets and git repositories mixed — plus the export folder. Order is not decoration:
  the topmost usable connection of a kind is that kind's default
  (`AppSettings.primaryOf`), so dragging is how the user says which server is
  *the* server. Each row expands to that kind's own panel:
  `WebdavPanel`, `S3Panel` and `GitPanel` in `lib/widgets/dialogs/settings/`.
  They are ordinary widgets with an explicit API — the form they edit, a
  `ConfirmCertificate` callback, and an `onChanged` that tells the dialog the
  connection row's status line is stale — so they can be built, and tested,
  without opening the dialog. That typedef sits in
  `settings/confirm_certificate.dart` rather than in a panel, because peeking at
  a certificate leans on `dart:io` and a panel must not drag that into the web
  bundle. The git panel's token-scope help shows, under the token field and
  switching with the forge dropdown, which token permissions each forge needs
  (Gitea/GitHub/GitLab differ, and only GitLab's `read_api` unlocks its
  server-side search) — proactive, not only after a failed connection test. The
  form objects live per connection id in `_webdavForms`/`_gitForms`/`_s3Forms`,
  because a `TextEditingController` cannot sit in an immutable model. A fifth
  kind is a value in `StorageConnectionKind` plus a branch in
  `_connectionPanel`.
  Search over the settings lives in `parts/settings_dialog_search.dart`
  (`SettingsSearchEntry`, the search field, and the jump-and-flash), with the
  index of what is searchable in `parts/settings_dialog_search_index.dart`.
  Picking/creating/deleting a style profile plus exporting and importing one as
  a `.ocideckstyle` file is `parts/settings_dialog_profile.dart`; it mutates via
  `_adoptProfile` on the state class, since `setState` is protected and out of
  reach from an extension.
  `parts/settings_dialog_presentation.dart` composes the style tab and owns
  `_StyleSurface` — the three-way split of a style profile into **Algemeen**
  (what a document and a presentation share), **Document** and **Presentatie**
  (what only exists there). A field belongs to exactly one surface; that is the
  whole rule, and the tab shows it. `_activeSurface` overrides the user's choice
  when a jump (settings search, or the quality panel's `highlightThemeField`)
  points at an anchor that hangs in another surface's tree — landing on the
  wrong surface would show nothing at all.
  `parts/settings_dialog_style_builder.dart` supplies the profile cards, the
  surface selector and hint, and the three editor panels; the colour blocks
  themselves come from `parts/settings_dialog_colors.dart`
  (`_generalColorChildren` for the shared ones, `_slideColorChildren` for the
  title/section colours a document does not have). The document panel owns
  `ThemeProfile.documentBodyFontSize` — the base type size of a page, which
  `documentHeadingSize`, the footnote list, the timeline cards, the visual
  editor's typography, `documentKeepWithNextHeight` and the exported
  `.document{font-size:…}` all derive from, so screen, writing surface and print
  stay one size.
  `parts/settings_dialog_style_preview.dart` holds one preview per surface —
  a chrome-less sheet for the shared settings, the A4 title/content sheet with
  header and footer bands for a document, and the real 16:9 slide renderer for a
  presentation. Each shows what you can set on that surface and nothing more.
  Anchors are free: every section heading is a `SettingsSectionTitle`
  (`settings/settings_section_title.dart`), which registers a `GlobalKey` under
  its own text, so a hit can scroll its section into view without any of the tab
  bodies knowing about search. It reads the key map and the highlighted section
  from a `SettingsSectionAnchors` inherited widget rather than from the dialog
  state — that is what lets a panel live outside the `part` scope. Shared input
  fields are `settings/settings_text_field.dart`: `SettingsTextField`, and
  `SettingsSecretField`, which refuses on web with the reason beside the field
  because a browser has no keychain.
- `settings/ai_module_card.dart` — `AiModuleCard`, the AI-assistance module card on the Uitbreidingen tab (#731), and `AiModuleOffNotice`, the line the AI tab shows above its configuration when the module is off. The card's switch edits the *form* value (`AiForm.enabled`), never the stored preference: `AiForm.save` writes the whole form on Save, including `enabled`, so a card writing straight to prefs would be reverted by that Save. One switch, one save path. On web the card is visible but disabled, with the same message the AI tab shows there.
- `settings/integrations_panel.dart` — `IntegrationsPanel` (#1158): the Integraties tab. It reads `availableIntegrationsProvider` and draws one switch-card per available integration (`_IntegrationCard`), each with its own on/off switch, the system's logo and a subtitle, plus the "alles aan/uit" control (`_BulkControls`: *Alles inschakelen* / *Alles uitschakelen*, each greyed out when there is nothing left to do). A card shows the integration's own settings body once the switch is on or a folder is already set; with the switch off while content exists it shows a line naming the fixed project rule — the connection stays reachable so an existing deck can be updated. A standalone widget rather than a `part` (#631): the dialog class sits against its size ceiling and this panel only reads the register.
- `settings/openkat_integration_panel.dart` — `OpenKatIntegrationBody` (#767, #772, #1158, live 2026-08-05): the OpenKAT card's body on the Integraties tab — two blocks under one hierarchy: **Vanuit een map** (report folder + **Rapportages controleren…**) and **Vanuit een OpenKAT-server** (`OpenKatInstallationsSection`). *(Renamed 2026-08-03 from `OpenKatIntegrationPanel`: since #1158 the switch, logo and heading come from `IntegrationsPanel`.)* On web the body is a single disabled line (desktop-only). Folder import starts the same scenario wizard as the menu and welcome screen. Standalone widget rather than a `part` of the settings dialog.
- `settings/openkat_installations_section.dart` — `OpenKatInstallationsSection`: server block on Integraties — installation cards (test/edit/remove), **Server toevoegen…** and **Rapportage van server…**. Empty state with one CTA; no global active-server switch.
- `settings/import_module_card.dart` — `ImportModuleCard` (#772, B1): the *Importeren* module card. *(Updated 2026-08-03, #1158: OpenKAT moved out to its own integration, so this module now covers only presentation import — .pptx/.key/.odp.)* The subtitle names that source and points on to Integraties for connections such as OpenKAT. The presentation import runs on the picked bytes, so the switch works on every platform including web, and `importerContentProviders` is empty because it leaves nothing persistent behind.
- `settings/procesverbetering_module_card.dart` — `ProcesverbeteringModuleCard` (PROCESS_IMPROVEMENT.md Phase 0/8): the *Procesverbetering* module card. Switch writes straight to `procesverbeteringProvider`. When on, links to Gage R&R, hypothesis-test and regression dialogs (Phase 8). Its subtitle names concrete methods (SIPOC, DMAIC, Kaizen and A3) and explains that enabling the extension exposes their templates and slide layouts.
- `settings/collaboration_module_card.dart` — `CollaborationModuleCard` (SELF_ENCRYPTED_RELAY.md §6): the *Realtime samenwerken* module card. Off by default; the master switch writes to `collaborationProvider`. When on, a per-transport toggle appears beneath it — today just **Matrix** (default on), with Jitsi/XMPP to follow. The account itself is set on the Samenwerken tab.
- `improvement_msa_dialog.dart` — read-only Gage R&R calculator over `improvement_analysis_helpers.dart` and the stats engine; opened from the module card and from the chart editor (Phase 8).
- `improvement_inference_dialog.dart` — read-only hypothesis-test calculator (Phase 8); opened from the module card.
- `improvement_regression_dialog.dart` — read-only simple-linear-regression calculator (Phase 8); opened from the module card.
- `settings/managementsysteem_module_card.dart` — `ManagementsysteemModuleCard` (ISO_MANAGEMENTSYSTEEM.md §5): the *Managementsysteem* module card on the Uitbreidingen tab. Off by default; the switch writes straight to `managementsysteemProvider`, revealing the `controlStatus` slide type and its picker tab. When on, a line reports how much of the bundled ISO index is on the device (control count across the three standards) and states that only the numbers and short titles ship, not the standard text.
- `settings/online_storage_module_card.dart` — `OnlineStorageModuleCard` (#570): the *Online opslag* module card. Unlike the AI card its switch writes straight to `onlineStorageProvider`, like the information-security card beside it — there is no form that would revert it on Save, and enabling a module is an action in its own right. The subtitle names the maturity honestly (part of the remote paths has mostly seen test environments), and when the module is off while online connections exist, a line says what off actually means: existing connections and queued work keep working, only *adding* new online connections stops.
- `settings/settings_section_title.dart` — `SettingsSectionTitle` plus the `SettingsSectionAnchors` inherited widget that carries the search anchors and the highlighted section, so a section heading needs no access to the dialog's state.
- `settings/settings_text_field.dart` — `SettingsTextField` (the shared input row) and `SettingsSecretField`, which on web disables itself and says why beside the field: a browser has no keychain, so anything stored there is readable by any script on the page.
- `settings/set_aside_findings.dart` — `SetAsidePrivacyFindings`: the privacy findings the author reviewed and left in place, as chips that put one back (#651). The counterpart of *This one was reviewed and can stay* in the quality panel, the way `DisabledPrivacyRules` is the counterpart of *Never report this rule again* — and the design (FILE_FORMAT §6.7) puts it sharply: a set-aside you cannot find again is a deletion. A chip carries the rule and where you judged it, never the matched value: that value is not in the sidecar and does not belong in a settings screen. Putting one back writes a tombstone rather than dropping the entry, because a dropped set-aside returns from the other side on the next merge and the finding is hidden again with nobody having chosen that. `stillHidden` and `setAsideChipLabel` are top-level and tested directly.
- `settings/disabled_privacy_rules.dart` — `DisabledPrivacyRules`: the detection rules currently switched off, as chips that switch one back on. The counterpart of the *Never report this rule again* button; without it, switching off is a one-way street and nobody dares use it. Three of the heaviest art. 9 categories are off by default — not because they matter less, but because their keywords are too common on ordinary business slides. Lifted out of the settings dialog's own state alongside its twin: neither reads the settings dialog, both read a provider, and the class was at its ceiling.
- `settings/confirm_certificate.dart` — The `ConfirmCertificate` typedef the three network panels use to reach the trust dialog. Its own file because peeking at a certificate leans on `dart:io`, which a panel must not drag into the web bundle.
- `settings/keychain_secret.dart` — `KeychainSecret`: the one piece of secret bookkeeping the four sources genuinely share — whether this secret must be written, given the async keychain read and a possibly changed identity.
- `settings/webdav_form.dart` — Editing state of a WebDAV connection (controllers, test result, password), owned by the dialog and handed to `WebdavPanel`.
- `settings/s3_form.dart` — Idem for an S3 bucket: endpoint, region, addressing style and the secret access key.
- `settings/git_form.dart` — Idem for a git repository: forge, owner, repo, branch and the token.
- `settings/matrix_form.dart` — `MatrixForm`: editing state of the app-global Matrix collaboration account (homeserver, user id, device id, the SSRF posture) plus the access token via `KeychainSecret`. Keychain identity is `homeserver|user-id`, matching `SettingsNotifier.setMatrixToken`. One account, not a list (app-global, §8).
- `settings/ai_form.dart` — Idem for the AI backend: mode, base URL, model, the explicit cloud confirmation, and the API key.
- `settings/libreplan_module_card.dart` — `LibreplanModuleCard`: the module card for the LibrePlan-connector on the Uitbreidingen tab. Desktop-only; on web it shows a disabled notice.
- `libreplan_import_dialog.dart` — `LibreplanImportDialog`: the import dialog with per-slide-type checkboxes and a progress indicator. Inserts the imported slides into the current deck via `DeckNotifier.insertSlides`.
- `settings/webdav_panel.dart` — `WebdavPanel`: the WebDAV editing panel, including the offer to split a pasted Nextcloud DAV URL instead of silently dropping the subfolder it carries.
- `settings/s3_panel.dart` — `S3Panel`: the S3 editing panel, including the addressing choice and the connection test.
- `settings/git_panel.dart` — `GitPanel`: the git editing panel, the token-scope help per forge, and the native-git detection line.
- `settings/matrix_panel.dart` — `MatrixPanel`: the Matrix account editing panel. The setup flow is deliberately password-free (2026-08-01): the user pastes a homeserver and an access token, and "Verbinding testen" confirms it via `whoami` and fills the user id and — importantly — the device id, because a wrong device id silently drops a co-author's key-share (SELF_ENCRYPTED_RELAY.md §4.3). The test client is injectable for widget tests.
- `settings/appearance_legibility.dart` — `AppearancePreview` and `AppearanceLegibility`: the miniature of the app in the profile being edited, and the contrast measurement under it (#750). The preview builds the profile's real `ThemeData` and renders inside it — it used to paint its own colours through a black-or-white-by-luminance helper, which showed a legible title bar over a profile the app renders illegibly, flattering exactly the profile that deserved a warning. It also shows a checkbox, a switch and a text button: the roles that broke in #744 and that the old preview left out. Both are plain widgets rather than methods on `_SettingsDialogState`, because they are pure functions of the profile — and because the class-size ratchet is the right kind of pressure here.
- `slide_finder_dialog.dart` — Stay-open searcher for gathering slides from many presentations.
- `slide_quality_details_dialog.dart` — Issues grouped by severity with counts and navigation.
- `storage_connection_picker.dart` — Asks which file connection an action works with, for WebDAV, S3 and git alike. Shows nothing at all when there is exactly one usable connection of that kind, so the single-server case keeps the flow it always had; the question only appears once it is a real question. Deck-bound actions never reach it — they follow the origin (see `AppSettings.gitConnectionFor`).
- `s3_browser_dialog.dart` — Browses an S3 bucket to pick a deck or images, on the connection it is given rather than one it looks up itself. S3 has no folders; the common prefixes a delimited listing returns arrive as `S3Entry.isCollection`, so this screen needs to know nothing about prefixes.
- `webdav_browser_dialog.dart` — Browses WebDAV folders to pick a deck or images, on the connection it is given rather than one it looks up itself.
- `git_browser_dialog.dart` — Picks a deck from a configured git repository and hands back the deck folder (`decks/<name>`). The fetching and opening stay with the caller, so the security gate and the error wording live in one place, exactly as with `webdav_browser_dialog.dart`. Takes the connection it is given: the user already chose, and that choice may not quietly become another one here.
- `save_destination_dialog.dart` — `SaveDestinationDialog`/`SaveDestinationChoice`: pick a library (or another folder) for a new presentation and see exactly where the deck, images and media will land *before* the system save panel opens. Returns the chosen folder as that panel's starting directory; `null` means cancelled, which is not the same as "start somewhere default".
- `export_failure_text.dart` — `ExportStage` plus `exportFailureText`: which of the three export steps fell over (preparing / rendering the slides / building and writing the file), what the user can do about it, and only then the raw exception. All three throw from one `try`, so without the stage the dialog could only show the exception — and `Invalid argument(s): 1` (#714) points nowhere. Pure text choice, so it lives outside the dialog and is tested without opening one.
- `export_progress_text.dart` — `exportProgressText`: the progress line while an export runs (loading images / preparing / rendering / done). Same reason to live outside the dialog as `export_failure_text.dart` — pure text choice, no state — but here that is not a matter of taste. This *was* a private method on the dialog state, and that is precisely why #714 could hide in it: every export test calls the rasterizer without a progress callback, so nothing ever ran it. Note that `total` means images during `precache` and slides afterwards, and that it is legitimately **zero** for a deck without a single image — the old `(done + 1).clamp(1, total)` threw `ArgumentError(1)` on exactly that, which the user saw as "Invalid argument(s): 1".
- `finalize_seal_dialog.dart` — "Afronden & verzegelen": explains the one-way finalise plus the SHA-512 seal, and collects the optional visual signature (typed or drawn). Returns a `FinalizeSealResult`; cancelling returns null.
- `certificate_trust_dialog.dart` — Asks whether the user trusts *this* certificate, and shows what he can base that on. The case is a self-hosted server without a recognised issuer: refusing shuts that whole population out, accepting anything self-signed throws the security away — so it is this one certificate, recognised by its fingerprint, printed in full so it can be compared with what the server itself shows.
- `proxy_fallback_dialog.dart` — Asks whether a web import may send the whole URL to the app's own fetch helper. That helper fetches server-side and thereby holds the full URL — a different party than the one the user pointed at, and the URL itself can be the secret (a share link carries its key in the address). The fallback used to run automatically and silently; now whoever does not answer sends nothing.
- `ai_image_outbound_dialog.dart` — The confirmation before an image goes to an AI model. Text is redacted by `PrivacyProjection.forExternalProcessing` first; an image cannot be — the projection replaces characters and a JPEG has none, and the face detection demonstrably misses (from behind, in profile, masked, in HEIC). What is left is not stricter redaction but honesty: saying *what* leaves and *where to*, before it leaves. `docs/design/OCIWACHT.md` §6.1 calls this channel "no projection" and names this dialog as the safeguard standing in its place.
- `duplicate_cleanup_dialog.dart` — `DuplicateCleanupDialog`/`CleanupGroup`: one set of byte-identical copies of the same presentation, with the removal going through `TrashService` rather than a hard delete.
- `slide_diff_dialog.dart` — `SlideDiffRef` + the dialog that puts two versions of a slide side by side: each side rendered, with its project base path and its deck's theme, above a per-field list of what differs.

### `lib/widgets/editors/` — per-slide-type editors

- `_editor_field.dart` — Shared layout helpers for slide editors.
- `embed_editor_dialog.dart` — Shared dialog shell (`showEmbedEditorDialog`) for editing an embedded chart or table: the full editor in a viewport-clamped window with Cancel/Apply. Used by both the document source-preview (double-click embeds) and the visual editor's table embed, so both edit routes share one shell.
- `ai_suggest_control.dart` — Shared "suggest text (AI)" control: confirmation, progress, error feedback and the AI-concept/Nagekeken provenance chrome.
- `ai_suggest_field.dart` — Connects that shared control to finding free-text fields and their finding-specific AI service.
- `improvement_ai_suggest_field.dart` — Connects the shared control to Procesverbetering canvas/tree/flow fields; gated on both AI and Procesverbetering modules (`improvement_ai_provider.dart`).
- `alt_text_field.dart` — Per-image alt-text field with the optional "suggest alt-text (AI)" button and AI-draft badge.
- `audio_attachment_editor.dart` — Edits a slide's audio file attachment.
- `bullet_marker_selector.dart` — Per-slide bullet-marker override (dot or paw).
- `bullets_editor.dart` — Edits a bullet-list slide (title, subtitle, nested levels, markers, group headings/"tussenkoppen").
- `bullets_image_editor.dart` — Edits a bullets-with-image slide.
- `callout_editor.dart` — Dialog for editing image callouts on a bulletsImage slide: assign a reference letter to a bullet, click on the image to place a target, edit the description, delete (IMAGE_CALLOUTS.md §6).
- `callout_marker_helpers.dart` — Pure widget builders and value types extracted from `callout_editor.dart` to keep it under the 1000-line ceiling: the clipped-target warning badge (#1853), the drag-preview rectangle, the `Handle` enum and `DragRegion` value type.
- `chart_editor.dart` — Edits a chart slide (type, data grid, CSV import/linking). The grid is editable whether or not the data is linked to a file — a linked chart writes that file back on save. It was read-only while linked until that write-back existed.
- `chart_type_toolbar.dart` — Chart type dropdown plus Gage R&R / DOE / paste / CSV actions, kept outside `_ChartEditorState` for the class-size ratchet.
- `chart_histogram_limits.dart` — Y-01 deck-limit switch and local USL/LSL/process-target fields for histogram charts.
- `chart_doe_design_dialog.dart` — Dialog that builds a Yates DOE design grid into the chart editor.
- `checklist_editor.dart` — Edits a checklist slide (standard label, tri-state test rows, finding links).
- `cockpit_editor.dart` — Edits a cockpit slide: title, up to six meter specs,
  presenter-entry animation and its optional per-slide duration.
- `code_editor.dart` — Edits a code slide (syntax-highlighted monospace field).
- `editor_text_controller.dart` — `EditorTextController`: een tekstveldcontroller die zijn bewerkingsluisteraars (`addTextListener`) alleen wekt bij een échte tekstwijziging. Een gewone `TextEditingController` meldt ook een cursorwissel, en een editor die dat als bewerking leest maakt de presentatie 'gewijzigd' van één klik — waarna de niet-opgeslagen-stip meteen ná het opslaan weer opkomt. `addListener` blijft ongefilterd, want het tekstveld zelf moet de cursor wél zien bewegen.
- `finding_editor.dart` — Edits a finding header (scope, CVSS vector, CWE picker, CVE, sections; template + CWE pickers).
- `findings_summary_editor.dart` — Edits the findings-summary counts (with "refresh from deck").
- `free_markdown_editor.dart` — Edits a free-form custom-markdown slide.
- `image_slide_editor.dart` — Edits a full-slide image (title, caption).
- `list_style_selector.dart` — Selects list style (bullets/numbered/checklist/rich text).
- `markdown_command_palette.dart` — Searchable, keyboard-first insert/format command picker for Markdown source editing.
- `menu_editor.dart` — Edits a choice-menu slide (#1162): the slide's layout (*Raster* / *Onder elkaar* / *In een cirkel*, as a chip row at the top) and, per block, a label, a one-line description, a target slide, an optional image and — once the menu has more than one category — which category the block sits in. The target is picked by slide title from a dropdown (never a typed anchor) — the stable anchor underneath is managed by the app, and choosing a target runs through the deck notifier because it also freezes the target slide's anchor. Everything else touches only this slide (via `onUpdate`); blocks and categories are read and written through `menu_blocks.dart`, so the on-disk encoding lives in one place. The text controllers are held per block in one flat list that runs straight through the categories, and they are moved with the block on insert/delete/move so a cursor never lands in another block's field; dissolving a category moves its blocks into the neighbouring one rather than deleting them.
- `menu_editor_block.dart` — `part of menu_editor.dart`: the row of one block (label, description, target, image, category). Stateless — every change is a callback to the editor, which is the only place that writes the slide's bullets.
- `markdown_deck_editor.dart` — Syntax-aware, lossless Markdown source editor with live validation/quick fixes, highlighted find/replace, outline navigation, writing and speaking metrics, native spell suggestions, undo/redo, draft diff/recovery status, formatting tools and linked source↔slide selection. Its sliding scope toggle switches whole-deck vs. single-slide Markdown but refuses to reinterpret an unapplied draft under another scope.
- `markdown_editor_field.dart` — Quiet form-sized Markdown field with an **Bewerken** action that expands into the full word-processor. Deliberately plain: no floating toolbar over the text; formatting lives in the expand editor.
- `expanded_markdown_dialog.dart` — Full-window word-processor for one Markdown field. Opens in the visual (WYSIWYG) editor by default, with a switch to raw Markdown (remembered locally); content that cannot round-trip losslessly (tables, footnotes, front-matter, HTML comments) opens in source mode via `markdownNeedsSourceMode`. No separate preview pane — the visual editor is the view.
- `markdown_find_bar.dart` — In-editor find/replace bar for markdown mode.
- `find_replace_session.dart` — The find/replace *state* behind that bar — query, matches, current index, replace-current/replace-all — shared by the document editor and the deck source editor, which each held their own identical copy until they drifted apart in size and behaviour. Knows the editor only through its `MarkdownSourceController`; where the caret should go is the host's call (a source selection, or a signal into the rich-text layer), so the session stays a plain class and keeps `setState` with the widget that owns it. Replacements transform the existing selection through the changed ranges instead of assigning through `controller.text`, which would discard the caret and scroll the editor to the top.
- `markdown_source_controller.dart` — Source-preserving syntax/search/delimiter painter for the deck editor; caches lexical tokens and retains IME composing underlines.
- `markdown_smart_input_formatter.dart` — Direct-typing conveniences: continue lists/quotes and pair brackets, parentheses and backticks without rewriting pasted source.
- `question_editor.dart` — Edits a question slide (kind, answers, options, timing). The kind decides which fields appear; *how many options are shown* is offered only for the two kinds that draw from a pool (multiple-choice and ordering) instead of sitting there doing nothing.
- `question_editor_kinds.dart` — `part of question_editor.dart`: the two kinds that have no plain answer list. The image pair (two picker slots, a captions-are-not-"left"/"right" warning and a segmented control for which one is right) and the typed answer (accepted answers plus the similarity slider, shown as a percentage because "0.85 Jaro-Winkler" tells an author nothing). Extensions cannot call the protected `setState`, so both go through the main state's `_rebuild(fn)`.
- `quote_editor.dart` — Edits a quote slide (text, author, background image).
- `scope_matrix_editor.dart` — Edits a scope-matrix slide (objects × type/standard × coverage status).
- `control_status_editor.dart` — Edits a `controlStatus` slide (Managementsysteem module): a section heading plus per-control rows (id, control, status, optional maturity 0–5, owner, target, evidence, note). A one-click *Beheersmaatregelen laden…* appends a chosen ISO standard's index (optionally one section) from `ManagementSystemCatalog`, adding only ids not already present; *Genereer voortgangsoverzicht* triggers the deck-wide roll-up.
- `matrix_editor.dart` — Edits a Procesverbetering `matrix` slide: template picker, clipboard paste, derived RPN column shown read-only.
- `canvas_editor.dart` — Edits a Procesverbetering `canvas` slide: template picker and one Markdown field per `##` region.
- `tree_editor.dart` — Edits a Procesverbetering `tree` slide: template picker, tree/fishbone layout toggle, tab-indented bullet list.
- `flow_editor.dart` — Edits a Procesverbetering `flow` slide: template picker and step list with `::` attributes (process map / swimlane / VSM).
- `discoveries_editor.dart` — Edits a discoveries slide (up to six finds, four fields each), with a banner that restates the headline the slide will lead with as you type.
- `asset_overview_editor.dart` — Edits an asset-overview slide (up to eight kinds, four counts each), summing as you type and flagging a subtotal larger than its total.
- `scorecard_editor.dart` — Edits a scorecard slide (up to five figures: label, now, previous report, unit, direction). One figure is one compact two-row card carrying a live change chip; ordering is a drag handle, like every other reorderable list in the app.
- `section_editor.dart` — Edits a section-divider slide (title, subtitle).
- `signoff_editor.dart` — Edits the sign-off slide (truthfulness statement, signature, certification, seal).
- `slide_type_help.dart` — Collapsible "what can I do here?" hint per slide type (and the TLP hint); exhaustive switch guarantees every type has one.
- `table_editor.dart` — Edits a table slide (grid of cells, header row).
- `timeline_editor.dart` — Edits a timeline slide (reorderable events, layout).
