- `title_editor.dart` — Edits a title slide (title, subtitle, image, zoom).
- `two_bullets_editor.dart` — Edits a two-column bullet slide (per-column titles).
- `two_images_editor.dart` — Edits a two-image slide.
- `video_slide_editor.dart` — Edits a video slide (source, trim points, audio).
- `slide_editor_registry.dart` — `SlideEditorContext` + `slideEditorBuilders`: the `SlideType` → editor map, so `editor_panel.dart` routes through one table instead of a growing `switch`. A new slide type is registered here (see `docs/design/`'s slide-type chain).
- `advanced_section.dart` — `AdvancedSection`: the collapsible block for advanced options (progressive disclosure) — basics stay visible, the rarely-needed sits behind one quiet header. Deliberately a lightweight widget of its own instead of `ExpansionTile`, whose dividers and Material margins are out of place in the compact editors. Starts expanded when the option already has a non-default value, because hiding it then is the confusing choice.
- `animation_duration_control.dart` — The per-slide activation-duration slider shared by the animated editors (cockpit, chart). While the slide inherits the theme's duration the read-out is muted and the reset button disabled; dragging starts a per-slide override.
- `split_continuation_switch.dart` — `SplitContinuationSwitch`: makes `Slide.continuesSplit` visible and adjustable. That flag arose as a by-product of *Split slide* and could afterwards only be found in the Markdown — while it decides how large the text is drawn, since pages of one run share the fullest page's size. The subtitle names that consequence on purpose: the label sounds like an administrative tick box, and coupling your font size to the neighbour's is the reason you would switch it off.

### `lib/widgets/markdown_editor/` — notes editor

- `markdown_editor.dart` — Notes editor with side-by-side WYSIWYG and raw-markdown modes. `insertSignal`/`insertMarkdownBlock` laten de ouder een blok op de *Quill*-cursor invoegen: in de visuele stand leeft de tekst in het Quill-document en staat de bron-cursor stil, waardoor een invoeging via de bron onderaan het document belandde.
- `markdown_editor_actions.dart` — Selection-wrapping and line-prefix helpers.
- `markdown_editor_theme.dart` — `MarkdownEditorTheme`: readable editor chrome (notes toolbar + field) independent of the surrounding slide/panel colours. The `editorPanel` factory's surface follows the theme (`AppTheme.paper`), not a hardcoded white — it was white, and since the text arrives mode-aware (light in dark mode) that made the notes field a white bar with near-invisible light text in dark mode: 1.1:1 for text, 1.4:1 for the toolbar icons (#821). The sister factory `presenterOverlay` already picked its surface by luminance; this one didn't. Measured by `test/markdown_editor_theme_contrast_test.dart`.
- `markdown_editor_toolbar.dart` — Formatting toolbar for the markdown editor.
- `notes_editor_mode.dart` — Enum: rendered vs raw editing surface.
- `notes_mode_toggle.dart` — Toggle between visual and markdown editing.
- `wysiwyg_notes_field.dart` — WYSIWYG rich-text field (Flutter Quill). `defaultStylesFor` (`@visibleForTesting`) builds the Quill `DefaultStyles` from the `MarkdownEditorTheme` so every block honours the app theme. Quill *merges* these over its own ambient `DefaultStyles.getInstance(context)`, so any block type left unset inherits the surrounding `DefaultTextStyle` — a brand/link colour on the document surface in light (EU-blue under Europa). Every rendered block must therefore be pinned here; a list needs **two** fields — `lists.style` for the item text and `leading.style` for the marker (bullet dot / number, which the leading builder colours from `leading.style.color`) — both set to the body colour so items and markers match a paragraph.
- `wysiwyg_notes_toolbar.dart` — Quill formatting toolbar.
- `footnote_embed_builder.dart` — Tekent de twee voetnoot-embeds in de visuele editor: de verwijzing als volgnummer in superscript, de definitie als invulbare notenregel die byte-getrouw terugschrijft. `footnoteNumbersInDocument` nummert door op de volgorde in het Quill-document zelf — tijdens het typen is dát de waarheid, en een nummer dat achterloopt op wat je net invoegde leest als een fout.
- `pentest_block_embed_builder.dart` — Tekent een `x-embed-pentest-block` in de visuele editor als de gerenderde vorm van zijn eigen bron, via dezelfde `DocumentMarkdownView` die de lezer gebruikt — geen tweede renderpad. Zonder deze builder viel het hele document terug op brontekst zodra er één envelop in stond (een marker is rauwe HTML), de val waar de inhoudsopgave en de voetnoot eerder in liepen. Een rijke bevindingkaart met bewerkknop hoort bij de fase die de editors aan de enveloppen koppelt; tot die tijd is "zichtbaar en onaantastbaar" beter dan "onzichtbaar".
- `image_embed_builder.dart` — `ImageEmbedBuilder` tekent een `x-embed-image` als merkteken met de alt-tekst (of de bestandsnaam) op de plek in de zin — bewust niet de afbeelding zelf: het uitzoeken van een `mem:`/`asset:`/deck-relatief pad hoort bij `ImageService` met de deckmap erbij, en de documentlezer tekent afbeeldingen vandaag ook niet. `FallbackEmbedBuilder` is het vangnet eronder (`unknownEmbedBuilder`): een embed-type zonder bouwer wordt een zichtbaar rafeltje in plaats van een foutscherm in plaats van het document.
- `toc_embed_builder.dart` — Tekent een `x-embed-toc`-blok in de visuele editor als dezelfde inhoudsopgave-voorbeeldweergave die de documentlezer toont. De koppen komen uit het Quill-document zelf (alleen de kopregels — deze builder draait bij elke toetsaanslag), niet uit de embed: de marker draagt geen inhoud.
- `mermaid_embed_builder.dart` — Tekent een `x-embed-mermaid`-blok in de visuele editor als het gerenderde diagram, via dezelfde `DocumentMarkdownView` die de lezer gebruikt — geen tweede renderpad, dus ook dezelfde terugval op een codeblok wanneer er geen WebView is om mee te tekenen. Sluit het gat tussen de belofte in `DOCUMENT_MODE.md` §4.3 en de editor, die als enige weergave de fence als brontekst liet staan (#1920). De kaart is alleen-lezen; bewerken gaat via de Bron-modus, net als bij de andere atomaire blokken.
- `table_embed_builder.dart` — Renders a GFM table (an `x-embed-table` block embed) inside the visual editor as a real, editable table, writes in-place or full-editor changes back through the atomic embed and owns the timeline-activation preview. Its stable offset key and selection-preserving write-back keep the same cell controller, focus and document position while the atomic embed is replaced after a keystroke. This is why a table is no longer a `markdownVisualLimitations` fall-back to raw source.
- `table_sort_actions.dart` — The shared visual sort workflow for ordinary tables and timeline tables: automatic or explicit kind/direction, friendly attention-point confirmation and literal row-value review. It returns the sorted GFM source; each embed remains responsible for one discrete write/undo step.
- `timeline_table_embed_builder.dart` — Renders the atomic document-timeline embed in Visual, shares the in-place row editor and generic sort actions, leaves a malformed marked table visually editable with its diagnosis, and removes only the marker when the author switches back to a table.
- `embedded_field_actions.dart` — `EmbeddedFieldActions`: gives a text field that lives *inside* a Quill embed (a table cell, a footnote line) its own text editing back. An `EditableText` deliberately lets its editing actions be overridden by an `Actions` higher up the tree (`Action.overridable`), and Quill puts those there — so one arrow key in a cell had *Quill* move the document's caret and then write that result into the cell: the whole document text landed in one cell with `<br>` separators, and because those are raw HTML the visual mode silently fell back to source text (#1565). This wrapper registers the same actions closer to the field; each one calls the field's own default action (`Action.callingAction`), so Quill never gets a say over a nested field.
- `divider_embed_builder.dart` — Renders a Markdown thematic break (`---`), which `MarkdownQuillCodec` turns into a `BlockEmbed('divider')`, as a horizontal rule in the visual (Quill) editor. Registered in `wysiwyg_notes_field.dart`'s `embedBuilders` beside `TableEmbedBuilder`. Without a builder for the `divider` embed Quill draws a `RenderErrorBox`, so any document containing a `---` — most visibly an inserted **page break** — would break the WYSIWYG surface; this keeps it intact, the rule reading as the page break the export turns into a new sheet (DOCUMENT_MODE.md §13).

### `lib/widgets/slides/` — slide rendering

- `chart_hover.dart` — `ChartHover` (a series/category index pair), `ChartHoverController` (a `ChangeNotifier` bus holding a `local` and an `external` hover) and the inherited `ChartHoverScope`. The presenter's current-slide canvas and the beamer window each own a controller; a method-channel message copies one window's `local` hover into the other's `external`, so hovering a chart on one screen highlights the same series/slice/point on the other (#930-style, the chart twin of `MermaidViewController`). Applying `external` never touches `local`, so the two windows can't echo each other.
- `image_crop_dialog.dart` — Interactive crop/reposition dialog: drag to set the focal point (and zoom for full-slide/title images), WYSIWYG in the slot's aspect ratio.
- `image_zoom_dialog.dart` — Full-screen pan/zoom image viewer.
- `inline_markdown.dart` — `InlineMarkdownText`: renders inline markdown with tappable links (and disposes their recognizers). The parsing and styling live in `lib/utils/inline_markdown.dart`.
- `mermaid_diagram.dart` — Renders Mermaid definitions to inline SVG in previews. Everything the renderer returns goes through `sanitizeMermaidSvg` first; markup that is not SVG falls back to showing the source rather than an empty box. The renderer itself hangs on an optional `renderer` seam (default: the shared `MermaidRenderService`), in the same shape as `FileService.saveDestination` — the real one runs on a hidden WebView.
- `slide_preview.dart` — Central preview library coordinating all slide-type renderers + shared helpers, including inherited Marp colours/backgrounds, local header/footer Markdown, heading fit, offline emoji and bitmap filters; the same widget feeds preview, presenter and PDF/PPTX rasterization. `sharedSplitFitScale` reads the prepared `SplitRunLayoutIndex`; `splitRunMemberScale` computes an individual run member's fit. The quality analyzer uses the same member measurement, so a reported size is the size actually rendered.
- `slide_thumbnail.dart` — Thumbnail with slide preview, metadata, and action buttons. Carries the two finding badges (quality left, privacy right): click to read, double-click to accept or undo. Top-left it flags the two ways a slide can fail to reach the audience: *Overgeslagen* and *Achtergehouden* (`slideWithheldByTlp`), each dimming the preview, each in its own colour, stacked rather than side by side because a slide can be both — and the withheld one names its level in a tooltip, since knowing *that* it drops out does not tell you which control to change. Both states also go into the semantics label, so a screen-reader user hears them.
- `slide_badge_tone.dart` — `SlideBadgeTone` plus the two threshold functions. Grey (`accepted`) is a state of its own, not `none`: a slide where you accepted something must not look like a slide where nothing was found.
- `slide_badge_popover.dart` — The anchored list behind a badge, and `toggleSlideBadgeAcceptance`. Reads the raw results so a grey badge still opens a readable list; refuses to undo `redact`/`shield`, which would put redacted data back into an export.
- `video_playhead_bus.dart` — Cross-widget channel syncing the video playhead across previews.

### `lib/widgets/slides/previews/` (each `part of slide_preview.dart`)

- `slide_preview_support.dart` — Standalone shared Marp image-filter and link-scope widgets used by preview, presenter and raster export, extracted to keep the central preview library within its size ratchet.
- `callout_overlay.dart` — Standalone callout overlay widget: paints numbered markers on top of the image slot using `ImageViewportGeometry` to map targets from image space to slot pixels (IMAGE_CALLOUTS.md §4.1). Imported by `slide_preview.dart` and used by the bulletsImage preview.

- `preview_scaffold.dart` — Also home to `_logoSafeInsets`/`_logoAwareBottomPadding`, the logo-safe insets every logo-avoiding preview uses (moved out of `slide_preview.dart`, which sits at its line ceiling). `_PreviewScaffold`: the outer skeleton the report-style previews share — a filled area, a scale-down `FittedBox` over a fixed slide width, the logo-aware margin, and the column under it. Checklist, scope matrix, findings summary, sign-off, finding, free markdown, asset overview and discoveries use it. Chart, cockpit, table and scorecard deliberately do not: they compute their own aspect ratio, drop the `SizedBox.expand`, or give the box a fixed height, and bending the scaffold to cover them would turn it into a widget with ten switches.
- `bullets_previews.dart` — Bullet-point slide layout.
- `chart_preview.dart` — Chart slide rendering + dispatch, legend, hover, and screen-reader text for every chart type. A chart tooltip's box is `AppTheme.chartTooltipBg` (a fixed dark navy), not the mode-dependent `ink`: white on `ink` was 1.18:1 in dark mode because `ink` there is near-white. A tooltip belongs to the slide, so it doesn't move with the theme (#822).
- `chart_preview_extra.dart` — Hand-drawn builders for the horizontal-bar, horizontal-stacked-bar, combo, waterfall, and heatmap chart types.
- `chart_preview_improvement.dart` — The painters for the six Procesverbetering chart types (PROCESS_IMPROVEMENT.md Phase 2/8): control chart, histogram, Pareto, run chart, box plot and probability plot.
- `scene_painter.dart` — `ScenePainter`/`ScenePreview`: the canvas backend for `services/scene/scene.dart`, the twin of its `sceneToSvg`. Draws what an engine already positioned and decides nothing itself; `SceneImageRef` is a placeholder frame, because decoding an image belongs to the host and not to a painter. Reached from `matrix_preview.dart` and `canvas_preview.dart` via the matrix and canvas engines.
- `matrix_preview.dart` — Builds a `Scene` with `buildMatrixScene` and paints it; derived columns and RPN sort live in the engine, not here.
- `canvas_preview.dart` — Builds a `Scene` with `buildCanvasScene` and paints it; region layout lives in the engine, not here.
- `checklist_previews.dart` — Checklist slides with a progress bar; `_ChecklistBulletRow` and the `_GroupHeadingRow` (group-heading/divider) row widget shared by the bullet previews.
- `control_status_preview.dart` — `controlStatus` slides (Managementsysteem module): the section heading with a derived *implemented / applicable* progress bar, then the controls as an id × control × status × maturity table. Colours reuse the deterministic `AppTheme` status tokens so it renders identically in an export isolate; content comes from `ControlStatusSpec.fromSlide`.
- `gantt_preview.dart` — `gantt` slides (Procesverbetering module): leidt Mermaid gantt-DSL af uit de tabelrijen en rendert die via de bestaande `MermaidDiagram`-widget. De DSL wordt nooit opgeslagen.
- `cockpit_preview.dart` — Animated cockpit/gauge dashboard slides: the
  `_CockpitPreview` state (boot animation, per-slide duration override), the
  `_CockpitGrid` that places the cells from `CockpitGridPlan`, and the
  `_CockpitInstrument` cell — a painter for instrument and read-out window plus
  a `Text` label in the label strip, already run through the character-count
  cascade (`CockpitCellPlan.fitLabel`) so it can never fall over the bezel.
  The authentic branch staggers instruments and drives lamp test,
  minimum→maximum→target needle motion and read-out roll; classic keeps the
  card frame and its simpler sweep.
- `cockpit_instrument_painter.dart` — `_CockpitInstrumentPainter`: the seven
  meter types on the dial (arc gauges, thermometer, climb/descent, horizon,
  heading), each drawn around the dial centre and side that `CockpitCellPlan`
  hands it, then the read-out window beside the dial with the lines from
  `cockpitReadoutLines`. Since the September 2026 redesign
  (`docs/design/COCKPIT_LAYOUT.md`) no free text is painted on the dial face —
  only the two scale numerals (now also on the thermometer and in classic; the
  climb dial shows "+max / 0 / min") — so a long unit can no longer run through
  the scale, the band ends or the bezel.
- `cockpit_painter_support.dart` — Material details shared by the instrument
  painter (bezel, screws on the instrument band, glass, lamps, hub, needle, the
  sweep functions, the read-out plate and read-out line), split out to keep the
  geometry file below its size ceiling. The heading read-out window of #1110
  became the read-out plate every instrument now uses.
- `code_preview.dart` — Syntax-highlighted code slides with fit.
- `media_previews.dart` — Shared audio/video playback lifecycle (`_MediaPlaybackHost`) + remote-media SSRF gate.
- `media_previews_image.dart` — Image rendering helpers, captions, and the placeholders. `ImagePlaceholderReason` keeps "no image chosen yet" visually distinct from "file missing", "outside the deck", "gone after reload", and `redacted` — the last one paints a fixed-black redaction block rather than the grey placeholder, and deliberately does not follow the slate palette (which inverts in dark mode). The shared blocked-online-media placeholder names *why* it is not playing via `remoteBlockedReasonFor` (`RemoteBlockedReason.web`/`settingOff`/`urlRejected`) and, for `settingOff`, shows an "enable in settings" button — but only when the editor preview supplied `onEnableOnlineMedia` through `_SlideLinkScope`; presenter, rail, export and play-only leave it null (#852).
- `media_previews_video.dart` — Video/audio slide rendering: local playback, embeds, and the shared media placeholder. Both embeds are a bare iframe built from `VideoSource.embedUri` — YouTube on `kYouTubeEmbedOrigin` (`youtube-nocookie.com`) with events over the player's own `postMessage` channel, Vimeo with `player.vimeo.com/api/player.js`. `_youTubePlayerNavigationAllowed` matches on the **host**, and refuses `www.youtube.com` so the player's "Watch on YouTube" link cannot navigate the slide onto the tracking origin.
- `overlays.dart` — Logo overlay and TLP-marking badge renderers. The PERSOONSGEGEVENS marking label uses the fixed brand yellow, not `warningFg`: on the black overlay the mode-dependent token wrote its light side (#92400E, 2.96:1) into the export while the dark-mode preview showed amber — one fixed colour makes preview and export agree (#822).
- `question_preview.dart` — Question slides with answer reveal: the option-list kinds, the shared card, the option colours (`_visualColors`, one table for both the text tile and the image tile so "correct" is the same green in both) and the author hint, which states per kind what the presentation randomises rather than always claiming "n of m options" — the kinds that draw from no pool have nothing to say there.
- `question_preview_answers.dart` — `part of slide_preview.dart`: the two kinds whose answers are not a list of text options. The image pair renders the two pictures side by side (a row, not a stack — "which of these two" only reads when they are next to each other); the typed answer renders an input field that is editable on the presenter side and read-only on the beamer, and swaps that field for the two-line correction (`_answerLine`, marked with a `TextDecoration` beside the colour) once the answer is in. The whole block shrinks to fit, because the reveal adds content exactly when the viewer is looking at it.
- `menu_preview.dart` — Choice-`menu` slides (#1162): the title, the category bar and the block area, in the theme's colours (background, text and accent from the `ThemeProfile`, never a palette of its own). The category bar (`_MenuCategoryBar`) is a row of pills, one filled; it appears only when `menuHasCategories` says so, and it is clickable only when the caller passes `onCategoryChanged` — the slide rail and the beamer window show it, the presenter and the editor preview steer it. The open category is kept locally as well as accepted from outside, so a tap shows immediately while the beamer still follows the presenter; switching slides resets it to the first. The block area is given the height that is left after the title and the bar on the real slide height, except for the list layout, which keeps its row height and lets the scaffold scale the slide down instead.
- `menu_preview_layouts.dart` — `part of slide_preview.dart`: the three block layouts and the card they share. `_menuBlockArea` is the one entry point, so preview, presenter, beamer, slide rail and the raster exports cannot draw a menu differently; the column ladder itself (`menuGridColumns`) sits in `menu_blocks.dart`, shared with the HTML export. The card puts the block image as a small square thumb beside the text (label above, description under it) with an arrow when the block jumps; the circle layout lays the blocks out as discs on a ring, sized and placed by the shared `menuDiscFraction`/`menuRingRadius`.
- `table_preview.dart` — Table slides with a cell-edit scope.
- `text_previews.dart` — Title and text-based slides. Code blocks and the GitHub-neutral fills (`ghSurface`/`ghInk`/`ghBorder`) are `const`, not theme-dependent: a slide is a fixed white canvas rendered identically in the preview and a headless export isolate, and the syntax theme beside them (`githubTheme`) is the light one. As getters they inverted the box in dark mode while the letters stayed light — 1.23:1, and diverging from the always-light export (#822).
- `timeline_preview.dart` — Animated timeline renderer with event cards.
- `discoveries_preview.dart` — The longest exposure as a headline, then one row per find: name over kind, an exposure bar on the shared scale, the figure in words and the owner (red when there is none).
- `asset_overview_preview.dart` — Per-kind bars on one shared scale with the at-risk share filled, four aligned count columns and a derived totals row.
- `scorecard_preview.dart` — Scorecard cards: label, figure, the signed change as a tinted pill and the figure it replaced. The number of figures decides the grid (one becomes a hero, four a 2×2, five three-above-two) and the card each figure gets decides its type size — `_CardMetrics` measures the label and hands the figure whatever is left. Card tint and rule follow the profile's accent; the green/red sentiment tokens stay fixed, because they carry meaning rather than styling.

### `lib/widgets/presentation/` — presenter & dual-screen

- `annotation_overlay.dart` — `AnnotationLayer` for interactive drawing/laser pointer on slides.
- `audience_window.dart` — `AudienceWindowApp`: fullscreen slide on the secondary (beamer) window. Its own engine, so it forwards every key it does not handle itself to the presenter over `presenterChannel` (modifiers included — the other side's `HardwareKeyboard` knows nothing of this window); without that bridge the whole shortcut set died the moment this window took the keyboard focus.
- `fullscreen_presenter.dart` — `FullscreenPresenter`: dual-screen presenter mode (notes/clock/grid). `F` fixes the quality problem on the slide on screen without interrupting (#914): `planLiveFix` (top-level, so it stays off the class ceiling) splits an overfull slide in place or cuts multi-sentence bullets apart, writing the split through to the source by slide id (`onSlideSplit` → `DeckNotifier.splitSlide`) and pushing the fresh deck to the beamer over a new `replaceDeck` channel; a redacted slide is left untouched.
- `display_info.dart` — Export selector: schermdetectie en vensterverhuizing voor de presentator (desktop-nativeapi of web-no-op).
- `display_info_data.dart` — `DisplayInfo`: platform-agnostische schermgegevens (positie + afmeting).
- `display_info_io.dart` — Desktop: schermdetectie en vensterverhuizing via nativeapi.
- `display_info_web.dart` — Web: no-op (geen schermdetectie in de browser).
- `audience_controls_bar.dart` — `AudienceSurface` wraps the audience view and reveals an `AudienceControlsBar` for three seconds on mouse movement: an arrow each way and a close button whose tooltip names `Esc`. It owns that timer itself, because the visibility is the only thing there is to steer and it concerns nothing else. Hidden by default on purpose — a projected image should not carry permanent buttons, they end up in every photo of the room — but before #607 the audience view had *no* controls at all, so a first-time presenter had to guess how to get out, in front of a room. Deliberately shows **no slide number** (#864): the count distracts on the projected image and ends up in every photo; it lives in the presenter cockpit (`_buildPresenterControls`) instead.
- `rehearsal_summary.dart` — Post-rehearsal timing summary dialog with a per-slide breakdown and, under it, one line per answered question attempt (time and right/wrong, repeat attempts numbered). The on-screen list and the clipboard copy read the same numbered attempts, so they cannot drift apart. Never shown for a `Deck.playOnly` deck — the gate for that sits in `FullscreenPresenter` itself, not only at the call site.
- `session_export.dart` — Afloop-flow voor session-data-edits na een presentatie (#1235): biedt de spreker aan om checklists/tabellen die tijdens het presenteren zijn ingevuld als losse `.md`-bestanden te bewaren en het deck schoon te houden (één undo-stap), of in het deck te behouden. De export gaat door `PrivacyProjection.forAudience` (audience-oppervlak) en toont een rood waarschuwingsschildje als de scan persoonsgegevens vindt. De live-fix #914 (`onSlideChanged`) is een deck-bewerking en wordt niet aangeboden.

### `lib/widgets/presentation/parts/` (each `part of fullscreen_presenter.dart`, an `extension _PresenterX` unless noted)

- `presenter_beamer_payload.dart` — `buildBeamerMarkdown`: the self-contained markdown handed to the audience window. A top-level function, not an extension. Everything the beamer cannot look up for itself travels inside this string — hence the inlined style profile *and* the inlined chart data (a chart's `source` is a projectmap-relative path the second screen cannot resolve).
- `presenter_displays.dart` — Multi-monitor screen management.
- `presenter_ink.dart` — Annotation layer stroking/erasing/laser.
- `presenter_keys.dart` — Keyboard input during presentation. `_handleKey` is a thin `KeyEvent` adapter over `_handleLogicalKey`, which takes the key and its modifiers as plain values so the beamer window can feed its forwarded keys through the same ladder.
- `presenter_navigation.dart` — Slide/page navigation.
- `presenter_notes.dart` — Speaker- and user-note management.
- `presenter_overlays.dart` — UI overlays (badges/help/grid/clock).
- `presenter_playback.dart` — Auto-advance and media playback.
- `presenter_questions.dart` — Question-slide logic: drawing a round per kind, judging the answer, the timer, and feeding `RehearsalController.startQuestion`/`finishQuestion`. `_answerInput` is derived from the current view rather than kept as its own flag, and is what tells `presenter_keys.dart` that the keyboard belongs to the answer field. Three rules are enforced here: a round that is not `answerable` gets no countdown and never blocks; an open-text question keeps its `expectedAnswer` out of the `QuestionView` until the reveal, because that object is the render state and whatever is in it gets painted (not a confidentiality measure — `buildBeamerMarkdown` sends the whole deck, `correct` flags included); and an image pair draws one correct and one wrong answer per round rather than taking the first two, so a pool longer than the editor's two slots stays presentable.
- `presenter_table.dart` — Live table editing during presentation.

### `lib/widgets/reader/` — the in-app documentation reader

- `document_reader_screen.dart` — `DocumentReaderScreen`: a full-screen, accessible reader for one bundled Markdown document. Content fills the height and the full available window width — tables, code blocks and Mermaid diagrams span the whole measure, prose stays at a readable line length (`_proseMaxWidth`) — and a text-size control in the app bar is remembered separately from the app-wide interface scale. A find-in-page bar searches within the open document: it counts the matching blocks, steps through them with wrap-around, and scrolls the active hit into view (`_activeMatchKey`). Pushed on the root navigator, so it sits above dialogs and can be opened from the consent gate and the settings screen alike. Taps on links are routed through `resolveDocLink` (`doc_link.dart`): an internal document link pushes a new reader on top (bundled) or opens the repository version in the browser (repo-only, checked against `DocumentationService.bundledDocAssets`); a `#anchor` scrolls to that heading via a single moving key (`_anchorKey`/`_anchorBlockIndex`, the same trick as the find-in-page scroll); an external link opens in the browser. A link that carries an anchor into a freshly opened document (`initialAnchor`) scrolls there once the document has rendered.
- `document_markdown_view.dart` — `DocumentMarkdownView`: renders a whole Markdown document as widgets (headings, paragraphs, bullet and numbered lists, GFM task lists, block quotes, fenced code, ```mermaid diagrams, rules, pipe tables and marked document timelines), reusing the slide renderer's `InlineMarkdownText` for inline formatting. A pragmatic reader, not a CommonMark engine: it covers what the bundled documentation uses, and anything it does not recognise falls back to a paragraph so unknown syntax shows as readable text rather than disappearing. Prose is bounded to `maxTextWidth` for a readable line length while tables, code and diagrams take the full width they are given. Parsing is a pure step shared by rendering and by `blockTexts` (the searchable text of each block in render order), so the reader's find-in-page can locate the block holding a term and — via `activeMatchBlockIndex`/`activeMatchKey` — tint the matching blocks and scroll the active one into view, without the match indices and the rendered blocks ever drifting apart. The same block list backs anchor navigation: `headingBlockIndex(markdown, slug)` maps a `#anchor` to the block of the first heading whose `headingSlug` matches, and `anchorBlockIndex`/`anchorKey` carry a single moving key on that block so the reader can scroll to a section. Block rendering itself sits in `parts/document_markdown_blocks.dart`; the table block (width decision, styled table, cell) is in `parts/document_markdown_table.dart`; and the marked-table card projection is in `parts/document_markdown_timeline.dart` — all `part`s, split off at the 1000-line ceiling. A pipe table is measured before it is drawn: the columns get their natural widths when they fit, and are otherwise scaled down proportionally so the table stays inside the available measure instead of overflowing it — the same table that the HTML and LaTeX exports lay out, and it is bounded by the theme's table house style (`ThemeProfile.tableBorderStyle` and friends). A `<!-- toc -->` marker on its own line renders as a live contents list generated from the document's own headings, so the author sees what the export will produce rather than an HTML comment. The optional `blockWrapper(index, block)` hook wraps every block in render order; `PagedDocumentView` uses it to measure each block's real height, which is what lets the page view break between blocks without a second render path (`null` leaves the tree exactly as a plain reader builds it).
- `table_edit_controller.dart` — `TableEditController`: the edit state of one table that is filled in *inside the rendered table itself*, cell by cell, the way a spreadsheet works. It owns the per-cell `TextEditingController`s and `FocusNode`s, the column alignments, and the structural operations (insert/remove/move a row or column, set a column's alignment); drawing stays with `DocumentMarkdownView`, so the editable table has by construction the same column widths, borders, zebra striping and house style as the one you read. Key handling makes a cell behave like a spreadsheet cell: Tab walks the cells and grows a row at the end, Enter drops a row (Shift+Enter is a line break within the cell), Cmd/Ctrl+C copies the whole grid as TSV unless text is selected inside the cell, and Cmd/Ctrl+V pastes a whole grid from a spreadsheet, CSV or Markdown table (`table_clipboard.dart`), growing the grid to fit. Every content change calls `onChanged` with the full grid once — a fingerprint suppresses the no-op notifications a `TextEditingController` also fires for caret and selection moves, which would otherwise write the table back with the previous content.
- `table_editable_cell.dart` — `TableEditableCell`: one editable cell inside the rendered table. Deliberately chrome-free (no frame, no fill, no margin of its own): it uses exactly the text style and cell padding of the read-only table, so typing shows what you get, including the column width the table picks while you type. The inactive cell shows the rendered reading of its Markdown over an invisible but stable text field; the active cell shows the Markdown being edited. Tab/Enter/paste are intercepted before the `TextField`, focus changes report the active cell, and `EmbeddedFieldActions` keeps Quill from performing the nested field's text editing.
- `table_edit_scaffold.dart` — `TableEditScaffold`: the wrapper around an in-place editable table. It rebuilds the table on every change (column widths follow cell content, so a table that does not repaint would keep the old measures while you type), restores the focus for a cell that only came into existence during this build (the row Tab or Enter just added), and shows a compact toolbar as soon as the caret sits in a cell — row above/below, typed sort intent, column left/right, remove, and column alignment, all acting on the cell you are in. The toolbar is inside an `ExcludeFocus` so clicking it does not pull the caret out of the cell, and it disappears when the table loses focus, leaving the table to read as a plain table.
- `paged_document_view.dart` — `PagedDocumentView`: the document on real sheets — the chosen ISO-216 page size, the margins, the header/footer band (`DocumentChromeBand`) and the page number, with the printer's bleed drawn as a rim around the trim line. It renders the document once off-screen through the *same* `DocumentMarkdownView` as the other two editor views (no second render path, DOCUMENT_MODE.md §2.1), collects the height of every block via the `blockWrapper` hook, and hands those to `documentPageOffsets`; each sheet then shows its own window on that one continuous render. A sheet that starts at a later event in a document timeline shows the localised `Tijdlijn · vervolg` continuation label and that event's first-column marker in its top margin while the card restarts the rail. The sheet is `AppTheme.paper`, so it dims along in dark mode instead of glaring.
- `writing_page_breaks.dart` — `WritingPageBreakOverlay` plus de drie pure functies eronder: waar de pagina's breken terwijl je *schrijft*. De schrijfstand blijft één doorlopende Quill-editor (in vellen knippen zou typen, cursor en selectie kapotmaken), dus de einden worden erover getekend als streepjeslijn met het nummer van de pagina die erachter begint. De hoogtes komen uit de echte render van de editor via `editorKey.currentState?.renderEditor` — `writingBlockHeights` leidt ze af uit de *posities* van de blokken, niet uit `size.height`, want anders valt de tussenruimte weg en zakt elke volgende lijn verder van zijn grens; `writingContentTop` verrekent de binnenmarge van de editor, anders staat elke lijn precies die marge te hoog. `writingPageBreaks` laat het breken zelf over aan `documentPageOffsets`, dezelfde regels als `PagedDocumentView`. Het paginanummer reist met de lijn mee (`(y, page)`), omdat op een vel dat hoger is dan het venster nooit meer dan één lijn in beeld staat en tellen vanaf het zichtbare dus overal "2" gaf. Rollen meet niets opnieuw uit: de gemeten einden blijven staan en worden per schuifstap alleen omgerekend naar posities in het omhulsel.
- `doc_mermaid_view.dart` — `DocMermaidView`: draws a ```mermaid fence in the reader as a diagram instead of leaving its source as monospace text (which is what the reader did before — the diagrams shipped but were never rendered). Rendering runs on the shared `MermaidRenderService` (the same hidden-WebView engine the slides use), so a diagram looks identical in the reader and on a slide; it is sized from the SVG `viewBox` and scrolls horizontally when wider than the column, its height flowing into the page's own vertical scroll rather than nesting a second vertical scroller. When rendering is impossible (most notably under `flutter test`, where there is no WebView) or fails, it shows the `fallback` code block, so the source is never lost.
- `documentation_search_tab.dart` — `DocEntry`, `DocSection` and the search across the bundled documents for Settings → Documentation: an empty query shows the full list grouped by audience, typing filters to the documents whose title or body contains every typed word (with a highlighted excerpt), and a footer card (`repositoryDocsUrl`) links out to the repository for the documentation that is deliberately not bundled — shown even when a search matches nothing. The `assetBase` string literals deliberately live where the entries are built (`settings_dialog_docs.dart`) so `docs_registration_test` keeps finding them.

## `testbed/` — local integration test infrastructure

- `docker-jitsi-meet/` — a local Prosody XMPP server (plain `prosody/prosody`, not the full Jitsi stack) for `test/xmpp/xmpp_docker_jitsi_integration_test.dart`. `docker-compose.yml` exposes the WebSocket endpoint on `127.0.0.1:5280`; `prosody.cfg.lua` configures anonymous auth, MUC with `muc_room_locking = false` (so a second client can join a room the first just created), and `cross_domain_websocket = { "*" }` (so a non-browser client can connect). The test is gated behind `OCIDECK_XMPP_INTEGRATION=1` and run via `make test-xmpp-integration`; it is not part of `make check`. See `testbed/docker-jitsi-meet/README.md` for why a plain Prosody replaced the full `docker-jitsi-meet` stack (Jitsi's custom Prosody modules — `muc_domain_mapper`, `muc_resource_validate`, hardcoded `restrict_room_creation` — interfere with non-Jitsi clients).
